# PC_PORT divergences from the GBA build

> **About this file (2026-10-31, main `39bbd7cb`).** This is the port's working log of every difference, published as it is. The status lines and dates inside it are history. Pointers such as `pending/...`, `port-plan/...`, `DECISIONS.md`, `HANDOFF.md`, `THANK-YOU.md`, `wf-fe8\...` and `~/...` name the developers' private notes, scripts and work folders; they are not published. The code lines and commit IDs can be checked in this repository. ACCURACY.md summarises the differences a player can see.

This file lists every place where the PC build compiles or runs the shared game code differently from the GBA build. DECISIONS.md (frame-4672 entry) requires it.

## 1. Changes to shared source files (guarded)

| ID | File and lines | What the GBA does | What the PC does | Why |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| D-1 | `src/bmsave-lib.c:251-256`, `WriteSaveBlockInfo` (commit `35ea0410`, decision D7). Guard: `#if BUGFIX \|\| defined(PC_PORT)` | Stores `(uintptr_t)GetSaveWriteAddr(index)` in the u16 `offset` field. The cast truncates `0x0E00xxxx` to `xxxx`. | Stores `SramAddrToOffset(GetSaveWriteAddr(index))`, which is `addr - gSram`, so also `xxxx`. This is the decomp's own `BUGFIX` path, with its typo fixed (`chuck` to `chunk`). | On a 64-bit host the cast would truncate a host pointer. Under D1-a (32-bit, SRAM at `0x0E000000`) both paths give the same value, so save files stay byte-compatible. Not visible to the player. |
| D-2 | `src/difficultymenu.c:59-68`, `SaveMenuInitSaveSlotData` (the decomp marks it `// BUG?`). Guard: `#ifdef PC_PORT` | Calls `IsGameNotFirstChapter((struct PlaySt *)slot)`, passing the slot number (0-2) as a pointer. `IsGameNotFirstChapter` (GBA `0x080A52BC`) reads the byte at `0x14+slot` (bit 5 = complete) and the signed byte at `0x0E+slot` (chapter index). Both addresses are in the BIOS area, so they return open-bus bytes. Two open-bus values can occur after boot: `0xE3A02004` after a SWI and `0xE55EC002` after an IRQ. With either one the function returns true for all three slots. So the GBA sets bit 0 of `unk_3a[slot]` for every valid save. | Calls `IsGameNotFirstChapter(&playSt)`. `playSt` is the `PlaySt` read from that slot a few lines earlier, with its chapter index restored at line 54. That gives the same result as `IsGameSaveNotFirstChapter(slot)`. Bit 0 is set only when the save is past the Prologue or the game is complete. | The host has no page at address 0, so the GBA call faulted at frame 4,672 (reading `0x00000014`). DECISIONS.md chose guarded fixes over BIOS open-bus emulation, because emulation needs the user's BIOS file, which we cannot ship. The argument is the one the code plainly intended: the function's signature, the `PlaySt` already in hand, and the matching calls in `sio_menu.c:68` and `sio_term.c:100`. |
| D-3 (was P-1) | `src/face.c:421-431`, `EndFace` (GBA `0x08005738`). Guard: `#ifdef PC_PORT`. Made exact 2026-10-19 (commit `bdf2162f`, merged in `ee874c9f`). | `ResetFaces` (`face.c:290`) calls `EndFaceById(i)`, so `EndFace(NULL)` runs for each empty slot as the Prologue's first world map opens (`SetupGraphicSystemsForWorldMap`, `worldmap_main.c:320`). `ldrb r1, [r1]` at `0x08005740` reads `0x40`, open-bus lane 0: `0x04` after a SWI, or `0x02` after an IRQ. The game then stores 0 (`str` at `0x08005748`) to `gFaces[4]`, which is past the array end and is `gpBgVerticalScrollSt` (`0x03004990`), or to `gFaces[2]`, which the same loop clears anyway. It then calls `Proc_End(NULL)` (`0x08002D6C`), which tests for NULL and returns. | When `proc == NULL`: stores NULL to `gpBgVerticalScrollSt`, then returns. This is the GBA's store for the post-SWI latch. On the PC, `gFaces` (`0x03004980`) and `gpBgVerticalScrollSt` (`0x03004990`) are absolute symbols at their GBA addresses (`nm build/pc-full/fe8pc`, from `gba_mem.ld`), and the host code is `movl $0x0, 0x3004990`. `Proc_End(NULL)` is skipped, because it does nothing on either build. | The host has no page at address 0, so the read faulted at frame 8,412 (address `0x00000040`, host EIP `0x1007C7C0`). Exact for the post-SWI latch: the same store and no other effect. A probe run (not committed) showed the four `EndFace(NULL)` calls of that `ResetFaces`. The first one found `gpBgVerticalScrollSt = 0x02000000`, set by the save menu (`savedraw.c:367`, `StartBgVerticalScroll(EWRAM_ENTRY)`). The old guard (return at once, no store) left that value on the PC; now the PC clears it as the GBA does. RAM traces no longer need to mask it after `ResetFaces`. The IRQ latch (`0x02`) would store to `gFaces[2]` instead, which the loop clears anyway; it applies only if an IRQ lands between the last SWI and the read. **Round 10 (D-3's amendment, was working ID D-R10-FACES-4; `port-r10-faces` `15b0cb58`; `src/face.c:421-438` on `port-integrate11`):** when `proc == NULL` the PC stores NULL to `gFaces[PORT_NULL_READ(proc, faceSlot)]`, the rule's lane: slot 4 (`gpBgVerticalScrollSt`) post-SWI, slot 2 (`gFaces[2]`) post-IRQ, then returns. Measured over 35,240 `EndFace(NULL)` calls (mGBA, the 39 route files and `w_i10`; EndFaceById, EndFacePtr): slot 4 at 34,985 (no HBlank IRQ), slot 2 at 208 with the HBlank IRQ on, as the rule gives, and slot 2 at 47 without it (a VBlank IRQ in a lag frame after the last SWI, which the rule does not model: these get slot 4; `route_i13` 34,067 has 4 of them, the faces review). Exact at 35,193; `gFaces[2]` and `gpBgVerticalScrollSt` were NULL at every slot-2 call, so no route changes. So the store goes to `gFaces[2]`, not `gpBgVerticalScrollSt`, at every `EndFace(NULL)` made while the HBlank IRQ is on, from any caller (`EndFacePtr`, all 30 `EndFaceById` sites); "which the same loop clears anyway" (above) holds for `ResetFaces` only. |
| D-4 (was P-2) | `src/face.c:1277-1283`, `SetFaceBlinkControl` (GBA `0x08006438`). Guard: `#ifdef PC_PORT` | A world-map portrait has `pBlinkProc == NULL` (`EventC4_WmShowPortrait`, `eventscr_gmap.c:1189`, calls `WmDrawFace`, `worldmap_face.c:356`, which calls `SetFaceBlinkControlById`). The GBA stores `blinkControl` to `0x30` (ignored), then calls `FaceBlinkProc_GenBlinkInterval(NULL)`. That function calls `AdvanceGetLCGRNValue()` once (`bl` at `0x08006474`) before it reads `0x30` (`ldrh` at `0x0800647C`, open bus), and the result is stored to `0x38` (ignored). Net effect: the LCG advances once, nothing else changes. | When `blinkProc == NULL`, calls `AdvanceGetLCGRNValue()` once and returns. | The read of `0x30` faulted at frame 8,677. Exact: the one RNG advance is kept, so the LCG sequence matches the GBA, and every other GBA effect on this path is a write the GBA ignores. No RAM difference. **Line references (round 10):** D-70 and D-71 add lines above it; on `port-integrate11` D-4's guard is at about `src/face.c:1350` (find it by its comment, "see DIVERGENCES.md D-4"), right after D-71's block (`1326-1343`). |
| D-5 (was P-3) | `src/worldmap_rm.c:803-815`, `IsGmapRmBorder1ActiveAtIndex` (GBA `0x080C2BC4`). Guard: `#ifdef PC_PORT ... #else <retail line> #endif`. Made exact 2026-10-19 (commit `bdf2162f`, merged in `ee874c9f`); latch by the rule since 2026-10-20 (`port-integrate2` `8953fb21`). | For `index >= 0`, the loop reads `proc->index` before it checks `proc != NULL`. When no border proc matches, `Proc_FindNext` returns NULL, and `ldrb r0, [r0]` at `0x080C2BF2` reads `0x2A`, open-bus lane 2: `0xA0` after a SWI, or `0x5E` after an IRQ, zero-extended. `cmp r0, r4` / `beq` at `0x080C2BF4` returns 1 when that value equals `index`. Otherwise `cmp r1, #0` / `bne` ends the loop, and the function returns 0. No store and no RNG call on this path. **Measured 2026-10-20** (group G analysis, mGBA, scripted keys to tick 20,000): these reads come with the world map's HBlank IRQ on (`WorldMapHblank`; IE `0x2003`, DISPSTAT `0x0018`/`0x0019`, the last IRQ about 311 instructions before the read, the last SWI 43,197 instructions before it), and all 8 NULL reads (ticks 9,762-13,777) load `0x5E`, the post-IRQ byte. | `PORT_NULL_READ(proc, index)`: the retail compare with a real proc; with `proc == NULL` the open-bus byte by the latch rule (below), so `0x5E` with the world map's HBlank IRQ on and `0xA0` with it off. A scratch PC build that logged the NULL path (not committed) saw the GBA's 8 ticks and indices (9,762 idx 7, 10,122 idx 0, 10,482 idx 2, 10,842 idx 5, 11,202 idx 1, 11,781 idx 3, 13,323 idx 1, 13,777 idx 7), each with IME 1, IE `0x2003`, DISPSTAT `0x0019`, latch `0xE55EC002`, byte `0x5E`. | The read faulted at frame 8,850 (address `0x0000002A`, host EIP `0x100EE747`), called from `EventBA_WmRemoveHighlightNationPart2` (`eventscr_gmap.c:1008`) on the Prologue world map. **Corrected 2026-10-20:** the guard of 2026-10-19 compared with the post-SWI `0xA0` (`cmp $0xa0, %ebx`), and the latch rule listed D-5 as post-SWI; the GBA reads `0x5E` there. Nation indices are 0-7 (`WM_NATION_MAX` is 8) and come from the event script, so the function returns 0 with either latch and the retail scripts never reach the difference: the scripted 60,000-tick Gate 0 stays `LAG-ONLY` with 0 not lag-only and 0 replay-only (`CHECK_LONG` on `port-integrate2`). The first guard (2026-10-19, before "made exact") left the loop and returned 0 for every index, which matched the GBA for every index except the latch byte. |
| D-7 (was P-6) | `src/banim-ekrmain.c:528-532`, `GetAnimPosition` (GBA `0x0805A16C`). Guard: `#ifdef PC_PORT`. Added 2026-10-19 (commit `dc8b78e4`). | As the first battle animation starts, `ekrBaStart_InitBattleScreen` (`banim-ekrbattlestarting.c:198`) calls `NewEkrUnitKakudai` (`banim-ekrbattleintro.c:466`), which calls `GetBanimDragonStatusType` (`banim-ekrdragonstatus.c:79`) while `gEkrDragonStatusLeft.anim` is still NULL. `GetEkrDragonStatus` (line 33) then calls `GetAnimPosition(NULL)`. `ldrh r1, [r0, #12]` at `0x0805A16E` reads open-bus lanes 0-1: `0x2004` after a SWI, or `0xC002` after an IRQ. Bit 9 (`ANIM_BIT2_POS_RIGHT`, mask `0x200`) is clear in both, so it returns `EKR_POS_L` and the caller reads `gEkrDragonStatusLeft.type`. The second call, with `gEkrDragonStatusRight.anim` (line 83), gives the same answer. No RNG call and no store on this path. | Returns `EKR_POS_L` at once when `anim == NULL`. | The read faulted at frame 14,139 of the scripted run (address `0x0000000D`, host EIP `0x1002DDCE`: GCC tests bit 9 as bit 1 of the byte at `0x0D`). Exact: the same return value for both open-bus values, and no state changes. The guard sits in `GetAnimPosition` itself, so any other caller that passes NULL also gets the GBA answer. Result: the scripted run (`FE8PC_AUTOPRESS=0x001,90,4300`) passes frame 14,139 and stops one frame later at 14,140 (P-7), the same in two runs. Attract mode (60,000 frames, no input) is unchanged: DISPCNT `0x3F00`, 61,742 non-zero VRAM bytes (FNV-1a `0x50C426A8`), 264 palette entries. GBA check: `fireemblem8.gba: OK`. |
| D-9 (was P-8) | `src/player_interface.c:1124-1130` (`1165-1171` since D-10 added lines above it), `TerrainDisplay_Loop_OnSideChange` (GBA `0x0808CB5C`). Guard: `#ifdef PC_PORT`, nested inside the `#else` of the decomp's `#if BUGFIX`, so the GBA build still compiles the retail line `if (ui1Proc != NULL)`. Added 2026-10-19 (commit `1282a179`). | When the minimug box (`UI1`) is up and no goal window is (`piProc == NULL`), retail tests `ui1Proc` (`cmp r4, #0` at `0x0808CBB0`), then reads `piProc->windowQuadrant`: `ldrsb r0, [r1, r0]` at `0x0808CBBA` with r1 = `0x57`. That is open-bus lane 3: `0xE3` after a SWI, `0xE5` after an IRQ. Every BIOS latch value is an ARM opcode with condition AL, so this byte is `0xE0`-`0xEF`, which is negative. `blt` skips the early return. With no host definition, `gen_host_link.py` binds `AnimScr_EkrPopup` (used by `DrawBattlePopup`) to `0x0878D518`, and `ROM_DATA_NAMED` binds `AnimSprite_EkrPopup` to `0x0878D500` by name although only the ROM script word points to it, so the PC binary keeps both GBA names (`0878d500 A`, `0878d518 A`) for debuggers. A name in `ROM_DATA_NAMED` that is not ROM data in the GBA ELF is a `gen_host_link` warning and is not bound. The ROM word is no longer relocated: `fixptr` has 12,319 entries, was 12,320 (`port/tools/fixptr_unresolved.txt` regenerated in `f3a9f5be`). | **A port pointer problem; no retail behaviour involved.** `ANIMSCR_FORCE_SPRITE` keeps only address bits 2-27 of the sprite (bits 0-1 and 28-30 hold the duration, `anime.h:162-189`). The host-built sprite sat at `0x10254C48`, so the script word `0x10254C49` decoded to sprite `0x00254C48` (unmapped: the probe's 6 sites, about 86 junk OAM entries a frame, renderer tripwires T1 and T8) and timer 5. It was the only relocation entry that carried flag bits into a pointer word (`{0x0878D518, 0x0878D501, AnimSprite_EkrPopup, 1}`). Exact: the same bytes and decode as the GBA. With the fix `gOam` equals the GBA's on all 206 popup ticks (before: different on all 206), and the popup slot of `sAnimPool` equals the GBA's byte for byte. Rule for later: host-built data must not pack a host pointer into a word whose top bits are flags; the review found no other C data of this kind (asm `FORCE_SPRITE`/`PTRINS` words with top-nibble flags were not audited; `fixptr` never relocates them). The round-2 report's "script word plus NULL `pSpriteDataPool`" (the FRAME path, `animedrv.c:289-290`) was wrong: it is the FORCESPRITE path. |
| D-27 (was NULL-probe group F, N-6 to N-21) | `src/bmmap.c:327-392`, `DisplayBmTile` (GBA `0x08019B18`): the whole function under `#ifdef PC_PORT`, the retail function unchanged in `#else` (`379-391`). Helper `port/hw/gbabus.c` (`PortGbaLoad32/16/8`, lines 73-93) and `port/include/port_gbabus.h`, included by `port.h`; `PortRomGbaWord` (`port/hw/romreloc.c:60`). Added 2026-10-20 (`port-nullfix` `4f8fd1f3`, `ecd68766`). Design and the rejected options (an exact memory model with mirror views; a fog-only helper): `pending/merged/groupF-design.md`. | **Retail behaviour.** Chapter `0x38` (Castle Frelia) has a 2x2 map, the only map smaller than the 15x10 window (all 79 maps checked). At the Chapter 1 → 2 change (route ticks 20,271-20,272, a 12-VBlank lag tick), `RenderBmMap` (from `Event30_ENUN`, `Event25_ChangeMap`, `InitMoreBMapGraphics`) draws rows -8..9 and columns -13..14, and `RenderBmMapColumn` (u16 arithmetic) rows 65528..65538 and column 65523, with no bounds check. `gBmMapBaseTiles[y]` / `gBmMapFog[y]` read the words next to the row tables (terrain-lookup bytes, tile ids, fog bytes `0x01`, zeros); the loads through them hit the BIOS area (post-SWI `0xE3A02004` at all 111 BIOS reads: IE `0x2001`, no HBlank IRQ), unused space (open bus = the load's prefetched THUMB opcode: `0x4912` for the tile-id `ldrh` at `0x08019B32`, `0x021B` for the fog `ldrb` at `0x08019B44`), EWRAM mirrors (row slots at `0x0206F44C-0x02072F94`, tile config at `0x0204xxxx`), and ROM past the 16 MiB image (`0x0B2BFFE6-0x0B2C0002`). Only `gBG3TilemapBuffer` changes (junk tiles, palette 6 off-RAM since every latch lane and `0x021B021B` are nonzero); the screen is black (BLDY 16) and BG3 is redrawn at 20,278. No RNG, no game state. | The 8 loads that can leave the map (row pointer, tile id, fog row, fog byte, four tileset halfwords) go through `PortGbaLoad32/16/8` with the GBA's 32-bit address arithmetic, that load's open-bus word (`PORT_THUMB_OPEN_BUS(opcode at load + 4)`) and `PortOpenBusLatch()`; the two global-pointer loads (`gBmMapBaseTiles`, `gBmMapFog`) stay plain C, since they are always valid. On the map they read the same EWRAM as the retail lines. ROM past the image: the undriven-bus halfword address, zero-extended (hardware `LDRH`). | Every playthrough faulted at tick 20,271 (`0x0101010F`, the fog byte, then the tile reads). Exact for the BIOS latch (mGBA latched post-SWI at all 111 reads), the per-load open bus, the mirrors and the fog test (latch-independent). BG3 against the GBA: 1,024 of 1,024 cells equal at 20,270-20,271 and 20,278-20,279; 1,018 of 1,024 at 20,272-20,277, where the other 6 are OL-5 (a scratch build copying mGBA's sign extension matched every cell on every tick). **Open user choice:** what a retail cartridge drives past the 16 MiB image: the undriven bus (chosen; GBATEK's no-cartridge value and mGBA's default) or mirrored ROM data (if the mask ROM ignores the 25th address line), which the review rates at least as likely. Either way the GBA zero-extends, and it changes only these 6 junk BG3 cells on a black screen. Not exact, not reached: I/O below `0x04000400` returns the host's register image (a write-only register reads back its value; `0x04000800` is a real register on the GBA); open bus right after a sound-FIFO DMA. Lag tick 20,272's `RenderBmMapColumn` call runs after an IRQ (mGBA post-IRQ, the rule post-SWI) but reads no BIOS address. The guard accepts any wild pointer here for the whole game, so a later PC-only corruption of these pointers would draw junk instead of faulting (BG3 is in the Gate 0 trace). **Second gap hit (round 9, `pending/folded/r9-ch8exp.md`):** Ephraim's Chapter 8 ending (a scratch key file), 16 BG3 cells on ticks 86,716-86,721 in every build (GBA `0x8000`/`0x8222`, PC `0x6000`): the map change's second `RenderBmMap` reads the BIOS area after a lag VBlank that came after the last SWI on that branch, so the GBA reads the post-IRQ latch where the PC gives the post-SWI one; invisible (black screen, `frame15` equal). |

A script word stored past `scripts[9]` is stored as the script's GBA address when a ROM data word points at it (`PortRomGbaAddressOf`), else as its host address; on route_br8 the 11th script's was found (`flags[0] = F0DC`, the GBA's). | Before, GCC kept `idx` in a register: 11, 12, 13, writing `flags[2..5]` with halves of host addresses and the 13th flag into `gUdefCnt` (`0x03001CC0`). No route reaches either. |

### Open-bus latch rule (2026-10-19; one shared copy since 2026-10-20)

A guard that has to reproduce an open-bus value picks the latch per site, from the interrupt setup the GBA has at that read. It is not a global setting.

- **Post-SWI `0xE3A02004`** when only VBlank (and Game Pak) interrupts are on and no IRQ can land between the last BIOS call and the read (no HBlank handler, `IE` without HBlank, not a lag frame). The last BIOS exit before the read is then a SWI (`VBlankIntrWait`, `CpuSet` and so on). A fixed value in D-3 and D-10. Measured (mGBA) at every route hit of D-21, D-22, D-24, D-25, D-27, D-37 and D-38, and in round 9 at D-48 (Ch2, Ch8, the TAS run, P-21's suspend), D-49 (all 32 back-outs), D-50 (Chapter 7's ending) and D-51 (1) and (2).
- **Post-IRQ `0xE55EC002`** when the HBlank interrupt is on and an IRQ is sure to land between the last SWI and the read (for example, more than 1,232 cycles of code with no SWI in between, since an HBlank IRQ comes every 1,232 cycles). A fixed value in D-14 (save menu and bonus claim set an HBlank handler). Measured (mGBA) at every hit of D-5 and D-35 (the world map's `WorldMapHblank`).
- **Bytes by lane:** a byte or halfword load takes the bytes of the latch word at `address & 3`. For example `0x04` (lane 0) gives `0x04` post-SWI and `0x02` post-IRQ; `0x2A` (lane 2) gives `0xA0` and `0x5E`; `0x57` (lane 3) gives `0xE3` and `0xE5`.
- Prefer a guard that is exact for every latch when the GBA drops or masks the value: D-4, D-7, D-9, D-13, D-23, D-36, D-38 and D-51 (1) and (2) (no latch read on the PC), and D-22, D-25, D-35, D-37, D-48, D-49 (they read the latch but reach the same result with any word). When the value matters and the setup is not certain (lag frames, debug-menu callers), log the site as an open latch choice: D-10, D-24 and D-51 (3).
- How to find the setup: `IE`, `IME`, `DISPSTAT` and the HBlank handler pointers (`sHBlankHandler1`, `sHBlankHandler2`) in an mGBA run at that tick, and the `vblanks` count of the Gate 0 trace (a lag tick can have an IRQ inside `OnMain`). The NULL probe (`FE8PC_NULLPROBE=1`, section 3, "Round 3 integration") records the rule's choice for every BIOS-area read of a PC run.
- **Shared helper (2026-10-20, `port-nullfix` `b6a504d5`; one copy of the rule since `port-integrate2` `d7c7a22d`).** `port/include/port.h:87-134` (force-included in every shared file on the host, never in the GBA build): `PortOpenBusLatch()` (post-IRQ while IME bit 0, IE bit 1 and DISPSTAT bit 4 are all set, read at the load; else post-SWI), `PortOpenBusRead(addr, size)` (the lanes at `addr & 3`) and `PORT_NULL_READ(p, member)` (`p->member`, or the GBA's load of that member through NULL: its offset, width and sign, from the D-18 layouts). The latch words and the post-IRQ test exist once, in `port/include/port_gbabus.h:45-48` (`PORT_OPEN_BUS_SWI`, `PORT_OPEN_BUS_IRQ`, `PORT_OPEN_BUS_HBLANK_IRQ_ON`); `PortOpenBusLatch` and the NULL probe's `LatchRule` (`port/hw/nullprobe.c:162`) both use them, so they cannot drift apart. `PORT_NULL_READ` fails to compile unless the member is a 1-, 2- or 4-byte field, naturally aligned, at an offset below `0x4000` (beyond that a NULL load gets the prefetch open bus: use `PortGbaLoad*`). Users: D-5, D-21, D-22, D-24 (`PortOpenBusRead`), D-25, D-27 (`PortOpenBusLatch`), D-35, D-37, D-39 and D-50 (`PortOpenBusLatch` with `PortGbaLoad*`), D-48, D-49, D-51 (`PORT_NULL_READ`); in test builds only, TB-3 to TB-6 (`PortOpenBusLatch`, since `port-integrate4` `88e20b3a`, which removed `port-konami`'s own copy of the rule). D-10 and D-14 keep their fixed values (D-14's is what the helper would give at both retail callers; moving D-10 would change its documented choice). New guards that reproduce an open-bus value use the helper, so every site makes the same choice. **`PORT_HOST_ADDRESS(p)`** (`port/include/port.h:157-168`, round 9's fix passes, `647fad0f`): whether `p` lies in the host image (0x10000000-0x1FFFFFFF: Linux `-Ttext-segment`, MinGW `--image-base`, `main_pc.c`'s convention). For guards whose pointer is a host table on every retail path but can be a GBA address taken from GBA memory (D-55, D-57); outside the image they load through `PortGbaLoad*`. Limit: a wild GBA address that happened to lie in 0x10000000-0x1FFFFFFF would be read as host memory (the GBA has open bus there); none is reached. **Round 10 and integration 11 (`port/include/port.h`, `port_gbabus.h`, `port/hw/gbabus.c`, `port/hw/romreloc.c`):** `PORT_NULL_READ_VIA(p, link, member, openBus)` (`p->link->member` with `p` or `p->link` NULL: the latch word by the rule, then `PORT_GBA_READ` at it; D-60, D-73; it evaluates `p` and `p->link` more than once); `PortGbaRead(addr, size, openBus)` and `PORT_GBA_READ(p, member, openBus)` (the one sized GBA load, the latch by the rule inside; D-74, D-80; integration 11 merged traps' and paths' identical `PortGbaLoadSized` into it; the signed cast is LDRSH/LDRSB only at an aligned address; a word in `0x04xxxxxx` reads the host's register image); `PORT_PROC_PARENT_READ32` (a proc's parent field when the parent is a tree root index; D-66); `PORT_UNIT_CATTRIBUTES(unit)` (`PORT_NULL_READ` of both `attributes` words, ORed: D-67, D-78, and D-37's `CUW_CATTRIBUTES`); `PORT_UNIT_FACTION(unit)` (`UNIT_FACTION` through a NULL unit: lane 3 sign-extended, `& 0xC0`, `0xC0` for every latch: D-79 and D-22); `PortRomTableLoad8` and `PortRomGbaAddressOf` (a host-built ROM byte table read past its end at the GBA's address; D-68); `PortBmMapWrite` and `PortGbaStore8` (the store side of `PortBmMapRead` and `PortGbaLoad8`: a byte store as the GBA bus takes it, EWRAM and IWRAM on their mirrors, palette and BG VRAM in both halves of the halfword, OBJ VRAM, OAM, the BIOS area, unused space and the cartridge windows ignoring it; I/O not modelled; self-test 28 of 28 against mGBA's `GBAStore8`; D-69); `PortGbaRamAddress` / `PORT_GBA_RAM` (a GBA pointer on an EWRAM or IWRAM mirror folded onto the mapped copy; D-81; the same masks also live in `gbabus.c`, a shared definition is on the round 11 list). H-10's flood store (`port/asm/arm_pc.c`, built without `port.h`) could use `PortGbaStore8` later.
- **What the rule and the helper do not decide.** (1) A lag VBlank IRQ after the last SWI: the PC does not model lag (D-24 is the case that matters so far). Measured in round 9: the Prologue's heal read (D-48, 5,219: `0x02` where the rule gives `0x04`, masked), the Ephraim debug jumps' cursor (D-51 (3), I21 3,235 and I12 3,001: post-IRQ, a case that matters, P-28), E11 16,624 (post-IRQ, but no BIOS-area read there) and Chapter 7's ending, 41,543 (a lag tick whose reads are post-SWI: a SWI came after the lag VBlanks). A lag-aware latch prototype exists (`~/gate0-scratch/r9-maprange/proto-lag-latch.patch`; a decision). (2) Other IRQ sources: with a VCount IRQ on (the phase intro, `SetInterrupt_LCDVCountMatch` in `phasechangefx.c`), or a timer, DMA, serial or keypad IRQ, the rule does not decide the latch; the helper gives post-SWI there, and the probe marks the case `other_irq` (undecided). No guarded read runs during the phase intro so far; a site that does needs its own analysis. (3) Reads inside an IRQ handler, where hardware gives `0xE25EF004` (the probe tracks `gPortIrqDepth`; the helper ignores it). (4) Misaligned loads (not modelled by `PortOpenBusRead`). None of these is flagged at run time; each user's entry says why it does not apply there. **Round 10:** (1) also the Link Arena's unit List (D-68, P-40): a measured lag case that matters, logged as an open latch choice (integration 11: option A, keep the rule); and D-66's window (3-4 lines from the last SWI to the load) and D-3's 47 lag-frame `EndFace(NULL)` calls, where a VBlank IRQ after the last SWI would give post-IRQ (no measured hit of D-66 was; D-3's targets were NULL). Modelling "a VBlank IRQ after the last SWI" in `PortOpenBusLatch` (the lag-aware prototype) stays a pending decision.
- **GBA bus helper (2026-10-20, `port-nullfix` `4f8fd1f3`).** `port/include/port_gbabus.h` (included by `port.h`) and `port/hw/gbabus.c`: `PortGbaLoad32/16/8(addr, openBus, biosLatch)` give the GBA's value for a load at any 32-bit GBA address without touching an unmapped host page: EWRAM, IWRAM, palette, VRAM and OAM at their mirrors; the BIOS latch below `0x4000`; `openBus` in unused space (`0x4000-0x01FFFFFF`, `0x10000000` up, I/O above `0x04000400`); ROM in the three wait-state windows with the GBA's word where `romreloc.c` rewrote a pointer (`PortRomGbaWord`, a binary search of the relocation table); the undriven-bus halfword address past the image (zero-extended); SRAM's byte on every lane; misaligned `LDR`/`LDRH` rotate as on the ARM7TDMI. A guard whose code loads through a wild pointer that can leave the BIOS area uses it, with `PORT_THUMB_OPEN_BUS(opcode at load + 4)` for the open bus and `PortOpenBusLatch()` for the latch. Used by D-27, D-39, D-55, D-56, D-57, H-10 (`FloodLoad8`, which writes the latch rule from these shared words because `arm_pc.c` is built without `port.h`) and, through `PortBmMapRead` (`port/include/port.h:136-155`), D-50. Not exact, not reached: I/O below `0x04000400` (the host's register image); open bus right after a sound-FIFO DMA (cycle timing; met since round 9's fix passes, OL-7). Self-tests (scratch, not committed): 45 of 45 checks by the author and 13,996,892 checks, 0 failed, by the review, on Linux and on Windows. Every game object and the two helper objects depend on `port_gbabus.h` in `port/Makefile:141-146` (`087ef561`).

### Host-ABI values: the rule and the round-4 sweep (2026-10-20)

A fidelity class of its own (DECISIONS.md, 2026-10-20 overnight). When retail C reads an uninitialised local, or uses the result of a function that falls off its end (or of a void function called as one that returns a value), the GBA gets whatever agbcc's registers or stack slot hold there, and the host gets whatever GCC's hold. The PC must give the GBA's value. Found by the round-2 integration trace (route ch0: tick 1,591 was D-29, tick 1,642 was D-28). Folded from `pending/hostabi.md` and `uninit-{ai,save,event,menus,map,battle-fx}.md`, now in `pending/merged/`, and the reviews in `wf-fe8/round4_results.json`.

- **Rule** (DECISIONS.md, "Host-ABI rule refined (round 4 sweep)"). A site gets a `PC_PORT` fix when its uninitialised value or fall-off return can reach observable state on a retail path. The fix gives the GBA's value as worked out from the agbcc code (the registers at that point, not a guess) and is checked in mGBA where a route or a forced path reaches it. A site that is provably unreachable in retail, or whose value is overwritten or unused on every path, gets no code change; its GBA value and the proof are recorded here (the list below). The first wording ("each site gets a `PC_PORT` fix") left no room for that; the save group's review asked for the "no change" stance to be accepted explicitly, and the refined rule does. On those unreached paths the PC gives whatever GCC makes of them, which equals the GBA's value only by chance (SAVE-4; MENU-8 before D-43).
- **Shared premise.** D-28, D-36 and D-43 rest on `r8` to `r10` being 0 at `AgbMain` entry. Static: no code from crt0 (and `IrqMain`) down to those functions writes them. Measured in mGBA, which starts the cartridge without a BIOS boot. GBATEK's `SoftReset` clears `r0`-`r12`. Not measured after a cold boot on hardware (no BIOS dump).
- **The sweep** (`round4_results.json`, `sweep`; a scratch worktree at `9da96127`). All 349 FULL game objects compiled with `HOST_CFLAGS` without `-w`, plus `-Wmaybe-uninitialized -Wuninitialized -Wreturn-type`: **135 unique warnings** (91 `-Wreturn-type`, 42 `-Wmaybe-uninitialized`, 2 `-Wuninitialized`). 64 `-Wreturn-type` sites were dropped as unused at C level: every caller discards the result (proc-script `PROC_CALL` targets, menu and target-selection callback slots whose result the menu code drops, plain statements), or nothing calls the function. 6 were handled before: EV-H1 (`TalkAdvanceDeamon_Loop`, which every host build already returns right), EV-H2 to EV-H4 (`Event2F_MoveUnit`, D-28), MAP-H1 (`GmMuPrim_UpdateStepSounds`) and FX-H1 (`ply_xwave`). 65 went to six groups (`port-uninit-<group>`), each analysed from the agbcc code by one agent and checked by a second that tried to refute it; all six were approved. Fixed: 11 sites (D-30 to D-34, D-40 to D-45), plus the two `Event2F_MoveUnit` warnings fixed as D-28, so 13 of the 135. D-29 gives no warning (void targets); the trace found it.

- **The 64 dropped sites** are listed with their C-level reason only (`round4_results.json`, `sweep.list.dropped`); round 4 worked out no GBA value for them. The reviews' coverage notes (`SetTalkChoiceResult`, `GetBgYOffset`, the world-map `Start*` functions, `RemoveTrap`, `AddLightRune`) are all on that list. Round 6's `port-uninit-gaps` works out each one's GBA `r0` and readers (`pending/uninit-gaps.md`, not folded here).
- **What the sweep cannot see (open).**
  1. **Uninitialised reads through memory.** GCC warns about a local only while it follows it in registers. It does not see a local passed by address and read by a callee, a struct filled field by field and then copied whole, padding, a stack buffer that is partly written, a read past the end of a stack array, or stale bytes in a reused proc slot. Known cases: AI-X1, `AiAttemptBallistaCombat`'s `tmpResult.itemSlot` (P-23); the stack bytes retail writes into saves (P-18); D-36's `trainees[3..6]` (found by the NULL probe, not by a warning); `Event27_MapChange`'s pointer (P-16); and stale proc-slot bytes, which stay retail (`TerrainDisplay_Loop_OnSideChange` reads `ui1Proc->windowQuadrant` before `MMB_Init` sets it: section 5, "Knock-on"). No tool covers this class yet; each case so far came from a GBA-side probe, a review or a Gate 0 difference. **Round 9** (`pending/folded/r9-ch8exp.md` section 7) swept the struct locals (225 in `src/`): `AutolevelSecondaryLord`'s is D-58; `UnitAutolevelRealistic`'s was checked, no change (its unset `canCounter` is never read: curHP is 0 there); `ArenaGenerateOpponentUnit`'s `UnitDefinition` is D-54; the 9 `spline.c` structs are not fully checked; the rest are clean or P-18. For a local whose callee chain is short, a GBA probe on every load from the local (fp9's `fp9_ch8exp_probe.lua`) or a poison test settles which unset fields are read. Partly written non-struct stack buffers and stale proc-slot bytes are still unswept.
  2. **Only the 349 FULL objects.** Of the 10 files `port/Makefile` leaves out (`FULL_EXCLUDE`), `ramfunc.c` and `agb_sram.c` compile clean; the other eight do not compile on the host, and their partial output showed `bmguide.c:1020` (`-Wreturn-type`) and `sio_multiboot.c:366` (`-Wmaybe-uninitialized`), not triaged. `bmguide.c` is also P-20. **Since `port-integrate5` (2026-10-20)** `bmguide.c` is built (B-18), so 9 files are left out; its `-Wreturn-type` site (`GetGuideAction`, now line 1043), the only warning the sweep had seen in it, is D-47. The other seven compile failures are unchanged; `uiarena.c` among them is P-24 (the map menu's Suspend), and its host-ABI values are not reviewed yet. **Since `port-integrate9` (2026-10-21)** `uiarena.c` is built too (B-19; its host-ABI review is in the row), so 8 files are left out.
  3. **GCC's view depends on its flags.** The warnings come from `-O1` with the port's flags. A site that GCC folds to a constant gives the GBA's value only by chance (SAVE-4; MENU-8 before D-43), and another compiler or flag can change that.
  4. **No standing gate.** check-all's warnings gate covers the port code only (the game objects are compiled with `-w`, B-16), so a new site in game code is not caught; the sweep was one run, at `9da96127`.
  5. **Unreached fixes.** Most fixed paths run on no route: D-31, D-33, D-34 and D-41 to D-45 rest on the agbcc code and forced or poked mGBA runs, D-30 on a synthetic mGBA run. D-32 was checked against the GBA on all 36 route calls on the GBA side and on 13 of them on the PC. No PC run has gone through a village visit, a promotion, the Support screen, a closed-eye talk portrait or the minimap of chapter `0x18`.
- Noted in the sweep, not a divergence: `gProcScr_PhaseIntroUnk` (`src/phasechangefx.c:37-40`) has no `PROC_END`. If its loop reached `Proc_Break` it would run on into the next array in ROM (`gProcScr_PhaseIntroText`), where the PC would follow its own data layout. On the routes the parent proc always ends it first (62 loop calls per phase change).

- **Declaration types (round 10, C10-6; D-64, D-65, B-22).** A function declared with a wider return type than its definition (an `int` declaration of an `s8` function) reads the GBA's clean value on the GBA (agbcc extends in the callee) and the other bytes of EAX on the host (GCC's callee sets only AL or AX): C10-6's Demon King summoned into the wall. `port/tools/decl_sweep.py` lists every declaration whose type differs from its definition (the FULL build's DWARF, cross-checked by `-flto`); 30 sites, 14 fixed under `PC_PORT` (D-64, D-65: three differed, the rest equal only by GCC's register choice), 16 benign, all in `decl_accept.txt`, and check-all's `declsweep` row keeps it so: the first standing gate of this section's kinds. **Not covered (deferred to round 11, integration 11's choice):** function-pointer slots whose type differs from the function stored in them (proc `PROC_WHILE`/`PROC_REPEAT` routines, menu and selection callbacks: a narrow-return function called through an `int`-returning slot is the same hazard; D-29 covers only `PROC_CALL_ARG`'s void targets), with the paths group's find for it: `AttackBallistaCommandUsability` (`src/bmmenu.c:2297`) calls `IsBallista` through an `s32 (*)(struct Trap*)` cast (the decomp's own "TODO: FIXME: UB"); the host's `IsBallista` returns with `sete %al` over the trap pointer, so a non-NULL non-ballista trap would read as non-zero; not reachable today (the function runs only for a unit in a ballista, whose square holds that ballista: 1 on both).

### Argument evaluation order: the rule and the round-9 sweep (2026-10-21; D-52)

C leaves the order in which a call's arguments are evaluated unspecified, and the two compilers pick opposite orders (`pending/folded/r9-argorder.md`, from both compilers' source and output, checked in the game's binaries and by its review):

- **agbcc** (GCC 2.9, Thumb): call arguments left to right (`PUSH_ARGS_REVERSED` is never defined in `calls.c`; a plain memory read is copied into a register in its turn, `SMALL_REGISTER_CLASSES`). With stack arguments (more than 4 words; `ACCUMULATE_OUTGOING_ARGS`), `precompute_arguments` first evaluates every argument that contains a call, left to right, then the rest. Binary operators, comparisons, condition jumps and assignments whose left side is not a plain variable run their calls first (`preexpand_calls`), then the plain reads and the left side's address. A function pointer is loaded after the arguments. `old_agbcc` (`m4a.c`) the same.
- **Host GCC 15** (`HOST_CFLAGS`, i386 `-O1`; the SDL, Gate 0 and MinGW builds alike): every argument right to left (`gimplify_call_expr`), memory reads included; a plain non-addressable local is read at the call itself, after the other arguments (the review: `f2(x++, x)` gives `(x, x+1)` on both compilers, so the tool can over-report such a call, never miss one; there is none in `src/` or `port/`). Operators left to right, except that `fold` puts a plain variable last in a commutative operator or a comparison; `lhs = call()` takes the left-hand address first, any other right side and `op=` take the right side first.
- **No compiler flag fixes it for C** (`-maccumulate-outgoing-args` and `-mno-push-args` were tried; `-fstrong-eval-order` is C++ only), so there is no build row (section 2).
- **Rule:** two arguments i < j are taken in opposite orders unless the call has stack arguments, j contains a call and i does not. A site whose two orders can give different results gets a `PC_PORT` block that sequences it through locals in agbcc's order, the retail call in `#else` (D-52).
- **The sweep** (`port/tools/argorder_sweep.py`, Python standard library only). It parses all 358 game C files (not `msg_data.c`) as the GBA build preprocesses them (`--view gba`) or as the PC builds do (`--view pc`), summarises each function's reads and writes through its calls (struct fields by type; a call through a function pointer it cannot follow is "unknown"), and flags every call with two or more arguments that make calls or have effects (class A), every call where one such argument meets a memory read (class B), and every operator, assignment and function-pointer call whose two orders differ (class C), when one part writes what the other reads or writes (conflict) or makes a call the tool cannot follow (unknown). `--selftest` checks the model against 34 cases compiled with both compilers (`port/tools/argorder_cases.c`). `--accept port/tools/argorder_accept.txt --check` fails on a flagged site not judged there (key: file, function and a hash of the expression), on a stale entry (GBA view) and on a `fixed` site still flagged in the PC view ("FIX MISSING"). check-all's `argorder` row (default set, `--needs gba`) runs the self-test and both views, about 12 s.
- **Counts** (`port-integrate8`): 14,535 calls with two or more arguments, 398 class A or B candidates, 194 class C sites; flagged: 25 in the GBA view (19 conflict, 6 unknown; 11 fixed, 14 benign), 14 in the PC view (all benign). The review's own per-statement call-order diff of agbcc against PC objects (6,573 functions) found 59 reordered statements after the fix: the accepted ones and pairs of pure getters.
- **The 14 benign sites**, left retail, each with its reason in `argorder_accept.txt`: `DoUsePutTrap` (that selection decodes no text), `RepairSelectOnSelect`, `GenericSelection_BackToUM`, `ClearText`, `InitBattleForecastLabels`, `PrepUnit_DrawLeftUnitName`, `DrawPrepScreenItemUseStatValues`, `sub_8099328`, `PrepUnit_DrawUnitItems`, `SaveDraw_Init`, `Talk_OnIdle`, `TalkLoadFace`, `StartTalkFace`, `SioPostBattle_Loop_Main`.
- **What it cannot see (open).** Calls through function pointers (proc scripts, menu and selection callbacks; judged by hand, and a changed callback is not re-checked unless the site's expression changes); approximate aliasing (false conflicts rather than missed ones, except a write through a pointer local of another type); initializer lists and statement expressions inside stack-argument calls (none in retail code; untested on agbcc); the orders are `-O2`'s (and `-O1`'s for `agb_sram.c`).
- **Rename hazard (review).** The accept keys hash the function name and the expression text, so a bulk rename breaks the row: a simulation of `port-names10`'s `apply_names.py` renames of `sub_8029D6C` and `gUnknown_08A280A8` gave 2 open sites and 2 stale entries. Re-run the sweep and re-key `argorder_accept.txt` in the same commit as such a rename, or take the identifiers out of the hash.

- **An unsequenced side effect inside one expression (round 10, D-72, B-25).** `*p++ = *(p + 1)`: class C of `argorder_sweep.py` does not model an operand that both reads and increments one variable. GCC's `-Wsequence-point` (PC view, all game files) found 2 such sites, 1 that differs (`RemoveTrap`, fixed as D-72) and 1 benign (`Title_IDLE`); check-all's `seqpoint` row (B-25, integration 11) keeps the list judged.

### What D-2 changes for the player

- **In play: nothing.** Bit 0 of `unk_3a[slot]` is read only at `savemenu.c:1190`, under `EXTRA_MENU_OPTION_6` (`CallExtraMap`). The Extras menu setup in `savedraw.c` (`InitSaveMenuChoice`, lines 1043-1108; the Extras entries from about line 1068, PB-1's block at 1074-1089 on `port-integrate11`) never adds option 6, so the retail game cannot reach that check.
- **In RAM traces: one byte.** For a valid save still on the Prologue, `SaveMenuProc.unk_3a[slot]` has bit 0 set on the GBA and clear on the PC. Mask this byte when Gate 0 traces are compared.
- **Hardware-exact alternative.** If a trace-exact build is ever wanted, a constant `1` reproduces both open-bus values. It is not used, because the decision was to pass the intended argument.

### Test builds only (`FE8PC_TESTBUILD=1`): TB-1 to TB-6

A test build compiles extra code in shared files, all of it under `#if defined(PC_PORT) && defined(FE8PC_TESTBUILD)` (or `#ifdef FE8PC_TESTBUILD` in `port/`), with the retail line in `#else` where one is replaced. Release builds compile none of it: their loaded code and data equal those of a tree without it (the four Linux binaries and the Windows exe, compared section by section; only the ELF build-id and the debug info differ), and they hold no test-build symbol or string (`port-konami` and its review; on `port-integrate4` the four release binaries' loaded sections are the same before and after `88e20b3a`, apart from the build-id, again with no test-build symbol or string). The GBA build is unchanged. Folded from `pending/konami.md` (now `pending/merged/`); the user approved the shortcut as a test tool (HANDOFF, next step 4).

Added 2026-10-20 (`port-konami` `34f85534`, `6ae397d9`, `02c29851`, merged on `port-integrate4` in `8e199573`; the build directories and the command-line rule in `88e20b3a`). | Retail has no way into the game's leftover debug menu. `StartDebugMenu` (`bmdebug.c`) is unused; it has the signature of `MenuDef.onRPress`, where retail's map menu has `MenuAutoHelpBoxSelect`, so the debug build's way in was probably A on the map (the map menu), then R. FE6 has the same unused function (`func_fe6_0801A674`). | On the map, in the player phase with the cursor free (`PlayerPhase_MainIdle`), Up Up Down Down Left Right Left Right B A (X and Z on the keyboard; in the window either Shift is also Select). The final A belongs to the shortcut, so the menu opens whatever is under the cursor; the debug build would have needed A on an empty or turn-ended tile and a later R (`uimenu.c:516` ignores `onRPress`'s result). The proof is "the PC equals the GBA for these calls", not "equals the debug build". Checks (`make -f port/test/konami.mk konami`): release build LAG-ONLY on 0-2,167 (575 lag-only, 0 not), the keys being ordinary keys; test build KONAMI CHECK PASS: the menu opens once (tick 2,076), and against a GBA run whose oracle script makes the same two calls (`port/test/oracle/konami_capture.lua`) every traced field is equal on every tick 0-2,396, with `frame15` different on 11 ticks, only in the Weather row (TB-5). Its key file counts `OnMain` calls, not Gate 0 ticks: a battle animation's own main loop does not advance it, so a Gate 0 key file replays the same way only up to the first battle animation. |

The other caller, `StartupDebugMenu_ChapterSelectEffect` (`bmdebug.c:676`), cannot run: no ROM word points to it and no code calls it. **Finding:** with the world map skipped, a story chapter whose world-map node is not open yet loads as a skirmish (`GetBattleMapKind`, `worldmap_path.c:1920`), on the GBA too. From the Prologue, Chapter 2's title shows (tick 2,312), then `EventScr_SkirmishCommonBeginning` pushes 22 values on the event slot queue and pops 23 (tick 2,397, `gEventSlots[0xD]` 0): the GBA's `SlotQueuePop` shifts about 256 KiB and the GBA makes no further tick; the PC faults in the same call (at `0x03008000`, where the GBA wraps into an IWRAM mirror). So the jump is useful only for chapters that `GetBattleMapKind` treats as story without a world-map node (Prologue, 1, 5x, 12, 21, 21x, Castle Frelia, 3B/3C, 3F, 0x40-0x47); chapter saves (section 3, "Round 3 integration") are the tool for chapter tests. **Open user choices** (`konami.md`): keep the jump as the debug code reads (recommended), and keep or drop this stand-in (without it the Map item does what retail does: back to the title). |

A decode that ran past `0x03008000` would fault on the PC where the GBA wraps into a mirror; it did not in the checked run (review). |

Merged on `port-rfo-int5` (`port-rfo-hpbars` `b6f8e009`); the convention on `port-rfo-int6`. | No bars or marks on the map. | **Bars** (1 or 3): every drawn unit (any faction) with `0 < curHP < GetUnitMaxHp` (the effective maximum) shows a 13x4 bar at the bottom of its square with 11 fill steps, `11 - floor(11 * (max - HP) / max)` pixels, coloured by the fill: 9-11 green, 6-8 yellow, 4-5 orange, 1-3 red, the empty part navy (the game's `gPal_MiscUiGraphics` colours 11, 1, 4, 12 and 13, read from the ROM data; `port-rfo-polish`, HPB-1; `BAR_*_INDEX`, `BarColour`, `reforged_hpbars.c:100-104`, `:472` on `8c690589`); the 1-pixel frame is our own colour (`COL_FRAME` `RGB5(2, 2, 3)`, `:90`); none at full HP. **Warnings** (2 or 3): in the unit movement screen (`BM_FLAG_1`, a blue `gActiveUnit`, `PlayerPhase_RangeDisplayIdle`), each drawn unit not allied with the moving one gets at most one "!": white when its class is a slayer of the unit's (`IsUnitEffectiveAgainst`) or a weapon it can equip (`CanUnitUseWeapon`) is effective against it (`IsItemEffectiveAgainst`, Delphi Shield honoured), orange when such a weapon's own crit (`GetItemCrit`) is above 24 (retail: the Killer weapons and the Shamshir); the class first, then the slots in order, the first that qualifies decides. Worked out once per selection (new unit, chapter, turn or phase) for every unit, not per frame. The test for "a unit is selected" also searches the procs only once per selection and then reads the found proc's fields (`port-rfo-int6` 3aa68f9d, 3f353e80). Suspend `.sav` padding bytes 0x9B and 0xAB (P-18) can differ even between two Off runs of one binary, so the save proofs mask or repeat them (`pending/rfo-int6.md`). The pictures are drawn by the software renderer as extra OBJ pixels over that unit's own sprite pixels and pixels no sprite drew, never over another sprite's, at the sprite's priority (windows over the map hide them as they hide the units; a list more than 2 frame steps old, e.g. in battle animations, is not drawn). No OAM entry, OBJ tile, palette entry or GBA memory is used or written; the ballista's weapon and the arena are not looked at. **OFF:** the collect returns at the option test and the list stays empty: the pictures equal the base build's. | **Display only, the user's choice** (task brief 2026-10-29; the convention chosen by the lead 2026-10-30; idea and rules: the FE8 community's map HP bars and warnings, THANK-YOU rows 31 and 35, CREDITS-draft section 3 rows 11 and 15; no code used). Checked at int5: ON == OFF on every frame's RAM, palette+VRAM+OAM, sound and I/O and the `.sav` (window build, ch2 and ch5), OFF == the base build's pictures (`pending/rfo-hpbars.md`, `pending/rfo-int5.md`). Checked at int6 (`pending/rfo-int6.md`): Bars, Warnings and Both each == Off on every frame (window build, ch2 and ch5 routes), the `.sav` equal, 0 "GBA MEMORY CHANGED" in every check-mode run. The `.sav` is sensitive to P-18's host stack junk (SRAM `0xAB`, the suspend ALT block info's `_pad_07`): an earlier int6 commit, whose Warnings ran a proc search every frame, differed from Off there by one byte; the same byte differs between two binaries' Off runs. Not run: a real keyboard, a Windows run, the battle forecast or side windows over a bar (same priority rule, not pictured). |

Chapter 20's turn-1 enemy-phase event (`src/events/ch20a-eventinfo.h:4`, `EventScr_089F8C60`, `CAMERA2_CAHR(CHARACTER_RIEV)`, then `CUMO_CHAR`) freezes when Riev is dead. Reproduced on mGBA and pc-gate0 from `b20_start.sav` with Riev's suspend slot set to pid 0 (what a kill leaves; a save edit, not a played kill): both stop at tick 903, `Event26_CameraControl` called 86,723 times on the GBA. **Pierce:** C07's Pierce branch (`src/banim-main.c:209-218`, layer 0) clears every `state3` bit but `ANIM_BIT3_BLOCKING` of `gAnims[2]`/`[3]` and unfreezes them; Sure Shot's (`:191-197`) and Great Shield's branches do not. With battle animations on, a Pierce strike that does not kill followed by another strike: the next strike starts while the first HP drain runs, and after a long drain the battle never resumes (the "Pierce freeze" of the US and European versions). Pierce's branch acts as Sure Shot's: BEGIN keeps `gAnims[2]`/`[3]`'s `state3` and `state`, END puts them back (the same as not running the four statements); the frozen battle ends (HP 120 -> 51, back on the map). **This also changes the pace of every Pierce strike with battle animations on, not only those that would hang:** in a low-damage Pierce that does not hang in retail (`pierlow.sav`) the battle runs 80 frames longer (`OnMain` 2,107 vs 2,187), frames differ on 1,533 of 1,600, and the `.sav` differs in 3 bytes (the suspend's `time_saved` at offset 212 and its block checksum at 160, 162); who wins, the damage and the RNG are the same. **Off:** the original statements run (the event logs the first hang place once; Pierce's BEGIN only reads): both hang as on the GBA (Riev: 0 of 38,400 pixels differ at the freeze; Pierce: the same hang, `OnMain` 984 against the GBA's 982, 340 pixels of the name plate's gradient differ, the lag-only kind). On == Off (frames and `.sav`): no Pierce, Pierce on the killing second strike, Sure Shot, Riev alive. Never marks a save (`sDisplayOnly`). Not tested: an enemy Wyvern Knight's Pierce (attacker on the left: the same code then keeps the defender's anims, as Sure Shot's branch does for enemy Snipers); a played freeze inside the class caps (on paper a capped Wyvern Knight, `maxPow` 25 at `src/data_classes.c:2005`, does 31 with a Javelin); a played Riev kill. | **Not retail behaviour: the user's U10** (DECISIONS.md, 2026-10-30: fixes for game-stopping retail bugs may be on by default, each listed in the patch notes; the second exception to "Off = the GBA game", next to fast-forward, PB-5), each fix only after the bug reproduces on the GBA and the PC (`pending/retailfix.md`, Step 1). Off = the GBA game. Test builds byte-identical: pc-gate0's 456 objects and `fe8pc` equal `aff1f2d4`'s at `38328689` and `7b08e3aa`, GBA OK, check-all 23/23 (`pending/retailfix.md`, `retailfix-refute.md`). Ideas credited, no code taken: the FEU "Pierce Fix" thread, Serenes Forest, the Fire Emblem Wiki (THANK-YOU row 36, CREDITS-draft section 3 row 16). **The Pierce pacing is decided: (a), keep it as built** (the user, 2026-10-30, after the `port-rfo-int8` run; DECISIONS.md, "Decisions of 2026-10-30"): the fix stays on by default and every Pierce hit with animations on is paced as Sure Shot's; results and RNG the same, the save the same apart from the play clock. Not taken: (b) a row of its own, off by default; (c) leaving the Pierce fix out (`pending/retailfix.md`, "Decision still open"). Merged in `port-rfo-int8` `1a411bc0` (`pending/rfo-int8.md`). Not run: CHECK_LONG (running on `1a411bc0` since 2026-10-30), an SDL window run, a Windows hand test. |

## 2. Build-level choices (`port/Makefile`; GBA sources unchanged)

| ID | Where | What the GBA does | What the PC does | Why |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| B-1 | `port/Makefile`: `GENERATED_SRCS`, and `WITH_GENERATED`, now on by default for `FULL=1` (fix (a) of the frame-4672 decision) | Compiles `src/chapterdata.c`. `GetROMChapterStruct(ch)` returns `gChapterDataTable + ch` in ROM (GBA `0x08034618`). | Before: the file was not compiled, because its header is generated, so `GetROMChapterStruct` was a generated stub that returned NULL. `GetChapterTitleExtra` (`chapter_title.c:115`) then read `0x0000000E` at frame 4,672. Now the same source compiles unchanged with `-iquote .`, using `src/data/chapter_settings.h`, which the GBA `make` generates with jsonproc. It returns the host copy of `gChapterDataTable`, built from the same JSON. `fixptr` points ROM words that refer to the table at the host copy (7,279 host-built data entries in the table, up from 6,901). | This is the decomp's own build path, not a source change. `fontgrp.c` and `fontgrp-data.c` need generated headers too and are now built the same way. That removes the `SetLang`/`ResetText` stubs. 345 shared files are linked. `WITH_GENERATED=0` gives the older 342-file build in `build/pc-full-nogen`. |
| B-2 | `port/Makefile`: `HOST_CPPFLAGS`, `HOST_CFLAGS` | agbcc with the GBA flags. Fakematch blocks use `register asm()` or inline asm. | `-DPC_PORT=1 -DNONMATCHING=1`: `NONMATCHING` selects the decomp's equivalent C for fakematches. These blocks are meant to behave the same, so a mismatch found there is a decomp bug. Also `-funsigned-char` (plain `char` is unsigned on ARM), `-fwrapv`, `-fno-strict-aliasing` and `-malign-data=abi` (agbcc-like layout). | These let the shared C compile on x86 with the GBA's semantics. |
| B-3 | `port/include/port.h`, force-included | `DmaSet` writes the DMA registers, and the hardware runs the transfer. | `DmaSet` goes to `PortDmaSet`. Immediate transfers run at once. Timed ones (VBlank, HBlank, sound FIFO) are only logged. | A write to the I/O image starts nothing on the host. Without this, `main.c:27`'s IWRAM clear would do nothing. |
| B-4 | `port/Makefile`: `-Dasm(x)=((void)0)` for `src/hardware.o` only | `EnterSleepMode` runs `swi 3` (Stop). | The `swi 3` is removed. | ARM inline asm does not build on x86. It is only reachable from the debug menu. |
| B-5 (was P-4, **fixed**) | `port/Makefile`: `-fno-delete-null-pointer-checks` in `HOST_CFLAGS`, the flags for the shared game files. `PORT_CFLAGS` and every GBA build flag are unchanged. Added 2026-10-19. | agbcc keeps a NULL test even when it comes after a store through the same pointer. In `SetWmPlaceDotFlagForIndex` (`src/worldmap_rm.c:1208-1229`, GBA `0x080C30E0`), called with index < 0 from `EventBD_` (`eventscr_gmap.c:1065`) on the Prologue world map, the loop sets `flag` on every place-dot proc. Then it writes `1` to `0x29` (`strb` at `0x080C3114`, ignored), tests `proc` (`cmp r1, #0` at `0x080C3116`), and ends. | GCC now keeps the same test (`test eax / je` right after the store, checked in the rebuilt `worldmap_rm.o`). H-1 drops the write once, and the loop ends as on the GBA. | Without the flag, GCC at `-O1` assumed `proc` could not be NULL after the store and removed the loop's `while (proc != NULL)` test. The host code jumped straight back to `Proc_FindNext`, which kept returning NULL, so the run **hung** at frame 11,864 (H-1 dropped the write to `0x00000029` over and over). One flag covers every loop of this shape (the five listed in section 4), not just this one. Result: the scripted run (`FE8PC_AUTOPRESS=0x001,90,4300`) passes frame 11,864 and reaches 12,000, with the write dropped once (host EIP `0x100EEE0E`). Attract mode (60,000 frames, no input) is unchanged: DISPCNT `0x3F00`, 61,742 non-zero VRAM bytes, 264 palette entries. GBA check: `fireemblem8.gba: OK`. |
| B-6 (fixes the `eventcall.c` stubs) | `port/Makefile`: `src/eventcall.c` taken out of `FULL_EXCLUDE`, and the `LVCAST_SRCS` rule. Added 2026-10-19. | agbcc accepts a cast used as an lvalue. Six lines in the earthquake procs (`eventcall.c:258, 282, 342, 349, 374, 399`), such as `(u16)gBmSt.camera.x &= ~2;`, store the low 16 bits of the result back into the field. | The host compiles `build/pc-full/lvcast/eventcall.c`, a copy that `sed` makes at build time, in which each `(u16)gBmSt.camera.x` or `.y` reads `*(u16 *)&gBmSt.camera.x` or `.y`. The same bits are stored: `camera` is a `struct Vec2` (two `short`s), so the `u16` view is the field itself. The copy has the same lines, and `#line 1 "src/eventcall.c"` keeps the file name and line numbers in messages and debug info. A new cast of this kind elsewhere in the file still fails the build. | GCC rejects the cast, so the whole file was left out and its functions were generated stubs. Two were hit in the scripted run at frame 12,210 and changed state silently: `SetEventId_0x84` did not set event flag 0x84, and `ResetAllPlayerUnitState` did not reset the player units. Check, with a scratch build (not committed) that wraps the functions with `-Wl,--wrap` and runs the scripted input: at frame 12,210 flag 0x84 goes from 0 to 1. `ResetAllPlayerUnitState` finds no valid player unit at that point of this run, so it changes no unit there. A forced call at frame 13,000 on the 5 deployed player units, with the first two set first to HP 1 and 2, a status, torch 4, barrier 2, extra state bits (`0x00410052`) and rescue 7: all 5 end as the source says (HP at max, status 0/0, torch and barrier 0, state cut to the kept bits plus `US_HIDDEN`, so `0x00410052` becomes `0x00410001`, x = -1, rescue 0). |
| B-7 (fixes the `bmdifficulty.c` stub) | `port/Makefile`: `src/bmdifficulty.c` taken out of `FULL_EXCLUDE` and built with `-DPORT_CONST_DATA_DEFAULT_SECTION`; `port/include/port.h`. Added 2026-10-19. | `CONST_DATA` (`prelude.h`) puts const objects (`obj_859E79C`) and non-const ones (`sProcScr_DisplayDungeonRecord_FromMenu`) in the one section `.data`. agbcc allows it. | For this file only, `port.h` defines `CONST_DATA` as empty, so GCC puts const objects in `.rodata` and the rest in `.data`. It also defines `PRELUDE_H`, which `prelude.h` tests but never defines, so a later include cannot restore the macro. Only the host's own copies move. The object contents are the same, and the EWRAM overlay (`gUnknown_0200310C` and the rest, 0xA9C bytes, the same size as in the GBA map) sits at its GBA addresses. | GCC refused the file (section type conflict), so `GetCurrentPromotedLevelBonus` was a stub that returned 0, not 9 (19 on hard). `UnitAutolevel` (`bmunit.c:825`) then skipped the promoted-class autolevel and its RNG draws for promoted units. Check (same scratch build): at frame 12,916 it is called twice and returns 9 both times (normal mode), as the source says. With B-6 and B-7, 347 shared files are linked and the generated function stubs fall from 163 to 110. |
| B-8 (renderer build) | `port/Makefile`: `PORT_SRCS` adds `port/render/*.c` without `port/render/test_*.c`; the compile rule adds `-Iport/render`; a new `render-tests` target. Added 2026-10-19 (commit `a843f823` on `port-render`, merged in `22c2b06a`). | The PPU draws the picture. There is no renderer code to build. | The PC links the software renderer (D-15). `make -f port/Makefile render-tests` builds and runs the BG suite (`test_bg`) and the OBJ suite (`test_obj`) with `-Wall -Werror`. The suites use no game code and no ROM. | The frame step needs a picture. The GBA build compiles nothing in `port/`, so the ROM is unchanged. |
| B-9 (layout checks for D-18) | `port/tools/layout_probe.py` (new) and a `layout-check` target in `port/Makefile`; `port/save/layout_asserts.c` (new, built through the `port/save/*.c` wildcard); `port/test/`: `GATE0_SRAM` in `pc/gate0_hook.c` and `oracle/gate0_capture.lua`, and a "SRAM" paragraph in `README.md`. Added 2026-10-19 (commit `32ec6f81`). | One compiler, nothing to compare. | `make -f port/Makefile FULL=1 layout-check` compares every struct and union with agbcc's layout and fails on any difference (method in the D-18 details). `layout_asserts.c` makes no code. Its static asserts fail the PC build if a save struct (size, alignment 4, every member offset; `SuspendSaveBlock` 0x1F78, `GameSaveBlock` 0xDC8) or an `AGBCC_STRUCT` struct in `include/` (size, alignment) stops matching agbcc's numbers. `GATE0_SRAM=PATH` writes the 64 KiB at `0x0E000000` after the last tick, on both sides. | Locks D-18. 93 of the asserts fail on the old layout (checked with a copy of `include/` in which `AGBCC_STRUCT` is empty). Bitfield positions and the struct types local to `src/` files are checked only by `layout-check`. The GBA build compiles nothing in `port/`. |
| B-10 (SDL3 window build) | `port/Makefile.sdl` (new). It includes `port/Makefile` with `BUILD=build/pc-sdl`, `FULL=1` and `PORT_SRCS` set by `override` (read back from `make -p`), swaps `port/hw/platform_headless.c` for `port/sdl3/*.c`, and links `build/pc-sdl/fe8pc-sdl` against a 32-bit SDL3 (Ubuntu `libsdl3-dev:i386`, 3.4.2). The SDL link uses a copy of `autostubs.c` without the `SDL_*` stubs, so a missing SDL name fails the link. `port/Makefile` is not edited. Added 2026-10-19 (commits `cd6b369f`, `575774a7` on `port-sdl`, merged in `61905c3d`). | No window: the GBA has a screen. | A window build of the same game objects. The headless builds (`port/Makefile`, the Gate 0 build) keep `platform_headless.c` and behave as before. The platform layer itself is H-4 and H-5. | The player needs a window and live keys (PLAN 3.7, phases 2 and 4). The GBA build compiles nothing in `port/`. |
| B-11 (native Windows build) | `port/Makefile.mingw` and `port/win32/` (new files only): `pe_gba.py` (COFF tools for `fixptr.py`/`gen_host_link.py`, and the PE strip and layout check), `posix_win32.c` with `compat/` headers (`mmap` on `VirtualAlloc` at fixed bases, `sigaction`/`ucontext_t` on a vectored exception handler), `abi_check.py`, `vars.mk`. MinGW i686 cross-build in WSL, `-mno-ms-bitfields -mno-align-double`, image base 0x10000000 without ASLR or relocations. Added 2026-10-19 (commit `d8b68b03` on `port-win`, merged in `31b6db11`). At the merge, with `port-sdl` in, `platform_headless.c` joined the Windows build: MinGW has `clock_gettime`/`clock_nanosleep` only in libwinpthread (not linked) and no `fsync`, so `gen_host_link.py` stubbed all three silently. Fixed in `port/win32` only: `compat/time.h`, `unistd.h` and `stdio.h` map `clock_gettime`, `clock_nanosleep`, `fsync` and `rename` to `posix_win32.c` (`QueryPerformanceCounter`, a waitable timer, `_commit`, and `MoveFileExA` so a save replaces the old file as POSIX `rename` does). | n/a | A 32-bit headless `fe8pc.exe` with D1-a unchanged: GBA memory at GBA addresses, code at 0x10000000. The BIOS-area write drop (H-1) runs through the vectored handler. | A Windows player build. `abi_check.py` compares every struct and union layout of the Windows build with the Linux build (DWARF). The 60,000-frame attract run matches Linux exactly (merge check in "Integration of `port-gate0`, `port-sdl` and `port-win`" below). **The Windows window build (2026-10-21, `port-win9` `eb542d4d`, on main; `pending/folded/win9.md`):** `port/Makefile.mingw SDL=1` builds `build/win32-sdl/fe8pc-sdl.exe` (a GUI-subsystem exe, no console) with the official `SDL3.dll` 3.4.2 and `LICENSE-SDL3.txt` beside it. `SDL3.dll` is **delay-loaded**: loaded normally, its DLLs put Windows' own mappings exactly where the GBA map must go, and no start-up could reserve it; the build now fails unless the exe imports only `KERNEL32.dll` and `msvcrt.dll`. The vectored handler takes first-chance faults only for EIP in the exe, below 0x10000, in 0x02000000-0x0E00FFFF or on an execute fault; faults in a DLL go to its own SEH first. A log file and message boxes replace the console (`port/win32/gui/`); the save rules are H-9's, with `PortWin32SameFile` for "the same file". check-all rows `win-sdl`, `abi-sdl` (5,146 layouts, 0 differ) and `win-sdl-60000`. Linux and Windows window builds draw and sound the same, frame for frame and byte for byte (60,000-frame attract and scripted runs, a Chapter 2 save, Direct3D 11 at scale 3). No game-behaviour divergence: the Windows pieces are host-only. The review's notes (the handler's EIP window also covers host memory in the gaps of the GBA map; `stderr` buffered after `freopen`, so the box can miss an error; the ROM check reads the header only; an empty `%APPDATA%\fe8pc` is made on every run; the Windows rows read real gamepads; the release exe ships DWARF and symbols) are `port-winpolish`'s, not on `port-integrate9`. Its one picture difference, 2 frames of 26,000 on Linux against Windows from `bb.c`'s `bldyLut[32]`, is gone with D-53 (section 3, "Round 9 fix passes and integration 9"). |
| B-12 (was P-14, **fixed**: every RAM variable at its GBA address) | `port/include/port.h`: `EWRAM_DATA`, `IWRAM_DATA` and `EWRAM_OVERLAY(id)` are empty on the host, and `PRELUDE_H` is now defined for every file (it was for B-7's file only). `port/Makefile`: `-fdata-sections` in `HOST_CFLAGS`; `RAM_CHECK` after the link of `fe8pc` (also the Gate 0 build) and a `ram-check` target. `port/tools/gen_host_link.py`: placement per variable. `port/tools/check_ram_addrs.py`: the check. `port/win32/pe_gba.py`: COFF readers and `check-ram`. `port/Makefile.sdl` and `port/Makefile.mingw`: the check after their links. Added 2026-10-19 (commit `3ab0231b`). | The GBA linker script places each object's `ewram_data`, `ewram_overlay_*` and `.bss` section, and agbcc lays out the variables inside each one in definition order, aligning `.lcomm` statics by size. Common variables are placed by name (`sym_iwram.txt`, for example `gText_GoldBox` at `0x03001DA0`). | Each variable of a host-built game file has its own section (`.bss.NAME` or `.data.NAME`; `.data$NAME` in a MinGW object). `gen_host_link.py` puts each one at its GBA address in the NOLOAD `.gba_ewram*`/`.gba_iwram*` sections, with `SUBALIGN(1)` so a host alignment can never move it: a global at its value in the GBA ELF; a static at its offset in the GBA object (`src/x.o`) plus the GBA map's base of that object's section; a function-static (GCC `name.N`, agbcc `name.M`) by its name without the suffix, within the same file. After every link `check_ram_addrs.py` reads each data symbol of each game object, takes its final address from the link map, and compares it with the GBA address of the same variable in the same file. It also checks the absolute RAM symbols against the GBA ELF, and that no other symbol sits in GBA RAM. Any difference fails the build and removes the binary. | P-14: GCC's emit order and alignment moved 38 variables (details below). The check fails on the old layout (exit 1, 38 at another address) and passes on the new one, on Linux, SDL and Windows: now 617 RAM symbols, 430 host-built variables and 187 absolute symbols, all at their GBA addresses. (At `3ab0231b`: 613 = 425 + 188. The audio merge `0da9e831` builds `m4a.o`, which adds 5 host-built variables (B-13); one of them, `gSoundInfo`, was an absolute symbol before.) `gen_host_link` warnings: 5 before, 0 now. No game behaviour changed in the checked runs: the Gate 0 PC trace is byte-identical before and after on all 60,000 ticks. The GBA build compiles nothing in `port/`. |
| B-13 (sound driver built; was audio-design B-6) | `port/Makefile:91-98`: `src/m4a.c` is no longer in `FULL_EXCLUDE`, and `m4a.o` gets `-Dasm(x)=((void)0)`; `port/tools/layout_probe.py:51` copies the flag (`PER_FILE_HOST`). | B-15 (retail loop bound kept; found by the NULL probe) | `port/Makefile`: `-fno-aggressive-loop-optimizations` in `HOST_CFLAGS`, next to B-5's flag and of the same kind, so every PC build that compiles the shared game files gets it: headless core and `FULL=1`, `Makefile.sdl` (includes `port/Makefile`), `Makefile.mingw` (reuses its `HOST_CFLAGS`) and the Gate 0 test build (`port/test/Makefile pc`); the m4a difftest reads `HOST_CFLAGS` from it too (the text difftest builds port code only, with `PORT_CFLAGS`), and `port/tools/layout_probe.py` copies the flag since `port-followups` `ff809647`. `PORT_CFLAGS` and every GBA build flag are unchanged. Added 2026-10-19 night (`port-integrate` `9da96127`). | `PromoHandler_SetupAndStartUI` (`src/classchg-handler.c:79-117` at `9da96127`; the loop is lines 106-161 on `port-integrate2`, after D-36; Chapter 4 battle preparations; `PromoHandlerIdle`) runs the retail `for (i = 0; i < 7; i++)` over the 3-entry local `trainees[]` (the decomp's `BUGFIX` path has `i < 3`). agbcc keeps the end test, so the GBA runs 7 iterations and reads `trainees[3..6]` from its own stack: a retail bug that stays as it is. | GCC -O1 by default (`-faggressive-loop-optimizations`) takes the reads of `trainees[3..6]` as undefined behaviour and removed the end test: the compiled loop (`add $0x4,%ebx`, no compare) walked up the host stack until an entry looked promotable (NULL probe, `port-plan/pending/merged/nullprobe.md` finding 2 and group H: at least 17,825,792 probe hits from tick 67,669, then a crash at 67,699). With the flag the bound is back: `add $0x4,%ebx; lea 0x2c(%esp),%eax; cmp %ebx,%eax; je` (0x2c = 0x10 + 7 × 4) in `build/pc-full`, `build/pc-sdl` and `build/pc-gate0`, and `lea 0x40(%esp)` (0x24 + 7 × 4) in `build/win32`. The PC now runs the GBA's 7 iterations. Before D-36 it then read its own stack past the array (the stack-protector canary as `trainees[3]`, stale frame padding as `[4..6]`), where the GBA reads its own (the spill slot of `i` and the caller's `r8`-`r10`). Since D-36 (group H, `port-integrate2` `042e56a1`) the PC's array holds the GBA's values for `[3..6]`, so this loop stays in bounds and reads what the GBA reads, with or without the flag; the flag stays for the other loops of this kind. | Same reason as B-5: the host compiler must not turn retail undefined behaviour into a different control flow. The flag changes the code of 67 functions in 33 game objects (FULL build compared function by function against a scratch build without it); it only drops optimisations that assume no out-of-bounds access, so defined code keeps its meaning. Checks: section 3, "Round 2 integration". The GBA build does not read `port/Makefile`. |
