**Rejected build option (round 9, D-52).** GCC 15's argument evaluation order cannot be set for C: `-maccumulate-outgoing-args` and `-mno-push-args` leave the gimplified order right to left (`-fstrong-eval-order` is C++ only). The sites are sequenced in the source instead (section 1, "Argument evaluation order").

## 3. Port-layer stand-ins for hardware (no shared-file edit)

| ID | Where | What the GBA does | What the PC does |
|---|---|---|---|
| H-1 | `port/hw/main_pc.c` fault handler. Turn it off with `FE8PC_DROP_BIOS_WRITES=0` | Ignores game writes below `0x4000` (BIOS area). | Decodes the faulting x86 store, skips it and logs the site once. Exact. First seen at `AnimDelete` (`animedrv.c:170`), frame 8,349 of attract mode. **Round 10 (was working ID D-R10-DEMONLIGHT-2; `pending/folded/r10-demonlight.md` section 6):** `EkrDragonBodyAnimeSet54` (`src/banim-ekrdragon-demonking.c:563`, GBA store `0x08077070`, the decomp's `BUGFIX` site): a 32-bit store of -1 at `0x54` through a NULL `Proc_Find` result on the first round of each Demon King battle. The GBA ignores it (measured r0 = 0, also s1 8,783, the review's); every PC build compiles it to `movl $-1,0x54(%eax)`, which H-1 drops (plain PC, same ticks, every traced field equal). Exact. And (was D-R10-DEMONLIGHT-3, no code) `Proc_StartBlocking` on a tree root (`proc.c:105` and `:131`, `lockCnt` through the root index at n + 0x28; callers `bmusailment.c:498` and `worldmap_effect.c:461` with `PROC_TREE_3`, and any `Proc_StartBlocking(..., NULL)`): the GBA's `ldrb`/`strb` result is dropped with the store; the PC's single x86 read-modify-write (`addb`/`subb $1,0x28(%reg)`, every build) is skipped by H-1. Exact by the code; not reached in any run. |
| H-2 | `port/asm/arm_pc.c` `ClearOam` | Same: `SyncHiOam` clears through a NULL buffer on the first VBlank, before `InitOam`. | Drops writes below `0x4000` and logs them. Exact. |
| H-3 | none (decision of 2026-10-19) | Game reads below `0x4000` return the last fetched BIOS opcode (open bus). | Not emulated. Each such read faults, and each one needs its own guarded fix, listed in section 1 (the shared helpers are in "Open-bus latch rule"). Diagnostic only, since `port-integrate2`: with `FE8PC_NULLPROBE=1` (`port/hw/nullprobe.c`) the PC returns the latch instead, records the site and plays on, so one run lists every site (section 3, "Round 3 integration"). With the variable unset every build behaves as before. **Division traps (since `port-tas2` `cdcc59a4`, on `port-integrate5`):** the same switch turns on `PortNullProbe_OnDivFault`. A SIGFPE from a 32-bit `div`/`idiv` with a plain 32-bit dividend gets libgcc's results (quotient and remainder 0 for a zero divisor; `INT_MIN` and 0 for `INT_MIN / -1`; read from the ELF's `__divsi3`, `__modsi3`, `__udivsi3`, `__umodsi3`), the instruction is skipped and the site recorded (`how` `div0` or `div-overflow`). Everything else stays fatal: 8- and 16-bit divides, a 64-bit dividend, quotient overflow, SSE and x87 exceptions, a SIGFPE sent by hand (the integration review's 38 cases at -O0 to -O2; unset or `0`, every trap is fatal). Only the quotient and remainder are the GBA's: code GCC arranged on the assumption that the divisor is not 0 could still differ (a compare would show it). `nullprobe.c` is in `PORT_SRCS` of every Linux i386 build, the SDL release build `fe8pc-sdl` included (with the variable set it prints `[nullprobe] on: ... integer division traps get the GBA libgcc result`); the Windows builds compile stubs. **Open user choice** (review): keep the env-var gate in release Linux builds, as decided for the read probe, or compile the probe into test builds only (TB-1). `Record()` calls `backtrace()` inside the signal handler, so a trap in code without correct unwind information (hand-written asm only) dies with SIGSEGV instead of the probe's report. |
| H-4 (live input, **exact**) | `port/platform.h` (the library-neutral interface of PLAN 3.7), `port/hw/platform_headless.c` (no window, no live keys), `port/sdl3/platform_sdl3.c` (the only file that includes SDL), `port/hw/frame.c`. Added 2026-10-19 (`port-sdl`, merged in `61905c3d`). | `REG_KEYINPUT` shows the buttons as they are, active low; the game samples it once per frame in `UpdateKeyStatus` (`OnMain`). A D-pad cannot press two opposite directions. | Each frame step presents the frame, paces (H-5), polls the platform and writes the live keys to the `REG_KEYINPUT` image, active low, just before the next `OnMain` reads it (timing-input.md). Opposite directions held together read as neither (mGBA's default, as a real D-pad). The user's keys: A = Z, B = X, L = Q, R = E, Start = Enter, Select = Backspace, D-pad = arrows; gamepads by button label. `FE8PC_AUTOPRESS` is still ORed in. Headless builds have no live keys, so they run as before. No new divergence: the sampling point and the D-pad rule match hardware. |
| H-5 (frame pacing) | `port/hw/pacer.c`. Added 2026-10-19 (`port-sdl`, merged in `61905c3d`). | One frame is 280,896 cycles at 16,777,216 Hz: 59.7275 Hz. | 16,742,706 ns per frame plus a carried 153/512 ns, exactly 280896/16777216 s. After a stall of more than 250 ms the schedule restarts instead of racing to catch up. On by default for the window, off headless (`FE8PC_PACE` overrides). Presentation (VSync off) never drives logic. Method adapted from pokeemerald-expansion-native `platform/frame_pacer.c` (MIT; no code copied). Measured: 3,584 frames in 60.006 s (59.7274 Hz) in the window. |
| H-6 (sound timer, FIFOs and DMA; was audio-design H-4) | `port/audio/fifo_pc.c` (new), called by the frame step (`port/hw/frame.c:99-108`: `PortAudio_PreVBlank` before and `PortAudio_OnVBlank` after the VBlank IRQ of line 160); the output queue `port/audio/audio_out.c`; the device in `port/sdl3/platform_sdl3.c:373-448` (headless builds have none). Added 2026-10-19 (`port-audio` `c453b5e4`, merged in `0da9e831`). | Timer 0 overflows every 1,254 cycles, 224 times a frame. Each overflow plays one byte from FIFO A and one from FIFO B, refilled by DMA1 and DMA2 from the driver's PCM ring (two lanes of 7 slots of 224 bytes). `m4aSoundVSync` re-arms both DMAs when its counter reloads. | Once per VBlank, after the game's handler: `c = SOUND_INFO_PTR->pcmDmaCounter`, `p = period - c`; a DMA (1 or 2) that is enabled with FIFO timing and aimed at FIFO A or B plays bytes `[SAD + p*224, +224)`; otherwise that FIFO is silent. The bytes go with the PSG (H-7) through the DAC model (routing by `SOUNDCNT_H`, bias, 10-bit clip, 8-bit resolution): 224 stereo samples per frame at 13,379 Hz, with no filter and no resampling by the port (SDL converts the rate). No VBlank is skipped, since the PC never interrupts game code. Not modelled: a FIFO DMA left running without the VBlank IRQ (FE8 never does it; the model plays silence). The queue (decision D4) holds about 3 VBlanks and steers SDL's rate by at most 0.5%; it never waits and never runs an extra frame. `FE8PC_AUDIO=0` opens no device. Checked against mGBA by replay (P3.3 compare 3, rerun on the merge): PCM lanes equal on all 6,487 VBlanks of the title run and all 16,287 of the scripted route. The FIFO byte stream equals the GBA's for 2,124 VBlanks (35.5 s) of the title theme. The window build's samples equal the headless build's byte for byte. |
| H-7 (PSG register polling; was audio-design H-5) | `port/audio/psg.c` and `psg.h` (new; `PortPsg_Latch` `psg.c:853`, `PortPsg_Render` `psg.c:879`), called from `fifo_pc.c`. Derived from pokeemerald-expansion-native `platform/psg*.c` (MIT; licence at the top of each file). Added 2026-10-19 (`port-audio` `e6b1f998` and `c453b5e4`, merged in `0da9e831`). | The four Game Boy channels act on each register write when it happens. NRx4 bit 7 (Initial) starts a channel and reads 0. A write to the wave RAM window goes to the bank that is not playing. The 512 Hz frame sequencer has a phase fixed by boot timing. | The model reads the register image once per VBlank, after `OnVBlank`. A set NRx4 bit 7 counts as one trigger and is cleared in the image, so the image reads like the GBA's. Main-thread writes (`CgbOscOff` from `TrackStop`) take effect at the next VBlank, up to one frame late. A wave window that changed since the last latch goes to the bank selected at the latch, which is right for the driver's `NR30 = 0x40`, write, `NR30 = 0` (`m4a.c:929-938`). The frame sequencer starts when the master enable is first seen, so its phase against VBlank differs from the GBA's and PSG audio is not bit-exact; the register stream is (P3.4a: `gCgbChans` and the readable register bits equal mGBA's on every VBlank without a main-thread sound call). Not modelled, and unused by FE8: power-on and trigger-timing quirks, the NR10 negate quirk, the wave channel's first-sample delay, wave RAM reads while playing, noise shifts 14-15. Unit tests: 547 checks, 0 failed (`make -f port/test/audio/psg.mk`); no floating point in `psg.o`. |
| H-8 (SOUNDBIAS boot value, **fixed**; `sound-wiring.md` proposed H-6) | `port/hw/memory.c:118` (cold boot) and `port/bios/bios.c:259` (`RegisterRamReset` with the sound flag), both through `PortPsg_ResetIo` (`psg.c:843`). Added 2026-10-19 (`port-audio` `c453b5e4`, merged in `0da9e831`). | The BIOS leaves SOUNDBIAS at 0x0200 at boot [GBATEK], and `RegisterRamReset` with the sound flag clears the sound registers and leaves SOUNDBIAS at 0x0200, as mGBA's HLE BIOS does. The game calls it after a soft reset (`main.c:34`). | The same. Before: SOUNDBIAS started at 0, so the bias was 0 and every negative DirectSound sample clipped to 0 (`SOUNDBIAS_H` read 0x40, not 0x42; `CgbSound` takes the same branch either way). Exact. |
| H-9 (the player's save file; P-15 **fixed**) | `port/save/savefile_pc.c` (new); `port/hw/main_pc.c` (`PortParseArgs`, line 258: `fe8pc [--save PATH \| --save=PATH \| --no-save] [--] [ROM]`; `PortSave_Init` at line 349, after `PortMemInit` and the NULL probe's init, before `AgbMain`; `PortSave_Flush` in `PortExit`); `port/hw/frame.c:235` (`PortSave_Frame` at the end of every frame step); `port/platform.h:86-104` (`Platform_SaveFileAtomic(path, tmpPath, data, size)`, `Platform_UserDir`, `Platform_SetTitleNote`), both backends, and `port/win32/posix_win32.c` (the ROM search uses the same parser; `PortWin32FinalPath`). Added 2026-10-20 (`port-savefile` `f02cb394`, merged on `port-integrate4` in `77e22f33`; links, one temporary file per process, the per-user fallback and the title note in `68f7ea0f`). PLAN.md phase 5, work item 1. | The cartridge's battery keeps the 32 KiB SRAM between sessions. An emulator keeps it in a `.sav` file: mGBA writes exactly the 32,768 bytes of FE8's SRAM, with no header. | **Format:** the raw 32 KiB SRAM image (SRAM `0x0000-0x7FFF`), mGBA's `.sav`, so saves copy between mGBA and the PC both ways. **Which file:** `--save PATH` (any build); in the window build only, `FE8PC_SAVE=PATH` (`none` for no file) and by default the ROM path with `.sav` for its extension (`baserom.gba` gives `baserom.sav`), as mGBA names it; `--no-save` for none. The headless builds (`fe8pc`, the Gate 0 build, `fe8pc.exe`) are the test builds: they never read `FE8PC_SAVE` and keep a save only with `--save`, so a value left in the environment cannot reach a gate. Window build, default file only: when no file can be made in the ROM's folder, the file of that name in `Platform_UserDir()` (SDL's pref path: `$XDG_DATA_HOME/fe8pc/` or `~/.local/share/fe8pc/`, `%APPDATA%\fe8pc\` on Windows), logged once and shown in the window title for the run; if that file does not exist yet but one is next to the ROM, that one is loaded and the writes go to the per-user file. **Load** (before `SramInit`, `main.c:55`): the first 32 KiB into SRAM (`0x0E000000`); no file or a 0-byte file (mGBA makes one before the first save) gives the blank SRAM (`0xFF`) and the file is written at the game's first SRAM write (`SramInit` writes on every boot); a 64 KiB file (a `GATE0_SRAM` dump) gives its first 32 KiB (D-18) and is written back as 32 KiB; any other size, or a file that cannot be read, stops the run before the game starts, so it is never overwritten with data the game did not load from it. **Write:** at the end of every frame step, when the 32 KiB differ from what the file holds (a `memcmp`, so any SRAM write counts), all of it goes to `PATH.tmp.<pid>`, is flushed (`fsync`, `_commit` on Windows, `SDL_FlushIO`), then renamed over the file (`MoveFileExA` with `REPLACE_EXISTING` and `WRITE_THROUGH` on Windows, `SDL_RenamePath`), so a crash or a power cut leaves the old file or the new one. A `.sav` that is a symbolic link is followed once at start-up and written through (`GetFinalPathNameByHandle` on Windows), as mGBA writes in place, so the link stays a link. `PortExit` writes once more; a fault (`_exit`) or a kill does not, so the file holds the SRAM of the last frame step. A failed write is logged (3 lines, then quiet), retried every 60 frame steps, shown as `SAVE FAILED: cannot write PATH` in the window title until a write works, and reported at exit. Without it every save was lost at exit (P-15). Nothing changes what the game sees: it reads and writes SRAM in memory as before (the Gate 0 trace and raw dump with `--save` equal those without it). Checks (`pending/merged/savefile.md`, its review and `integrate4.md`): a fresh boot's `.sav` equals the GBA's SRAM (P5.1) and mGBA's own `.sav` byte for byte; mGBA saves load on the PC exactly as through `GATE0_SRAM_IN`; PC-written saves boot in mGBA and resume as on the PC (`pro.sav`: `LAG-ONLY` on 0-2,599, end SRAM equal; `ch2_play.sav`: `LAG-ONLY` on 0-1,827, then P-21); 52 kills at every stage of a write (review) left the old file or the new one, each loading fine; the Windows exe natively (`MoveFileExA` over an existing file, a locked target retried with no temp file left); links (a new file, a chain of 3, a dangling link, a loop that stops the run), two runs at once, a read-only ROM folder (the per-user file, title set), read-only fallback folders (SAVE FAILED in the title). A write takes 1.4 to 13.5 ms with `fsync` (WSL ext4; 1.8 ms on NTFS) inside the frame step. What still differs from the GBA's SRAM at the same tick: the play-time fields (the lag-free clock, P-12 cause 3), the uninitialised stack bytes retail writes (P-18) and the checksums over them, and the Guide flags (P-20). **Open user choices** (HANDOFF, "waiting on the user"; `integrate4.md` decision 3): the default location (next to the ROM, as mGBA; done) with the per-user fallback (done), and whether a missing per-user file should seed from the `.sav` next to a read-only ROM (as now) or start blank. Not run: a person playing in the window (only SDL's offscreen driver), a Windows SDL build, a symbolic link on Windows, kill tests of the new temporary name, P5.3 (all three slots), a chapter-end save by the PC (the base of `port-savefile` stopped at tick 3,714; `port-integrate4` gets past the Prologue's save at 5,008, not rerun with `--save`). Any future test that runs `fe8pc-sdl` must pass `--no-save`. With `GATE0_SRAM_IN` and `--save` together the hook's bytes reach the file at the first frame; the gates never pass `--save`. |
| D-6 (was P-5, **fixed**) | `port/asm/arm_pc.c`: `MapFloodCoreStep` and `MapFloodCore`, C rewrites of `asm/arm.s` (GBA `0x08000784` and `0x08000874`), per PLAN.md 3.5. No shared file and no build flag changed; the GBA build does not compile `port/`. It is filed here, as the port layer, under the ID it was given. Added 2026-10-19. | `GenerateMovementMap` (`bmidoten.c:79-111`) seeds the pool and calls the ARM flood through `CallARM_FillMovementMap`. The flood swaps two pools each pass and, for each source cell, steps to the neighbours other than the one it came from. The branch table at `0x080008D4` gives, per `connexion`: 0 = up, down, left; 1 = up, down, right; 2 = down, left, right; 3 = up, left, right; 4 = end of pass; 5 = all four. A step is taken when `cost = moveCost[terrain] + map[src]` is below the target cell (`bhs`), the target holds no unit of the other side (bit 7 of `gBmMapUnit ^ unitId`, only when `hasUnit`), and `cost <= movement` (`bhi`). | The same, line for line. It follows the asm where it differs from the C comment in `arm.s` and from the C variant `sub_801A570`: the source x and y are read as unsigned bytes (`ldrb`), the cost sum is not truncated before the two unsigned compares, and x or y = -1 indexes the map's safety border (`BmMapInit`) with the same 32-bit address arithmetic. A `connexion` above 5, which the asm would send into the middle of case 5 and the game never writes, stops the run with a log line. Exact: not a divergence, only a missing port piece. Before it, the stubs left the movement map unfilled (all `0xFF` but the start cell), so `GenerateBestMovementScript` (`bmidoten.c:146-262`) random-walked across equal costs and `RevertMovementScript` copied more than `MOVE_CMD_MAX_COUNT` (0x40) bytes into its stack buffer: the frame-12,364 stack smash. Result: the scripted run passes 12,364 with no smash and runs to frame 14,139 (P-6). Attract mode (60,000 frames, no input) is unchanged: DISPCNT `0x3F00`, 61,742 non-zero VRAM bytes, 264 palette entries. GBA check: `fireemblem8.gba: OK`. |
| D-8 (was P-7, **fixed**) | `port/bios/bios.c`: `CpuSet` and `CpuFastSet` (the C stand-ins for SWI 0x0B and 0x0C). No shared file. Added 2026-10-19. | A copy whose source is in the BIOS area does nothing. GBATEK ("BIOS Memory Copy"): the two functions "silently reject to do anything if the source start or end addresses are reaching into the BIOS area". mGBA's HLE BIOS does the same (`GBASwi16`: a source below the EWRAM region logs "Cannot CpuSet from BIOS" and skips the SWI). The game relies on it: `UpdateBanimFrame` (`banim-ekrmain.c:233` and `263`) copies `gBanimTriAtkPalettes[0]` and `[1]` to OBJ palettes 8 and 10, and both are NULL for every animation that is not a ballista. So the palettes keep their contents. | Both return without copying when the source start is below `0x4000`, or when a moving source runs past `0xFFFFFFFF` (on a 32-bit bus it would wrap into the BIOS area). The end of a fixed source (`CpuFill*`) is not tested: it reads one unit, and the fill value can sit on the host stack near `0xFFFFFFFF`, where start plus length wraps although nothing is read there. The BIOS's own test (address bits 25-27 clear) is not copied, because host code and data at `0x10000000` and up have those bits clear too. The first hit from each caller is logged (`[bios-copy]`). Exact: no copy, as on the GBA. Before, the read of address 0 faulted at frame 14,140. Result: the scripted run (`FE8PC_AUTOPRESS=0x001,90,4300`) skips the two copies at frame 14,140 (host callers at lines 233 and 263) and stops at frame 15,515 (P-8), the same in two runs. A zero-length `CpuFastSet` from NULL at frame 0 is now logged too (`SyncHiOam`, `hardware.c:1040`, before `InitOam`, the H-2 case). It copied nothing before either. Attract mode (60,000 frames, no input) is unchanged: DISPCNT `0x3F00`, 61,742 non-zero VRAM bytes (FNV-1a `0x50C426A8`), 264 palette entries. GBA check: `fireemblem8.gba: OK`. |
| D-11 (BIOS math, **fixed**) | `port/bios/bios_math.c` (new; the old routines were removed from `port/bios/bios.c`), with `bios_math.h`, `bios_sine_table.inc` and its generator `gen_sine_table.py`: `Div`, `DivRem`, `DivArm`, `Sqrt`, `ArcTan2`, `BgAffineSet`, `ObjAffineSet` (SWI 6, 7, 8, 10, 14, 15) and the port-internal SWI 9 `PortBiosArcTan`. Any other x/0 never returns (GBATEK: "endless loop"). INT_MIN / -1 = INT_MIN rem 0. ArcTan and ArcTan2 use a fixed-point polynomial. The affine SWIs use a 256-entry sine table with truncated values, and integer math with arithmetic shifts. | The same results. Before this fix: x/0 returned 0, INT_MIN / -1 crashed the host (x86 `idiv` raises SIGFPE), and the affine SWIs used floating point (not bit-exact). For an x/0 that never returns on the GBA, the port logs one line (`[bios] Div: ...`) and then runs frames forever, so interrupts go on and the game code stops, as on the GBA. Checks: all 123 hardware vectors of mgba-emu/suite `bios-math.c` pass (Div 4/4, ArcTan 24/24, ArcTan2 95/95), and the game's own entry points pass on the same rows; 4,676,017 checks in all, 0 failed (also 0 failed with `-O2`, UBSan and no `-fwrapv`); Sqrt equals floor(sqrt) on all 2^32 inputs; `bios_math.o` has no floating-point code. In the Gate 0 scripted compare the affine change shows up only against mGBA's HLE BIOS (section 5). Attract mode (60,000 frames) and the scripted run are not changed by it. Details and open choices below. |
| D-12 (text routines, **fixed**) | `port/asm/arm_pc.c`: `DrawGlyph` and `DecodeString`, C rewrites of `asm/arm.s` (GBA `0x08000564` and `0x080006E4`), reached through `ramfunc_pc.c` (`DrawGlyphRam`, `CallARM_DecompText`). Tests: `port/test/difftest/` (new files only). No shared file. Added 2026-10-19 (commit `6ef17770`, merged in `0e4d6678`). | `DecodeString` Huffman-decodes one message. Source bytes are read with `ldrb`, bits from bit 0 up, and the bit buffer carries over between characters. `gMsgHuffmanTableRoot` is read once. A node is a leaf only when bit 31 is set (bits 16-30 are ignored). A leaf with bits 8-15 set stores both bytes, low first, even when the low byte is 0; otherwise the low byte is stored, and 0 ends the string. `DrawGlyph` draws one 16x16 2bpp glyph into 4bpp text tiles: each row is `umull`'d by `bitTable[subx]` (`1 << 2*subx`), each result byte is looked up in the colour table, and the result is ORed into `dst[0]`, `dst[0x10]` and `dst[0x20]`, in that order. | The same, line for line (comments in `arm_pc.c`). For a `subx` outside 0-7 (the game always passes `x & 7`) it reads the same ROM word past `bitTable` as the asm, and logs once. Exact: not a divergence, only a missing port piece. Checks: a Unicorn difftest runs the decomp's ARM code from `fireemblem8.elf` against the port's `arm_pc.o` on 23,031 calls (all 3,404 real messages, random and fuzzed Huffman tables, real and fuzzed glyphs, overlapping buffers); every dump and CRC is identical. `mutate_text.py`: all 11 behaviour-changing mutants are caught. The host's `gMsgHuffmanTable` and all 3,404 compressed messages are byte-identical to the ROM's. Before it, the `DecodeString` stub wrote no text, so talks ended at once (the old Gate 0 difference at tick 8,628). Result: the scripted run now plays the Prologue talks as the GBA does and stops at frame 15,800 (P-11). That is earlier than the old P-10 stop at 21,581, which the run only reached because the talks ended early. Attract mode changes, as expected now that text is drawn: 63,604 non-zero VRAM bytes (FNV-1a `0xE8A769C8`), was 61,742 (`0x50C426A8`); DISPCNT `0x3F00` and 264 palette entries are unchanged. The `port-text` worktree's own binary gives the same attract numbers and the same 15,800 stop. Not verified: VRAM is not in the Gate 0 trace, so on-screen text pixels were not compared with the GBA. |
| D-15 (renderer, **new port piece**) | `port/render/` (new): `render.c` and `render.h` (top level), `bg_line.c`, `bg_affine.c`, `obj_line.c`, `window.c`, `blend.c`, `out_rgba.c`, and the unit tests `test_bg.c` and `test_obj.c`. It then copies the registers and BG rows across (`RENDER_FX_REGS_FROM`, `RENDER_COMPOSE_BG_FROM`) and runs the OBJ half (`RenderObjFxLine`), which writes the 240-pixel BGR555 row. **F2:** `RenderBeginFrame` at line 0; `RenderLine` after that line's VCount and VBlank IRQs and before its HBlank IRQ; `RenderEndFrame` after line 159, which calls `RenderHashFrame555` and `RenderFrameToRgba8888` (`out_rgba.c`, the only copy of each). **F3:** a log line when the tile queue holds more than 32 entries; it never fired. `RenderInit` runs at power-on and after a soft reset. The default blend math is the GBA's 5-bit math. Switches: `FE8PC_RENDER=0` skips drawing (logic-only runs: 60,000 frames in 0.2 s instead of 41.7 s); `FE8PC_BLEND=mgba8` uses mGBA's 8-bit blend math, for checkpoint compares only; `FE8PC_DUMP_FRAMES=a-b` with `FE8PC_DUMP_DIR` writes PPM files. Not a divergence: the PPU was missing. Checks: BG unit tests 22 passed, 0 failed (136,034 checks); OBJ 18 passed, 0 failed; the P2.1 first-picture run and the merge check below. **Open user choice:** the shipped blend math (OBJ design open question 1), the 5-bit hardware math (default) or mGBA's 8-bit math. Details below. **OBJ transparent pixels (2026-10-21, round 9, cov9 G8; `port-integrate8` `b3a86d7d`).** Sprites are drawn in OAM order, and a colour sprite's transparent pixel over an earlier sprite's pixel of a higher priority value gives that pixel this sprite's priority and semi-transparent mode (mGBA 0.10.5 `software-obj.c`, the `FLAG_UNWRITTEN` branch; pinned by the new PPU test ROM `port/test/oracle/pputest`, variants 0 and 1, 30 of 30 pixels, and `test_obj.c` RT19). The renderer had skipped transparent pixels, so the preparations item screens showed the wrong sprite in a few pixels (G8); `obj_line.c` `ObjPlot` now follows the rule (a clear pixel over nothing is skipped before the call: the same result, at less cost). Limits found by the review with more test scenes: with brighten or darken and OBJ as first target, mGBA's lifted pixel keeps its pre-effect colour or misses the effect while the PC applies the effect when it composites (4 of 10 such scenes differ from mGBA; the PC's way looks like the hardware rule but is unproven; FE8 has such a blend in `prep_menuproc.c`, no run has shown it, and a compare would report it, not hide it); and the OBJ-window part (an OBJ-window sprite lifts nothing) is mGBA 0.10.5's, which mGBA 0.11 changed (mgba.io/i/2809), so it is an oracle choice, not a proven hardware rule (section 5, OL-6's note). Gate 0 runs also set `FE8PC_MGBA_BGCLIP=1` (`bg_line.c` `RenderMgbaBgClip`): OL-6. |
| D-16 (DISPSTAT status bits, **fixed**) | `port/hw/frame.c`, the BG design's F1. No shared file. Added 2026-10-19 (commit `a843f823`, merged in `22c2b06a`). | DISPSTAT bits 0-2 (VBlank, HBlank, VCount match) are read-only and always show the current line state. Added 2026-10-28 (`port-lock3` `1b291f19`; merged on `port-integrate14` `a4edbcab`). | n/a (harness) | `FE8_COMPARE_SLOTS=N` (default 1: one long compare at a time, as before; a value that is not a number counts as 1) lets N long compares run at once: slot 0 is the lock file itself (`~/.fe8-compare.lock`), slot k > 0 is `<lock>.k`. A row tries each slot without waiting and takes the first free one; while all are busy it tries again every 2 s (with N = 1 it waits on the one lock, as before). No compare, trace or row result changes, only how many long rows run at once (the code's note: each long compare peaks at about 3.5 GB, so N follows the WSL memory). | Self-tested by its author (HANDOFF.md). CHECK_LONG with `FE8_COMPARE_SLOTS=3`: integrations 14 (`a4edbcab`, 14:01-21:41 on 2026-10-28) and 15 (`50335899`, 21:49-05:54) each end as integration 13's one-slot run did (01:48-09:49): 139 PASS, 4 KNOWN (`route-i21-lag`, `route-br9-lag`, `route-er3-lag`, `route-gallery-lag`), 0 fail. |

### D-11 details: BIOS math, routine by routine

| Routine | Behaviour now | Where it comes from | Checked by |
|---|---|---|---|
| `Div`, `DivRem`, `DivArm` | Divide the magnitudes as unsigned 32-bit numbers; quotient sign = sign of num XOR denom; remainder sign = sign of num; r3 = quotient magnitude. denom 0: q = 1, r = abs(num) for abs(num) <= 1, signs as above. Otherwise the BIOS never returns. | Written for the port from GBATEK (outputs r0, r1, r3; the -1234/10 example; "endless loop" on /0) and the 4 suite hardware vectors. Why exactly 0, 1 and -1 return is a model that fits the vectors [inferred]. No code from any emulator. | 4/4 hardware vectors (r0, r1, r3) and the game entry points on the same rows; exact division on 200,228 pairs; 24 never-returning cases. |
| `Sqrt` | floor(sqrt(x)). Code unchanged (bit-by-bit integer square root, our own). | GBATEK ("Sqrt(2) would return 1"). No hardware vectors, so floor on hardware is [inferred]. | All 2^32 inputs (`make -f port/test/bios/Makefile exhaustive`). |
| `PortBiosArcTan` (SWI 9) | s = -(t*t >> 14); Horner polynomial in s with 8 constants; r0 = t*p >> 16. 32-bit wrapping products, arithmetic shifts, negate after the shift. ArcTan2 uses it; the game does not call SWI 9 itself. | Clean rewrite (below). The 8 constants are BIOS facts, pinned by the hardware vectors. | 24/24 hardware vectors on r0, r1 and r3. |
| `ArcTan2` | y = 0: 0 or 0x8000. x = 0: 0x4000 or 0xC000. If abs(x) > abs(y): base + ArcTan((y << 14) / x), base 0, 0x8000 (x < 0) or 0x10000 (x > 0, y < 0). Otherwise 0x4000 or 0xC000 minus ArcTan((x << 14) / y). The ratio rounds toward zero. | Clean rewrite (below), designed from GBATEK's output range and the vectors. | 95/95 hardware vectors on r0 and r1; the 24 rows that fit in s16 also through the game's `ArcTan2(s16, s16)`; 498,212 s16 points within 2/65536 of a turn of `atan2`, all 8 octants. |
| Sine table | Entry i = trunc(sin(2*pi*i/256) * 0x4000). | Generated by `port/bios/gen_sine_table.py`, not copied. It equals the "original BIOS" table in Cult-of-GBA (MIT) in all 256 entries (one-off check, not committed). Rounding instead would change 120 entries. | The test compares all 256 entries with libm `trunc(sin())`; `make` regenerates the file and compares it byte for byte. |
| `BgAffineSet` | pa = sx*cos >> 14, pb = -(sx*sin >> 14) (negate after the shift), pc = sy*sin >> 14, pd = sy*cos >> 14. dx = texX - pa*scrX - pb*scrY, dy = texY - pc*scrX - pd*scrY, 32-bit with wrap. Angle index = alpha >> 8. Entries 20 and 16 bytes. Integer only. | The formula in `community-reuse-audio-bios.md` 2.2 (VBA-M and Cult-of-GBA agree; facts only). Cult-of-GBA's MIT asm was read to cross-check the start point and entry sizes. Our own C. | Hand-worked cases; 300,000 random inputs against a separate 64-bit reference. |
| `ObjAffineSet` | Same pa..pd, written `offset` bytes apart. The source is read 8 bytes per entry. | As above. The 8-byte stride follows Cult-of-GBA [inferred for the real BIOS]. FE8 always passes count 1. | Same terms as BgAffineSet on 300,000 inputs. |

- **ArcTan and ArcTan2 clean rewrite.** The old code in `port/bios/bios.c` had the same constants and branch structure as mGBA's `_ArcTan`/`_ArcTan2` (MPL-2.0). It is deleted. The new code was written from a plain description (GBATEK, the constants as BIOS facts, the suite vectors) without opening mGBA's source, and has a different structure. It is not a formal two-person clean room: the same agent had read the old port code first. The suite vectors are in `port/test/bios/suite_vectors.h` as data, with the suite's MIT notice.
- **Open choice 1: Div by zero that never returns.** The findings file suggested "log once and carry on". That would let the game continue where the GBA freezes, so the port follows the GBA instead (log, then run frames forever). Gate 0 runs with no frame limit, so there it would hit the harness's 300-second `timeout`. "Log and continue" is a two-line change in `DivNeverReturns` if the user prefers it. No `[bios]` line appeared in any run so far.
- **Not confirmed on hardware:** Sqrt (no vectors); the two ArcTan2 octants without vectors (abs(x) > abs(y) with y < 0); BgAffineSet at scale -0x8000 (start point uses the 32-bit pa..pd; FE8 never passes it); the 8-byte ObjAffineSet source stride and "count 0 or less does nothing" (FE8 always passes 1). Check them when a real BIOS is available.
- `port/test/README.md` and `port/test/Makefile` do not list `port/test/bios` or `port/test/difftest` yet.

Merged from `port-plan/pending/renderer-integration.md` (branch `port-render`, worktree `~/fe8-render`; commits `f1b4561b`, `2e9f5edc` (merge of `port-d1a-spike` at `ee874c9f`, no conflicts) and `a843f823`). All changes are in `port/`. The fragment is kept in `port-plan/pending/merged/`.

That run still exits 1 on `gGameClock` and `prevVCount`, as before.

- **The scenes.** Health and safety runs from tick 2 to about tick 3,720 (it advances on its own). Then the Nintendo and Intelligent Systems logos, the story opening, the title lights (6,565-6,656), the logo zoom (6,751-6,792), and the title screen from about tick 6,800.

| 2 | First non-blank frame (health and safety fading in) | 0 |
| 300 | Health and safety | 0 |
| 3,840 | Intro, Nintendo logo | 0 |
| 4,020 | Intro, IS logo | 0 |
| 5,100 | Intro, story opening | 0 |
| 6,600 | Title, rotating lights | 1,965 |
| 6,770 | Title, logo zoom | 0 |
| 6,840 | Title screen ("Press START") | 0 |

- **Overall:** 442 of 530 frames are identical. The 88 that differ are ticks 6,568-6,655, the rotating lights. The cause is the oracle's BIOS, not the renderer (section 5).
- **Title screen, P2.2 start.**
  - Rotating lights, BG2 affine, rotated, 512x512 (P2.2b). Differing pixels for the first 60 ticks (6,568-6,627): 3256, 3308, 3423, 3620, 3594, 2691, 4032, 4802, 2039, 3419, 3819, 664, 4262, 971, 1366, 2194, 4608, 3278, 1448, 2135, 2344, 3499, 2837, 2911, 1407, 3176, 1727, 2822, 1883, 2141, 2211, 1050, 1965, 778, 2119, 1594, 404, 1015, 1921, 871, 1318, 1523, 1358, 12, 1313, 273, 1251, 495, 1209, 380, 893, 643, 178, 742, 1048, 585, 663, 520, 550, 801. Ticks 6,628-6,655 differ by 120 to 634 pixels each. Ticks 6,566, 6,567 and 6,656 have angle 0 and match.
  - Logo zoom, BG2 affine, angle 0 (P2.2a): all 42 frames (6,751-6,792) are pixel-identical.
- **Shipped blend math against mGBA.** The same 7,000-tick run with the default 5-bit math has 63 differing frames outside the lights (for example the story opening at 4,260-5,160). Most differences are one step in one channel, such as GBA 0x7FFF and PC 0x7FDF. This confirms the OBJ design's point that mGBA blends in 8-bit space, so checkpoints must use `FE8PC_BLEND=mgba8`. The shipped build keeps the GBA's 5-bit math (open user choice in D-15).
- **Tripwires.** T8 (OBJ cycle budget exceeded): first hit at frame 10,630, line 72, in the class demo. The renderer models this as mGBA does, so it is information only. No other tripwire or one-time render warning fired in 60,000 frames.

### D-18 details: agbcc struct layout (2026-10-19, commit `32ec6f81`)

**Method** (`port/tools/layout_probe.py`, B-9). For each of the 348 files the FULL host build compiles:
1. The host compiles the file with the port flags plus `-fno-eliminate-unused-debug-types`. The DWARF lists every struct and union the file can see, with its members.
2. A probe file `#include`s the source file and adds, per type: a constant array of `sizeof`, `__alignof__` and the offset of every non-bitfield member; for each bitfield, a constant object of the type with only that field set to all ones (its bit position is read from the bytes); and one object of the type.
3. agbcc compiles the probe the way the GBA `make` does (`cc -E`, `iconv`, agbcc `-O2 -g`, `as`), and the host compiles it with the port flags (`-m32` and the rest). A type that agbcc cannot see in that file (host-only headers: `port.h`, libc) fails there and is dropped.
4. The numbers come from each compiler's constant output, not from DWARF, because agbcc's DWARF has wrong member offsets in packed structs (`BlendCnt._unused` is at 2, its DWARF says 4). DWARF is used only for the type and member lists, member sizes, and the few types the probe cannot name (types in a function, or anonymous types used only by a variable).

**agbcc's rules, as measured.** Every struct and union that is not packed has alignment 4, so its size is a multiple of 4. A packed one keeps alignment 1 (`TrapData`, `SioSaveConf`, `ClassReelAnimScr`) or what `aligned` sets (`Unk_80A6FBC_pat1`: 2). Bitfields are packed bit by bit across the boundary of their type (`Dungeon`: 96 bits in 12 bytes). GCC 15 for i386 gives the alignment of the largest member, and starts a new unit for a bitfield that would cross one.

**Audit before the fix** (tree `22c2b06a`): 701 types; 126 differ. 56 differ in alignment only (the host has 1 or 2), 48 in size (the host size is not a multiple of 4), 1 in bitfield positions (`Dungeon`), and 21 in member offsets or member sizes that follow from the others. One of the 126, the anonymous `sUnknown_0203A60C` in `src/bmbattle.c` (3 bytes, GBA 4), showed up only once the probe also checked types used only by a variable.

**The 109 edited definitions** (all `AGBCC_STRUCT`; `Dungeon` also `packed`):
`include/anime.h`: the anonymous `object` struct inside `AnimSpriteData.as`; `include/bmdifficulty.h`: Dungeon, Outer080D7FD0, Struct020038C8, Struct080D7FD0; `include/bmfx.h`: VectorBmfx; `include/bmguide.h`: GuideEnt, GuideSt; `include/ctc.h`: OAM_Attr0, oam2_data; `include/ekrbattle.h`: BanimRoundScript, BattleAnimDef; `include/ekrdragon.h`: EkrDragonQuakePriv; `include/ending_details.h`: EndingDefeatEnt; `include/event.h`: Struct03000428; The other 34 have another cause (P-14, fixed since by B-12).

**Gates** (logs in `~/fe8-port/build/d18_*.log`, not committed):
- GBA build: `fireemblem8.gba: OK`, SHA-1 `c25b145e37456171ada4b0d440bf88a19f4d509f`, after rebuilding all 358 C files.
- PC full build (`FULL=1`, from clean): links with 0 warnings; the static asserts of `layout_asserts.c` pass. Renderer unit tests: BG 22 passed, 0 failed (136,034 checks); OBJ 18 passed, 0 failed.
- Scripted run (`FE8PC_AUTOPRESS=0x001,90,4300`): reaches the 60,000-frame limit, exit 0, no fault; 55,744 `OnMain` calls; DISPCNT `0x3F00`, 65,565 non-zero VRAM bytes (FNV-1a `0x11347C3B`), 311 palette entries. The same as before D-18.
- Attract mode (60,000 frames, no input): exit 0; DISPCNT `0x3F00`, 63,604 non-zero VRAM bytes (FNV-1a `0xE8A769C8`), 264 palette entries. Unchanged.

**SRAM compare at tick 60,000** (Gate 0 key file `scripted_a90_long.keys` on both sides; mGBA 0.10.5 with the same ROM; `GATE0_SRAM` on both sides; output `build/gate0/{gba,pc}_d18.sram`, not committed). Before D-18 (P-12): 22,982 of 65,536 bytes differed. Now 19,747, of which 19,702 are the upper 32 KiB: FE8's SRAM is 32 KiB, mGBA shows it mirrored at `0x0E008000`, and the PC leaves that half at `0xFF`. The save code never goes past `0x8000` (`WipeSram` clears 0x8000 bytes; the last block, xmap, starts at `0x7400`), so a 32 KiB save image is not affected. In the 32 KiB save area, **45 bytes differ**, and all of them are expected leftovers:

| Where (SRAM offset) | Bytes | GBA | PC | Cause |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Suspend blocks 0 and 1 and game block 0: `PlaySt.time_saved` (+0x00-01) and `time_chapter_started` (+0x04) | 9 | | | Clock (P-12 cause 3): set from `GetGameClock()`, which counts the GBA's lag frames. |
| Game block 0 +0xCAE: `chapterStats[0].chapter_time`, low byte | 1 | `0xE7` | `0xE5` | Clock: the chapter time also comes from the game clock. |
| Block infos 0, 3 and 4 +0x07: `SaveBlockInfo._pad_07` | 3 | `00`, `03`, `03` | `3F` | Uninitialized stack byte (P-12 cause 2). |
| Block infos 3 and 4 +0x00: `magic32`, which `InvalidateSuspendSave` never sets | 8 | `0x080D0011` | `0x3F3F3F3F` | Uninitialized stack word. |
| Suspend blocks 0 and 1 +0x1F60, game block 0 +0xDA8: `GMapSaveInfo` byte 0x1C, bits 4-7 (bits 0-3 are `unk_1c_1..3` and match) | 3 | `7E`, `7E`, `4E` | `1E`, `9E`, `3E` | Padding bits of the stack-local `info` in `WriteWorldMapStuff` (`bmsave-gmap.c`). |
| Suspend blocks 0 and 1 +0x1F65, game block 0 +0xDAD and +0xDAF: `GMapSaveInfo` tail padding (+0x21..0x23) | 4 | `00`, `00`, `3F`, `0E` | `02`, `02`, `00`, `00` | The same stack-local `info`; agbcc's size 0x24 includes 3 padding bytes. |
| Suspend blocks 0 and 1 +0x19F3, game block 0 +0x84B: `supplyItems` byte 0xAF, the last one | 3 | `03`, `03`, `02` | `00` | `WriteSupplyItems` (`bmsave-lib.c:389`) packs 100 ids and 100 6-bit use counts into 175 bytes of a 176-byte stack buffer; byte 175 is never written. |
| Game block 0 +0x77E: `gmUnit` bytes 6-7 | 2 | `0A 08` | `00 00` | `WriteGMMonsterRnState` (`bmsave.c:479`) saves a `u32 buf[2]` from the stack, and `GetGmMonsterRnState` (`worldmap_path.c:2078`) copies only 3 halfwords into it. Bytes 2-5 come from the 4 bytes after `gGMData` (the copy starts at `unk_ce`, offset 0xCE of 0xD0), and they match. |
| Block infos 0, 3 and 4 +0x0C: `checksum32` | 12 | | | Covers the blocks above. |

- The global header (`0x00-0x63`) is equal. Block infos 0, 3 and 4 now store the GBA's offsets and sizes (game block 0 at `0x3FC4`). Unit slots, supply items 0-0xAE, BWL and chapter stats (other than the time), permanent flags, the bonus claim flags, `GMapSaveInfo` fields and `Dungeon` records are equal in every block.
- Not fixed, by the task's rule: the stack bytes are whatever the GBA's stack held, so they cannot come from the C source; the clock fields follow the lag question of PLAN.md 3.4. Each image is consistent in itself (each checksum covers the bytes stored next to it), so the leftovers should not stop a load. Loading the PC image in mGBA, and the mGBA image on the PC, has not been tried yet. (2026-10-20: mGBA-made saves load on the PC through the game's own code, the six chapter saves of section 3, "Round 3 integration"; a PC image in mGBA is `port-savefile`'s check, `pending/savefile.md`, not folded here. Done since: PC-written saves boot and resume in mGBA, and mGBA saves on the PC, H-9; the bytes still different are P-18's and the play time.)

**Gate 0 compare to tick 60,000 after D-18** (`build/gate0/compare_d18.txt`, the same key file, `--mask-oracle-limits`). The mGBA trace is byte-identical to the one before D-18 (`gba_l.jsonl`), and the PC trace gives the same supplementary result as the compare after D-13 and D-14: the mask hid 44 ticks (5,876-5,922); the first remaining difference is tick 5,876, `bg2affin` dx and dy (the same oracle limit, not masked); then `gOam` from 8,630 and `gPlaySt` from 15,184 (clock). `gPlaySt` differs only in `time_saved` (+0x00-01, from 33,642) and `time_chapter_started` (+0x04 from 15,184, +0x05 from 56,737); `gBmSt` only in `prevVCount`. **First difference after the clock and oracle-limit ones: tick 33,733, `gPaletteBuffer` palette 4** (then 33,733-35,649, 41,188-44,367, 48,388-51,747 and 56,949; BG0/BG1 tilemaps at 41,189-41,191; `bgoffset[2]` at 56,940-57,252). It is unchanged by D-18 and still not traced; the earlier notes (a cycling palette out of phase, right after GBA lag ticks) point to lag, not logic.

### Integration of `port-gate0`, `port-sdl` and `port-win` (2026-10-19, merge commits `35012930`, `61905c3d`, `31b6db11`; Gate 0 rule fix `059d0958`)

Merged one at a time into `port-d1a-spike` at `32ec6f81`, with the GBA build and the PC builds checked after each. Fragments `gate0-upgrade.md`, `sdl-window.md` and `windows-build.md` are kept in `port-plan/pending/merged/`. No game source file changed in any of the three merges.

- **`port-gate0` (`eace51a0`, Gate 0 upgrade, `port/test/` only).** Conflicts in 5 files of `port/test/`, all resolved there. The trace is now format 2: `gRNSeeds` (0x03000000), `gLCGRNValue` (0x03000008) and `sKeyStatusBuffer` (0x02024CC0) on both sides (file-static, so the compare checks their addresses in both symbol tables); on the GBA, libmgba debugger breakpoints at `OnMain` 0x080152F4, `VBlankIntrWait` 0x080D16DC and `OnVBlank` 0x080152A4, so both sides sample at the same program point and every tick carries `onmain_calls`, `vblank_waits` and `onvblank_calls`. Rules (`port/test/README.md`, "Rules and masks"): ticks matched by `OnMain` count with equal `vblank_waits`; `gGameClock` compared as the logic clock (per side, lag = `onvblank_calls - (vblank_waits - 1)`, and the GBA-PC offset must equal the lag difference exactly, with `SetGameTime` writes and the two `gPlaySt` clock copies checked); `gBmSt.prevVCount` left out (raster timing; nothing reads it). Oracle masks in `port/test/oracle_limits.json` (section 5). Kept from the spike: `frame15` from the renderer, `GATE0_SHOT_TICKS`, `GATE0_SRAM`, `FE8PC_BLEND=mgba8` in `pc-trace`, and `--mask-oracle-limits` as the older spelling of `--masks port/test/oracle_limits.json`. The PC hook now takes the three counters at the sample and writes `frame15` after the frame step, and flushes every complete tick. **Decided for the user at this merge: the clock replay is part of the standard Gate 0 result** (section 5, last bullet): new `pc-replay` stage, `gate0_compare.py --replay`, a LAG CHECK pass and a verdict line. New key file `port/test/inputs/scripted_a90_long.keys` (the scripted pattern to tick 61,001); `make -f port/test/Makefile scripted` now defaults to it and 60,000 ticks. On the upgrade branch itself (PC faulting at 15,800 then, P-11): tick alignment and the clock rule held on all 15,800 ticks, including the new-game `SetGameTime(0)` at 4,661 and the `time_chapter_started` copy at 15,184; OL-2 covered 44 ticks; the first remaining difference was tick 8,630, `gOam` entry 0 attr0 (the "press A" arrow), and with the clock replay RESULT matched on every tick. `gLCGRNValue` changed 254 times and matched on every tick; `gRNSeeds` did not change on either side before 15,800; `sKeyStatusBuffer` matched on every tick.
- **`port-sdl` (`575774a7`, platform layer and SDL3 window).** Clean merge. B-10, H-4, H-5. The window build, under WSLg, was checked on the branch: nearest-neighbour integer scaling (all 38,400 3x3 blocks of a 720x480 frame are one colour, and the frame scaled back equals the headless frame with 0 pixels different), 83 real key presses reached `REG_KEYINPUT` (81 also in the game's `newKeys` that frame; the other two were A presses during a screen change, not checked), and 3,584 frames in 60.006 s. Not tried: a gamepad (no passthrough in WSL), SDL's `wayland` driver, SDL on Windows. Audio is stubs (phase 3; real since the audio merge, H-6). The file calls (`Platform_LoadFile`, `Platform_SaveFileAtomic`, `Platform_UserDir`) exist but are unused until saves (phase 5).
- **`port-win` (`d8b68b03`, native Windows build).** Clean merge. B-11, including the compat fix this merge needed. On the branch: nothing occupies 0x02000000-0x0FFFFFFF before `main` (one free region of 0x0E000000 bytes); 40 of 40 native runs from PowerShell reserved every region; the fatal path with `FE8PC_DROP_BIOS_WRITES=0` reports the same fault as Linux (`AnimDelete`, `animedrv.c:170`, frame 8,349, exit 3). Open (from the fragment): the reservation is checked at run time, not guaranteed by the loader (the fallback would be the pokeemerald-expansion-native section layout, not implemented); `backtrace()` on Windows scans the stack, so later entries can be stale; the build is a WSL cross-build; log compares must normalise msvcrt's `%p` and the EWRAM/IWRAM lines; ld warns `section below image base` once per GBA RAM section (5, expected).

### B-12 details: every RAM variable at its GBA address (2026-10-19, commit `3ab0231b`; was P-14)

**What was wrong.** The old placement put each host object's RAM section at the GBA address of that object's section, so a variable was right only if GCC laid out the section as agbcc did. The new check (per object, statics included) finds **38** variables elsewhere on the old build; the old name-only check (`check_ram_addrs.py BINARY ELF`) showed 34 of them:
- **Emit order, 26 variables** in `banim-ekrbattleintro.o` (13, for example `gEkrBmLocation` host `0x0203E100`, GBA `0x0203E122`), `sio_core.o` (`gSioOutgoing`, `gSioIncoming`, `gSioStInstance`), `unitlistscreen.o` (6), `bmshop.o` (`sShopState`, `gShopItemTexts`) and `bmsave-lib.o` (`gBoolSramWorking`, `gUnused_0203E884`), as P-14 describes.
- **Alignment, 8 statics** in `scene.o` (`sTalkText` host `0x030000CC`, GBA `0x030000D0`, and two more), `bmmap.o` (2), `sio_core.o` (2) and `mu.o` (`sMuConfig`; P-14's grouping, but there the cause is order too: agbcc puts the two function-statics of `mu.c` first in `mu.o(.bss)`).
- **Missed by the old check, 4 more:** the two function-statics of `mu.c:1193-1194` (`sKeptPixelsWordMask` host `0x03001A2C`, GBA `0x030018F8`; `sClearedPixelWordMask` host `0x03001A28`, GBA `0x030018FC`; GCC names them `.0`/`.1`, agbcc `.189`/`.190`), and two variables the host kept outside GBA RAM: `gConvoyItemCount` (host `.data` at `0x102B6118`, GBA `0x02001F70`) and `gText_GoldBox` (host `.bss` at `0x102B6130`, GBA `0x03001DA0`). `gConvoyItemCount` is defined `EWRAM_OVERLAY(0)` in `convoymenu.c:22` but declared `EWRAM_DATA` in `bmcontainer.h:20`. agbcc takes the definition's section and GCC kept the header's, which the GBA map has no place for. So the `convoymenu.o(ewram_overlay_0)` size warning (GBA 0x1, host 0x0) was this variable, not "an unused 1-byte static that agbcc keeps", as P-14 said. `gText_GoldBox` (`bmshop.c:215`) has no section attribute; agbcc makes it a common symbol, which `sym_iwram.txt` places by name.
- The 5 `gen_host_link` size warnings came from the same causes. All are gone.

**How it is placed now.** See the B-12 row. 425 host-built RAM variables (430 since the audio merge added `m4a.o`'s 5) are placed one by one: 5 output sections on Linux; 6 on Windows, where COFF pads each section to 4 bytes, so `sSendCursor` (2 bytes, `0x030017F2`) gets a section of its own. Three files have two function-statics of one name (`bb.c` `lut`, `bksel.c` `offsetLut`, `mapanim_staffeffect.c` `lut`). They are initialised `.data` statics, in ROM on the GBA, so they stay in host memory. A pair like that in GBA RAM would be matched by size or reported. No GBA RAM static of a host-built file is missing on the host. No host alignment is larger than its GBA address allows.

**Effect.** Two ROM pointer words now keep their GBA value, which is now right, instead of being rewritten to the variable's old host address: `0x085A92E0` (to `gSioStInstance`) and `0x08A394D8` (to `sShopState`). The run log's `[reloc]` line changes from 12,045 rewritten and 274 already right to 12,043 and 276. `gConvoyItemCount` now shares `ewram_overlay_0` with the other overlay users, as on the GBA. RAM dumps by GBA address now match the GBA's layout. Nothing else changed in the checked runs.

- PC full build (`FULL=1`, from clean): 0 compiler warnings; `gen_host_link`: 425 RAM variables placed, 0 warnings, 0 unknown symbols stubbed; `check_ram_addrs`: 613 RAM symbols, 0 at another address. Name-only report: 423 at the GBA address, 0 at another (34 before); the other 2 are the `mu.c` function-statics, whose names differ by suffix. Core build (`make -f port/Makefile`): 25 of 25. Renderer unit tests: BG 22 passed, 0 failed; OBJ 18 passed, 0 failed. BIOS unit tests: 123 of 123 vectors, 4,676,017 checks, 0 failed. `layout-check`: 348 files, 702 types, 0 differ.
- `make -f port/Makefile.sdl` from clean: 0 compiler warnings; the same 425 and 613, check passed (the 44 `SDL_*` names stubbed in `autostubs.c` as before, removed for the SDL link).
- `make -f port/Makefile.mingw` from clean: 0 compiler warnings (ld's "section below image base", one per GBA RAM section, now 6); check on `fe8pc-linked.exe`: 613 RAM symbols, 0 at another address; `pe_gba strip` layout OK. `abi_check`: 5,048 layouts, 0 differ. Every one of the 613 RAM symbols is at the same address in the Linux `fe8pc` and in `fe8pc.exe`.
- Scripted run (`FE8PC_AUTOPRESS=0x001,90,4300`, 60,000 frames): exit 0; 55,744 `OnMain` calls; DISPCNT `0x3F00`, 65,565 non-zero VRAM bytes (FNV-1a `0x11347C3B`), 311 palette entries. Unchanged; the only log change is the `[reloc]` line.

So the palette-4 difference, and every other difference, does not come from where the variables sat.

### Audio merge details (2026-10-19, `port-audio` `c453b5e4` merged into `port-d1a-spike` `3ab0231b` as `0da9e831`)

Merged from `port-plan/pending/audio-core.md`, `sound-wiring.md` and `merge-audio.md` (kept in `pending/merged/`). In plain words: the sound driver's C half (`src/m4a.c`, B-13, D-19) and a C port of its ARM half (D-20) now run every frame as on the GBA, and a model of the timer, the FIFO DMAs, the four Game Boy channels and the DAC (H-6, H-7, H-8) turns their output into 224 stereo samples per frame at 13,379 Hz. The SDL window plays them.

- **IDs.** The fragments used audio-design.md's labels, and proposed B-10, B-11, H-4 and H-5, which are the SDL3 window, the Windows build, live input and frame pacing. Assigned: audio-design D-6 = D-19; B-6 = B-13; B-7 = B-14 (`SoundMainRAM`) plus B-12 (per-variable placement); H-4 = H-6; H-5 = H-7; `sound-wiring.md`'s SOUNDBIAS entry (its H-6) = H-8; OL-4 as proposed (section 5). The ARM-half port had no ID and is D-20. Committed comments carried the old labels (`src/m4a.c:393`, `port/Makefile:94-97`, `port/tools/gen_host_link.py:67`, `port/audio/fifo_pc.c:13`, `port/audio/psg.h:100`, `port/test/audio/m4a_difftest.py:121`). Branch `port-docids` (commit `9fe95f97`, comments only, GBA build OK) changes them; merged on `port-integrate` (section 3, "Round 2 integration").
- **Conflicts** (`port/` only). `port/tools/gen_host_link.py`: B-12's placement kept unchanged, `CODE_READ_AS_DATA` added (B-14), `--per-variable-objs` dropped (with both, each `m4a.o` variable would be planned twice, and its parser cannot read the MinGW build's COFF objects). `port/test/Makefile`: both sides' targets kept. Clashes git did not flag: `port/Makefile`'s `m4a.o`-only `-fdata-sections` and `PER_VARIABLE_OBJS` removed; `port/tools/layout_probe.py` given `m4a.c`'s `-Dasm(x)` flag (layout-check failed on `m4a.c:297`, `swi 0x2A`, before it); the `SND=map` note no longer says the PC stops at P-11.
- **Checks on the merge.** GBA build `OK`, SHA-1 `c25b145e37456171ada4b0d440bf88a19f4d509f`, twice. Core, FULL, SDL3 and MinGW builds: 0 compiler warnings. `check_ram_addrs`: 617 of 617 on Linux, SDL and Windows (B-12 row). `layout-check`: 349 files, 0 errors, 702 types, 0 differ. `abi_check`: 5,085 layouts, 0 differ. Gate 0 `boot300`: MATCH. Sound replay against mGBA: PASS on the title run (6,487 VBlanks) and the scripted route (16,287), the same numbers as on `port-audio`. PSG 547 checks, 0 failed; m4a difftest 289,353 cases, 0 failures; mutants 16 of 16 as expected; renderer BG 22 and OBJ 18 passed. Attract mode, 60,000 frames, Linux and Windows: unchanged (DISPCNT `0x3F00`, 63,604 non-zero VRAM bytes, FNV-1a `0xE8A769C8`, 264 palette entries); `[reloc]` 12,043 rewritten and 277 already right (B-12: 276; the one more is the ROM word `0x08A21304`, `gSoundInfo`, now at its GBA address). Scripted Gate 0 to tick 60,000 (run by the merge reviewer): RESULT, lag check, masks and clock identical to the B-12 baseline (25,558 ticks; 25,390 lag-only, 168 not), and `pc.raw` and `pc_replay.raw` byte-identical to the spike's. So the merge changes no traced game state in 60,000 ticks. That run logs no generated stub (its only `[stub]` line is H-2's `ClearOam` write at frame 0).
- **Closed.** `gPortM4aHostImageLo/Hi` on Windows (open in `audio-core.md` item 5 and `sound-wiring.md` item 4) is not needed: `port/Makefile.mingw` links with `--image-base=0x10000000 --disable-dynamicbase` (`pe_gba.py` checks it; the built `fe8pc.exe` has ImageBase `0x10000000`), as Linux does with `-Ttext-segment=0x10000000`. So host image addresses are at or above 0x02000000, and `chk_adr_r2`'s first test accepts them. The window stays empty on both. Also closed: the P-11 stop in the sound runs (D-13); `SND=map` now runs all 16,500 ticks.
- **Open.**
  1. Song 9 (the Prologue player phase) and P3.2 (loop point) are not reached in 16,500 ticks; they need a longer `SND=map` run.
  2. P3.4b and P3.4c, listening against mGBA recordings of SEs 0x60, 0x65, 0x6A, 0x6B and 0x13F: not done. `port-plan/frames/title_music.wav` (30 s, ROM-derived, local only) is there to listen to.
  3. DC offset: the output keeps the GBA's DC level (mean about -1,140 on the title theme), which the GBA's output capacitor removes. A ~10 Hz high-pass would be a product decision; the standing rule is no filters.
  4. **Closed** (`port-sndcheck` `1445f1cb`, tightened in the round 2 review; both on `port-integrate`). The old pass rule excused a differing VBlank as a "main-thread sound call" whenever track or player state differed, which was the difference itself, so only the PCM lanes were an independent check. Now the GBA capture records every top-level m4a call between two records (debugger breakpoints with returns on the 33 entry points), and `port/test/audio/m4a_replay.py` makes the same calls with the GBA's arguments in the GBA's order, then requires every compared byte equal. Only a call that was not clean (open across a record, or entered by another context while open) is not replayed, and it excuses only what it can touch on the VBlanks it spans. Round 2: the records taken before the boot `m4aSoundInit` returns are skipped, not excused; PASS needs every PCM lane equal; a call still open at the capture end fails unless it entered in the last window. Results and checks: section 3, "Round 2 integration". Compare 1 (`sndtool.py fifo`) is still information only (`|| true` in `port/test/Makefile`).
  5. Tried only on WSLg's PulseAudio ("RDP Sink"): no gamepad, no other audio driver, no real Windows audio device.
  6. `gClockTable` is the host copy in FULL builds (`m4a_tables.c` is compiled). A data byte without running status would read up to 128 bytes before it, which matches the GBA only for the ROM copy; mid2agb never writes such a byte.
  7. Doc nits: audio-design 1.3 and 1.4 name `psg_pc.c` and `PortPsg_Render(224)` (the code has `psg.c` and `PortPsg_Render(laneA, laneB, lr, n)`); `sound_capture.lua` and the README "Sound" section said keys are replayed as `gate0_capture.lua` does, but that one now samples at the `VBlankIntrWait` breakpoint (fixed by `port-sndcheck`: both now say how the two rules differ, and the capture counts the frames where they would: none on title or map); `make -f port/Makefile survey` has no `-Dasm(x)` rule for `m4a.c` (diagnostic only).
- **Credits.** `m4a_1_pc.c`, `psg.c` and `psg.h` derive from pokeemerald-expansion-native (https://github.com/uehlbran/pokeemerald-expansion-native, commit `bb3fd057`, MIT, scoped). The CREDITS draft should list it.

### Round 2 integration (2026-10-19/20 night, branch `port-integrate` at `9da96127`, worktree `~/fe8-integrate`)

Made from `port-d1a-spike` `0da9e831` and pushed with `git push local port-integrate` only, for the coordinator to move `port-d1a-spike` to (`~/fe8-port` was only read). Folded here from `pending/p22b.md`, `lagcheck-v2.md`, `sndcheck.md` and `check-all.md`, now in `pending/merged/`. `pending/nullprobe.md` and `port-nullprobe` are not merged (still under analysis; both came in with round 3, below).

- **Merges, in order** (each `git merge --no-ff`): `port-docids` `9fe95f97` (comments only) as `1c976953`; `port-p22b` `09702151` (P2.2b, section 5) as `c8435bc8`; `port-lagcheck` `ec4092a5` (lag check v2, trace format 3, section 5) as `5bbf8f26`; `port-sndcheck` `1445f1cb` (the sound replay replays the GBA's own m4a calls; audio "Open" item 4 above) as `c967b547`; `port-checkall` `ff00a585` (the bot routes and `make -f port/check-all.mk`) as `5c8adc91`. Conflicts only in the `port-sndcheck` merge, resolved by keeping both sides: `port/test/oracle/mgba_lua_run.c` (the lag check's `_irq`/`_resume` callbacks and the sound check's `:ret` return tracking; both struct fields kept, the return test runs after the IRQ test on each step), `port/test/README.md` (both runner bullets) and `port/test/audio/m4a_difftest.py` (the B-13 comment with the `m4a_source` argument). - **Approved changes** (DECISIONS.md, "night, round 2 review"), one commit each:

### Round 3 integration (2026-10-20, branch `port-integrate2`, worktree `~/fe8-integrate2`)

Made from `port-d1a-spike` `9da96127` and pushed with `git push local port-integrate2` only: head `f3a9f5be`, then this fold's two comment-only commits, `227309c8` and `0f440ff0`. Not merged into `port-d1a-spike` yet, which has moved on to round 4 (`3ab38885`); merged with round 4 and round 5 on `port-integrate4` (`7c2d2352`, "Round 5 and 6 integration" below). Folded here from `pending/nullprobe.md`, `nullfix-1.md`, `groupF-design.md`, `nullfix-groupF.md`, `site-H.md`, `site-I-N26.md`, `site-J.md`, `chapsave.md` and `integrate2.md`, now in `pending/merged/`. Groups G and L had no fragment; their rows (D-35, D-38) come from the analyses and reviews in `wf-fe8/round3_results.json`.

In plain words: the PC now plays the whole Chapter 5 test route (135,505 ticks) with no crash and no test switch; before round 3 it stopped at tick 3,714. The game logic matches the GBA on every tick of the Prologue, Chapter 1 and Chapter 2 routes, and on the longer routes on every tick but 198 at the Chapter 4 opening.

- **Commits, in order.** `9b5c8859` merges `port-nullfix` `ecd68766`, with `port-nullprobe` `1cd29ba5` (the probe), `b6a504d5` (the shared helper), `7daa59e7` D-21, `f73f4c72` D-22, `fdaf7cc7` D-23 and D-24, `763ab885` D-25, `4f8fd1f3` (the GBA bus helper) and `ecd68766` D-27. `e6bdee53` merges `port-chapsave` `4da9e2eb`. The review cleanups: `d7c7a22d` (one copy of the latch rule; `PORT_NULL_READ`'s compile-time check), `087ef561` (`port/Makefile` prerequisite on `port_gbabus.h`), `8953fb21` (D-5 by the rule, `0x5E`), `f44b663d` (the chapter-save README caveat and the 3,244 cause), `48295519` (the OL-5 mask, section 5). `f3a9f5be`: `port/tools/fixptr_unresolved.txt` (12,319 relocations after D-26). `227309c8`: the final IDs in the code comments, `BmMapFill` and the open latch choice in D-24's comment, the current loop lines in the B-15 comment, and OL-3's first hit in the README masks table. `0f440ff0`: the fragment paths in `port/hw/gbabus.c` and in OL-5's `reason` point to `pending/merged/`.
- **Conflicts.** `port-nullfix`: `port/test/Makefile` and `port/test/README.md` only (from `1cd29ba5`), both sides kept. Adapted to main: `nullprobe.py --lag-replay` passes `GATE0_LAG_FROM` (main's lag replay, a format-3 GBA trace) instead of the round-1 prototype's `GATE0_CLOCK_FROM`/`GATE0_LAG_REPLAY`, and runs of 60,000 ticks or more take `check_all.take_lock(COMPARE_LOCK)` (main has no `~/.fe8-heavy.lock` any more). `port-chapsave`: the `port/test/Makefile` header comment only.

**The NULL probe** (`port-nullprobe`, from `pending/merged/nullprobe.md`; `port/hw/nullprobe.c`, `port/tools/nullprobe.py`, `make -f port/test/Makefile nullprobe`, README "NULL probe"). A diagnostic mode: with `FE8PC_NULLPROBE=1` a fault at a low address (below `0x02000000`, or `0xFFFF0000` and up) no longer ends the run. The probe decodes the x86 instruction, moves the pointer register into a shadow area at `0x60000000` whose pages hold the latch word, single-steps the instruction (so it gets the latch bytes of its own address and width), then moves the register back; a load from an absolute address is emulated and a store there skipped, and writes land in the shadow and are lost, as on the GBA. The latch follows the rule; undecided cases (inside an IRQ handler, `gPortIrqDepth` in `port/hw/frame.c`; another IRQ source on; an address at `0x4000` and up) get post-SWI and are marked. GBA memory mirrors the host does not map reach their real address, and unused high space reads the shadow. Each site (instruction and caller, addresses, R/W, first and last Gate 0 tick, hits, the rule of each hit, the call chain) goes to `FE8PC_NULLPROBE_OUT`. `nullprobe.py` plays the routes, compares game state with a GBA trace (`--gba-trace`: RNG, `gPlaySt` without its clock copies, the key buffer without its frame counter) and merges and symbolises the sites (`report`). With the variable unset every build behaves as before (checked on boot300, route ch0 and the 60,000-tick scripted keys). The Windows build has a stub only.

The round-2 analysis run (the round-1 lag-replay prototype plus B-15's flag, both scratch then) ran every route to its end, with game state equal to the GBA's on every tick but 11 single lag ticks (29,097, 29,106, 67,614, 67,757, 75,851, 75,940, 76,807, 76,814, 76,829, 76,838, 109,594), and found **45 sites in 12 groups**, each first hit on a tick where game state equalled the GBA's:

| A | N-1, N-22, N-27: `AnimDelete` (`animedrv.c:170`, `:175`) writes `+0x34` through a NULL `pNext` | 2,111 | retail write, ignored by the GBA | H-1, exact (checked for N-22 and N-27 in the group J analysis: same counts and first and last ticks as the GBA; one of N-1's 279 stores lands one tick late on the PC, 109,542 against 109,541, near the lag tick 109,594, not traced) |
| B | N-2 `MoveActiveUnit` | 3,714 | a dead AI unit's `pCharacterData` | D-21 |
| C | N-3 `SetBattleMuPaletteByIndex` | 11,992 | a one-actor map animation | D-22 |
| D | N-4 `AiGetFriendZoneCombatScoreComponent` | 12,167 | an off-map row, byte unused | D-23 |
| E | N-5 `Event2E_CheckAt` | 13,534 | an AREA event after the active enemy died | D-25 |
| F | N-6 to N-21 `DisplayBmTile` | 20,271 | the 2x2 map drawn off the map | D-27 |
| G | N-23 `IsGmAutoMuActiveFor` | 37,699 | read before the NULL test | D-35 |
| H | N-24, N-25 `PromoHandler_SetupAndStartUI` | 67,669 | `trainees[3..6]` | D-36 (and B-15) |

**Chapter saves for Gate 0** (`port-chapsave` `4da9e2eb`, from `pending/merged/chapsave.md`; `port/test/` plus one line of `port/tools/check_all.py`, so no new divergence). `GATE0_SRAM_IN=PATH` (a 32 KiB `.sav`, or a 64 KiB `GATE0_SRAM` dump of which the first 32 KiB are used, D-18) boots both sides from power-on with that SRAM: mGBA gets it as its save before reset (`port/test/oracle/mgba_lua_run.c`, `core->loadSave`; `gate0_capture.lua` checks the SRAM it reads), and the PC hook (`port/test/pc/gate0_hook.c`) writes it to `0x0E000000` at the boot-time `UpdateKeyStatus` (`main.c:53`), before `SramInit` (`main.c:55`). The game's own code loads the save on both sides; tick 0, the trace format and every Gate 0 rule stay. Trace headers carry `sram_in` and `sram_fnv`, and `gate0_compare.py` exits 2 when the sides booted from different SRAM. `check_all.py` drops the variable, so check-all rows start from a blank save. New targets: `sram-capture` (`port/test/oracle/sram_capture.lua`: a GBA run's SRAM at `tick:T`, `save:C` or `suspend:C`), `sram-info` (`port/test/tools/sram_info.py`), `chapsave`, `chapsave-ch2`, `chapsave-frelia`; key files `resume.keys`, `chapsave_ch2.keys`, `chapsave_frelia.keys`; `port/test/saves/` is git-ignored (saves are game data, never committed). Saves made once from the routes: `ch1_seize` (suspend, route tick 19,883), `ch2_start` (20,342), `ch3_start` (37,495), `ch4_start` (66,705), `ch5_start` (106,919), `ch5x_start` (134,765). A save start reaches the Castle Frelia change in about 35 s, against 20,271 route ticks and a lag replay from power-on. **Caveat** (README, `f44b663d`): a save start does not have the route's RAM history (stale EWRAM, the proc pool order, stack bytes), so sites that read stale or uninitialised memory (D-27's off-map reads, D-36's stack words) can read other values than on the route; a chapter-save verdict does not replace a route run there.

Everything through the Castle Frelia scenes, the save menu and the world map is lag-only; OL-5 covered 4,309-4,314. SRAM at the end differs in 32 bytes. The PC side of that check is the plain lag-free run, so the game save's time fields (`0x3FC4`, `0x3FC8`), the block checksums and P-12's pad bytes follow from the lag offset, and the suspend blocks are written after 6,646. The three bytes before the pidStats copies (`0x1AC7`, `0x3A3F`, `0x480F`: GBA `03`/`03`/`02`, PC `00`) are suspend blocks 0 and 1 +0x19F3 and game block 0 +0x84B: the never-written last `supplyItems` byte of P-12 cause 2 (D-18 details, the same values). Identified by offset, not re-traced.
- `chapsave-ch2` (`ch2_start.sav`, 7,000 ticks): all 7,000 ticks (before: stop at 5,767 on N-3). `DIFFERENT`: first difference 1,800 (lag-only), first not lag-only 2,100 (P-17), then 3,244-3,245, where the lag replay leaves the sequence: `ChapterIntro_SetSkipTarget(0)` returns r0 = 0 on the GBA, so the proc script yields, while the PC runs on (host-ABI, as at route tick 1,591; the fragment's guess of HBlank/VCount handler events was wrong, chapsave review). With `port-hostabi` applied in scratch the event counts match on every tick, and one more not-lag-only tick appears at 5,160 (a `frame15` tick; logic, palette and OAM equal; not investigated). 315 lag-only, 3,707 not, 0 replay-only. SRAM at the end differs in 19 bytes (after 3,244; not traced). `ch5x_start` with `resume.keys` shows the same host-ABI signature at tick 2,249.

In the real captures the only such calls, apart from the skipped boot `m4aSoundInit`, are three `m4aSongNumStart(0x7A)` on the map run, one record each.

The review found a non-unit name at offset 0 too. Fix: accept `0` in `NAME_RE` for every name it parses (one line).

Suggested: fail when the boot call spans more than 2 records, or require 14 boot records skipped. With `MAX_OPEN_RECORDS = 1`, a capture with a second `m4aSoundInit` (after a soft reset; a real one spans 2 records) would fail; none exists.

  - The scan-tool and `GATE0_REJECT` gaps of D-29's check (B-17), and AI-X1 (P-23).

In plain words: the PC now plays the whole story the bots know, from power-on to the start of Eirika's Chapter 14, exactly like the GBA. That is 15 bot routes, about 805,000 ticks (3.7 hours of play), run on the plain PC with no test crutch, and 14 of them match the GBA on every recorded value of every tick from start to end. The fifteenth, Chapter 8, matches until its last 362 ticks, where the chapter-end ranking gets junk experience (G5, whose fix is held). Two "same buttons, different result" bugs are gone (the healing-staff help line showed "/", the critical-hit shake swapped left/right with up/down), and so are a one-frame dark flash and a few wrong sprite pixels. The same-buttons TAS run (with the scratch Suspend build) now plays all 249,800 ticks on the plain PC with every picture equal or carrying the exact "drawn while the screen was being drawn" label. The GBA ROM is still byte-identical to retail.

### Round 9 fix passes and integration 9 (2026-10-21, branch `port-integrate9`, worktree `~/fe8-integrate9`)

Round 9's three held groups each had a fix pass and a re-review, and all were approved (`wf-fe8/fixpass9_results.json`): `fp9-harness` (H-col-2, H-col-1, A2, with the head-check hole closed), `fp9-uiarena` (Suspend and the arenas, the claims narrowed, the Chapter 10 limit recorded) and `fp9-ch8exp` (G5, the dead second hunk dropped), plus `fp9-p13` (P-13's soft reset, with the held-buttons bug its refuter found fixed). `port-integrate9` = `port-integrate8` + main `eb542d4d` (`port-win9`) + those four patches, each its own commit + `port-snd10` (`1421977e`, sound against mGBA on any route), made with `git -C ~/fe8-port worktree add ~/fe8-integrate9 -b port-integrate9 port-integrate8` and pushed with `git push local port-integrate9` only. Main was not moved (the coordinator does that). Folded from `pending/folded/r9-harness.md`, `r9-uiarena.md`, `r9-ch8exp.md`, `p13.md`, `snd10.md`, `snd10-findings.md`, `snd10-monkey.md`, `snd-story.md` and the `port-win9` half of `win9.md` (`pending/folded/win9.md`; the `port-winpolish` half stays in `pending/win9.md`, that branch being still at work). Scripts: `wf-fe8/i9_*`; small outputs in `~/gate0-scratch/i9/`.

In plain words: the map menu's Suspend and the arenas now work on the PC exactly as on the GBA, Chapter 8's ending no longer puts junk experience into the rankings and the save, and a soft reset (A+B+Start+Select) behaves as on the GBA, held buttons included. With these, all 15 story routes the bots know (power-on to the start of Eirika's Chapter 14, about 805,000 ticks) match the GBA on every recorded value of every tick, Chapter 8 now included, and the same-buttons TAS run plays all 249,800 ticks on this branch itself, every picture equal or carrying the exact "drawn while the screen was being drawn" label. The sound was compared with the GBA's record by record on every story route and every monkey file: no sound difference anywhere; three test-tool gaps remain (below). The Windows window build plays a map-menu Suspend and Resume Chapter exactly as the Linux build does, and the 2-frame Linux/Windows picture difference the win9 review found is gone (D-53). The GBA ROM is still byte-identical to retail.

So all 15 story routes now match the GBA from start to end on the plain PC (805,532 ticks of story, the power-on routes being prefixes of each other), and the sound of every one of them, compared record by record, has no difference that is not one of the snd rows' two excused calls.

**The fix-pass reviewers' notes, where they went.** harness: the RAM-script opcode path has never run on real data (no head's script is in RAM), noted in "Lag check v2"; the DIVERGENCES wording asked for is the one used there; inside-handler lag VBlanks past a command's first instruction stay "before the next event" (the model). uiarena: OL-7's hardware sentence reworded as asked (a DMA's last word probably lingers briefly on hardware too); "7 variants" was 8 (the row says 8); D-57's comment now says "in clear weather"; its own check-all was not seen finishing (this branch's check-all covers it). ch8exp: the rank floor hides junk stores of 0x46 or less into `ranks[0..7]`, so the guard still matters for stores outside the array and above 0x46 (D-58's row); `port/Makefile` tracks no header dependencies for game objects (this branch's builds were fresh); identify a binary by its code sections, not its SHA-1. p13: KEYCNT, RCNT and JOYSTAT left as they are (D-59); a soft-reset gate row is a decision (below); P-33 is the cold-boot cousin.

**Decisions for the user (this integration):** (0) main moved to `79e83239` (`port-winpolish`, a fast-forward at 18:18) while this branch was being checked; `port-integrate9` is based on `eb542d4d`. A trial `git merge-tree` of main into it is clean (`port/check-all.mk`, `port/hw/main_pc.c` and `port/win32/README.md` merge on their own); merge it and re-run check-all's default rows before main moves to this branch. (1) a soft-reset check: the refuter's quick plain-build row (`FE8PC_AUTOPRESS=0x00F,1000,250,300 FE8PC_FRAMES=2000` must print exactly 3 `[port] soft reset` lines, the GBA's count) or a Gate 0 option that writes the keys once per tick as the GBA runner does (D-59's scratch hook); either is harness work. (2) The three snd harness gaps: H-story-1's one-line guard (clear `sSndMainOpen` before `LagReplaySplitExit`; with it ch6 and ch8 pass whole here), H-story-2 (mark such a tick in the capture and index the VBlank its wait returned on, rather than loosen check 3), H-sndmk-1 (skip the start-save rule for `start power-on`); then snd-route rows under `CHECK_LONG=1` (about 1.5 min per route for ch5x-ch7, 4-6 min for ch5 and e9-e13). (3) Commit a map-menu Suspend and Resume route (this integration's scratch `k7.keys`, a hand-made file from `route_ch6.keys`, not TAS-derived) and the arena routes (r9-uiarena's `route_arena_ch5.keys`, fp9's `route_arena_e10_yield.keys`) as CHECK_LONG rows, with the arena options for `route_bot.lua`. (4) Let the menu monkey pick Suspend and Arena now (new shard files only). (5) Make the open-bus tracer (`fp9_uiarena_mkob.py`) a runner option. (6) OL-7 as recorded (listed, no mask). (7) Earlier rounds' open decisions stand (integrate8's list: the accept list's re-key, `RasterEndFrame`'s check point, P-27, P-28, `pputest`, D-52's 14 benign sites).

**Not run:** a reviewer of `port-integrate9` itself (four re-approved patches, each applied as reviewed, with ID renames and one comment note; two merges, whose conflicts are described above); a visible window, sound through speakers or a real keyboard and gamepad on either host; the Ephraim 11 arena (no save), rain or snow at an arena, `ArenaResume`, the arena's loss and draw results; a soft reset on the Windows build (the same `bios.c`); a suspend made on the PC resumed in mGBA (P5.4); Eirika 14 onward and Ephraim's route (coverage round 10, beside this integration).

### Round 10 and integration 11 (2026-10-22/23, branch `port-integrate11`, worktree `~/fe8-integrate11`)

Coverage round 10 (`port-cover10`, on `port-integrate10` `fd46f35e` with the sweep builds) played both lords' routes to the end and found ten problem groups, C10-1 to C10-10 (`pending/c10-findings.md`, not folded). Round 10 gave each its own group, an analyst and implementer and an adversarial refuter (one rework when held): A, `wf_84b47e6e-5a3` (stubs, traps, tableend, slots, summon, demonlight, gone, and a CHECK_LONG census of all 39 routes on the base, `pending/folded/r10-checklong.md`); B, `wf_4e3e23db-e0b` (ending, frames, paths, lucky, sound). All twelve were approved. Integration 11 (`wf_d59e1923-523`) merged them on `port-integrate11` (from `port-integrate10`, `git -C ~/fe8-port worktree add`), then ran five small groups on that head, each with its refuter: petrify (the petrify palette crash that tableend found, with OL-7's mask), nullunit2 (audit10's N2 and the paths review's twin), faces (Ephraim's Chapter 10 opening, N-R10-STUBS-1, and the talk codes on an empty slot), wmface (coverage 11's F-P-1, every world-map skirmish start) and builds (the user's two choices of 2026-10-22). All five were approved; this step merged them, applied the reviewers' open choices, ran every gate on the final tree and folded the notes. Main was not moved (the coordinator does that after the review). Pushed with `git push local port-integrate11` only. Folded from `pending/folded/r10-*.md` (18 notes), `pending/folded/audit10-*.md` (the three static audits and their review) and `pending/folded/i11-merge.md` (the merge step). Scripts: `wf-fe8\i11m_*` (merge step), `wf-fe8\i11g_*` (this step); outputs `~/gate0-scratch/i11-merge/`, `~/gate0-scratch/i11-gates/`.

In plain words: every problem the round-10 bots found is fixed on this branch, the GBA ROM is still byte-identical to retail, and every test passes. All 39 story routes the bots know (both lords, power-on to Eirika's ending and her post-game, and Ephraim to the end of Chapter 20) now play on the PC exactly as on the GBA, every recorded value of every tick, with no crash: round 10's census had 14 of them stopping or differing. Beyond the gates, coverage round 11's 30 new route files (`port-cover11`, not on this branch) were played on this branch's binary too: Eirika's whole story with her real army to the end credits, Ephraim's final chapter part 1 with his real army and part 2, his ending and post-game from a boosted save, all 8 floors of the Tower of Valni, the Lagdou Ruins floors 2 to 9, four world-map skirmishes and five game features match the GBA, but two: one picture in Ephraim's final part 1 on a GBA slow-down tick (every value equal), and Ruins floor 9's flickering map colours one step out of order on 232 ticks (coverage 11's N-C11-1, a limit of the test tool). The sound is equal to the GBA's, record by record, on the story routes CHECK_LONG checks (the Prologue to Eirika's Chapter 13) and on her Chapter 15 (which needed one rule change in the test tool, H-17). The whole 249,800-tick same-buttons TAS run matches, every picture equal or carrying the exact "drawn while the screen was being drawn" label, as do all 31 monkey files. For the player: both lords' stories play from the start to the end credits; the Extras menu has no Link Arena (it needs a cable, PB-1); and the builds the player runs no longer have the Linux-only compiler checks (B-21).

**Choices made in integration 11** (the user said "I trust your judgement" for the open technical choices; the reviewers' recommended options unless the evidence said otherwise):
