### Coverage 11 and 12, the test cheats, fix round 12 and integration 12 (2026-10-23/24, branch `port-integrate12`, worktree `~/fe8-integrate12`)

Coverage round 11 (`port-cover11` `42db16cc`: three bot chains, 30 new route files, `pending/folded/c11-*.md`) and coverage round 12 (`port-cover12` `bcf72f69`: eleven shards, 217 new key files, the CHECK_LONG rows for both rounds' route files and the KNOWN status, `pending/folded/c12-*.md`) played what integration 11 had left: both lords' stories with their real armies to the credits, every story scene watched at a human's pace, the menu monkey on every late chapter and on the post-game, the Tower and the Ruins with Eirika's real army, every world-map skirmish node a story save offers, the side features, sound on every route file, the bug-detector builds on the late game. The test cheats (`port-cheat12` `d15fba53`, H-24) let the bot win Lagdou Ruins floor 10 (`route_br10`) and use every item of the game once (the item gallery, `route_gallery`). Fix round 12 (`port-r12-fixes` `42deb135`, approved; `pending/folded/r12-fixes.md`) fixed what those found in the game: the Warp crash (D-84), Windows' world-map monsters from Chapter 20 on (D-85), the event registry overflow (D-86), and one harness trap (H-21). Integration 12 merged the three branches and the fixes on `port-integrate12` (from main `9ef060ea`, `git -C ~/fe8-port worktree add`), applied the fix reviewer's notes, ran every gate on the final tree and folded the notes. Main was not moved (the coordinator does that). Pushed with `git push local port-integrate12` only. Folded from `pending/folded/c11-e.md`, `c11-i.md`, `c11-p.md`, `c11-findings.md`, the fourteen `c12-*.md`, `cheat12.md`, `cheat12-rework.md`, `cheat13.md`, `rv-cheat12.md`, `rv2-cheat12.md` and `r12-fixes.md`. Scripts `wf-fe8\i12_*`, `wf-fe8\i12g_*`; outputs `~/gate0-scratch/i12-gates/`.

In plain words: everything the last two coverage rounds and the cheat work made is now on one branch with the four fixes, the GBA ROM is still byte-identical to retail, and the tests pass and the tests pass but one long row: every story route of both lords from power-on or its chapter save to the credits, both lords' ends with their real armies, the post-game's Tower and Ruins (floor 10 with a test cheat), every skirmish node and the side features match the GBA on every recorded value of every tick (three of them by a recorded limit of the test tool, KNOWN). The one failing row is the item gallery (a test-cheat run): it now plays to its end without the Warp crash and differs only where P-41 and P-42 already said (a few map-cursor moments and picture frames; no game value). For the player: using the Warp staff no longer crashes the PC; on Windows the world map's monsters from Chapter 20 on are the GBA's again (before, every Windows player past Chapter 19 met other monsters and other skirmishes); a rare overflow of the game's "events that just started" list in Lagdou Ruins 7 and 8 now writes exactly what the GBA writes. Both lords' stories, with their real armies, play from power-on to the end credits like the GBA, and so do every story scene watched slowly, the post-game's Tower with the real army, every skirmish node, the side features and Lagdou Ruins floor 10 (with a test cheat).

**Choices made in integration 12:**
1. The fix reviewer's notes: D-84's wording (the code comment, `pending/folded/r12-fixes.md` row 27 and the D-84 row): the warped unit has no MU of its own, the GBA reads the latch in both free slots, `GetUnitMu` returns NULL and `EndMu(NULL)` does nothing; the impact is a fault whenever a free slot was reached. D-86's limit (a) gains the flag-14 case (the D-86 row and the note). H-21's never-firing guard stays (the H-21 row). N-C12-3 and the Windows world map stay unrun (checked from the ROM and the binaries only; the D-85 row).
2. The cheat-assisted files are CHECK_LONG rows (`ba5ac81b`; c12-br10's "no row" came from coverage 11's decision 2, which coverage 12 reversed for the post-game floors), titled as such; `route_gallery`'s row is the file's first full run on the plain PC since D-84: it FAILs on P-41's and P-42's sites only and stays a row as it is (no KNOWN entry: P-41 and P-42 are not traced, so they are not called a limit of the test tool; decision 3 below).
3. Coverage 12's decision 4: the KNOWN status stays the way a row records a measured limit of the test tool (H-23); no new entry was added here (every entry is measured; P-41's cursor moments are not traced, so they are not called a limit).

### Test cheats (harness only, test coverage only; H-24)

Folded from `pending/folded/cheat12.md`, `cheat12-rework.md`, `cheat13.md` (the author, `port-cheat12`) and `rv-cheat12.md`, `rv2-cheat12.md` (the adversarial reviews: the first held one hole, the cheat could be switched on in Eirika's Chapter 20 and final chapter; the rework closed it; the second approved). The user agreed on 2026-10-22 to a test-only "unlimited durability" cheat so the bot could reach what fair play cannot (Lagdou Ruins floor 10), and asked for "funds" and a convoy with no limit on 2026-10-23. The port itself has none of it: nothing in `src/`, `include/` or the played builds changed, and the five played and test binaries carry 0 cheat symbols and 0 cheat strings (`cheat12.md`).

In plain words: three switches for the robot's test runs only. **durability**: every item's uses go back to full; **funds**: the party's gold stays at 999,999 (the game's own cap); **convoy**: the convoy stays full and "rolls" (every empty slot gets the next of the game's 177 real items, and the list goes round). Both the GBA side (mGBA's script) and the PC side (the Gate 0 hook) apply them at the same moment of every tick, so the two still play exactly alike and every compare stays exact. A route made with a cheat says so in its own header, and the tools turn the same cheat on from that header by themselves.

- **How:** `GATE0_CHEAT` (and the bot's `ROUTE_CHEAT`) take `durability`, `funds`, `convoy` or several joined with `+` (written in that order). At each tick sample, after the refusal, convoy, durability then funds run (`C.refill()`, `CheatRefill`); the convoy uses the game's own `MakeNewItem`, its list read from `gItemData` (ids 0x01-0xCD whose name is not the placeholder `MSG_403`: 177; 28 left out). The undo words, the tick line's fields `cheat_items`, `cheat_refills`, `cheat_funds`, `cheat_convoy` and the convoy cursor are traced and compared (`gate0_compare.py`'s `cheat_fields`); a header naming any other cheat stops the compare.
- **Refusals:** chapter indices 0x14-0x16 (Eirika's Chapter 20 and both parts of the final chapter) are refused at the first tick by both sides and by every tool (the bot with or without `ROUTE_E7` and its search, `chain-route`, a resumed save state, walking in from Chapter 19: GBA and PC stop at the same tick, 154,137), but not with `PLAY_FLAG_COMPLETE` (the post-game from the clear saves is allowed, the user's choice). `snd-gba`, `monkey`, `monkey-check`, `movie-check`, `frame-trace` and the scripts `menu_monkey.lua`, `movie_check.lua`, `movie_presses.lua` refuse a cheat (`NO_CHEAT`); check-all stops when `GATE0_CHEAT` or `ROUTE_CHEAT` is in its environment, and a cheat-assisted route's row passes its own (`KEYS_CHEAT` from the key file's `# Generated` line, `CHEAT_ARG`; titled "cheat-assisted (GATE0_CHEAT=..., test coverage only)", `CHEAT_TITLE`). The hook stops (exit 4) without `GATE0_OUT` and on a bad value.
- **Labels:** the bot's `# Generated` line gets `, ROUTE_CHEAT=<cheats>` and a `# Cheat-assisted (test coverage only, not fair play): ...` line; a save made by `chain-save` from a cheat-assisted route carries the mark in its `.sav.json` through any number of later saves; a save state's prologue line names the run's cheats and a resume refuses one made without them.
- **Proof (the author and the reviewers):** switched off, the 60,000-tick scripted test gives byte-identical traces, raw dumps and screenshots against the tree before the cheat, route files regenerate byte for byte, and `make -n` of check-all `CHECK_LONG=1` is unchanged (145 lines). Switched on, GBA and PC equal on every tick: funds on `route_e17` 0-3,499 (the shop), convoy on `route_e15` 0-11,999 (three take-outs refilled), all three together, a convoy save state resumed (5,999-7,999), durability on Chapter 19 and Ephraim's final chapter (5,000 ticks each), the post-game from `b_clear` and `e_clear`. Default check-all PASS 23 of 23 at `8fbf5951`; at `2170d9f2` 22 of 23 (win-sdl-60000 never started: WSL's link to Windows timed out; the same binary passed run from Windows).
- **The cheat-assisted files** (README-routes.md, "Cheat-assisted routes"): `route_br10.keys` (Lagdou Ruins floor 10, index 0x37, boosted start `br10r_start`, `GATE0_CHEAT=durability`, 54,996 ticks; the bot's tactics R10, `L.R10`, only in 0x37; won; PC check MATCH on the cheat-assisted run, `pending/folded/c12-br10.md`) and `route_gallery.keys` (the item gallery: from `gal_start`, Ephraim's final chapter part 1 edited by `gallery/gal_prep.edit`, `GATE0_CHEAT=durability+funds+convoy`, every item of the convoy cheat's list taken from the supply once and used once, 161 of the 177, the 16 others not tried by rule; 159,706 ticks; `pending/folded/c12-gallery.md`: it found D-84, H-25, P-41, P-42). Both are CHECK_LONG rows since integration 12 (`ba5ac81b`): `route-br10-lag` MATCH; `route-gallery-lag` FAIL on P-41's and P-42's sites only (section 3, integration 12).
- **Credit:** durability's THANK-YOU row (row 30) stays; funds and the rolling convoy are the user's own requests (no new row).

Chain: `UnitKakudaiPrepareAnimScript` (`banim-ekrbattleintro.c:516`, GBA `0x08056974`) calls `UpdateBanimFrame`, which runs `CpuFastCopy(gBanimTriAtkPalettes[0], PAL_OBJ(0x8), 0x20)`. `gBanimTriAtkPalettes[POS_L]` (`0x0203E19C`) was set by `FilterBattleAnimCharacterPalette` (`banim-ekrcmd.c:86`, called from `banim-ekrbattleintro.c:1053`), which returns NULL for every animation that is not a ballista. So the source is NULL in almost every battle. Line 263 does the same with `gBanimTriAtkPalettes[1]` for the right side. | `CpuFastCopy` is the BIOS call `CpuFastSet` (SWI 0x0C). Per GBATEK, the GBA BIOS `CpuSet` and `CpuFastSet` do nothing when the source start or end address is in the BIOS area. So no copy happens: OBJ palette 8 in `gPaletteBuffer` keeps its old contents, and nothing else changes. | A port-layer fix, no shared-file edit: `CpuSet` and `CpuFastSet` in `port/bios/bios.c` return without copying when the source start or end is below `0x4000`, and log the site once. Do not copy the BIOS test literally (address bits 25-27 all zero): host code and host data sit at `0x10000000` and up, where those bits are also zero, so a literal copy would reject real copies. Done 2026-10-19 as D-8, after checking the GBA behaviour in GBATEK and in mGBA's HLE BIOS. The frame-14,140 fault is gone. |

Both are negative once sign-extended, so `blt` skips the early return, and the function goes on to store the quadrant and draw the terrain window. The read changes nothing. | A guarded fix in `src/player_interface.c` (`#ifdef PC_PORT`): `if (ui1Proc != NULL && piProc != NULL)`. It is exact for both open-bus values. Do not use the decomp's `BUGFIX` path: it also runs the test when `ui1Proc` is NULL and `piProc` is not, which the GBA never does, so it can return early where the GBA does not. | P-12 (**cause 1 fixed**, now D-18; causes 2 and 3 stay as logged leftovers) | The PC's SRAM image is not the GBA's. Found 2026-10-19 by comparing the whole 64 KiB SRAM at tick 60,000 of the Gate 0 run (the same key file on both sides; scratch probes, not committed): 22,982 bytes differ. The global header (`0x00-0x63`) is equal. Three causes: **(1) layout.** The save structs have GCC sizes, not agbcc sizes: `struct Dungeon` is 16 bytes on the PC and 12 on the GBA (the GBA's DWARF), and `struct GMapSaveInfo` is 34 on the PC and 36 on the GBA. So `struct SuspendSaveBlock` is `0x1F7C` (GBA `0x1F78`) and `struct GameSaveBlock` is `0xDD0` (GBA `0xDC8`), every block after the first suspend block starts at another offset (game block 0: PC `0x3FCC`, GBA `0x3FC4`), and block info 0 stores those offsets and sizes. `Dungeon` has `u32` bitfields that cross a 32-bit boundary (`turnCount:9` at bit 24); agbcc packs them across it, and GCC on i386 probably starts a new unit there (sizes checked, member offsets not yet). **(2) uninitialized stack bytes** written to SRAM: the pad byte of `SaveBlockInfo` (block info 0, `+0x07`: GBA `0x00`, PC `0x10` or `0xF7`) and the whole `magic32` that `InvalidateSuspendSave` leaves unset (block infos 3 and 4: GBA `0x080D0011`, a stale ROM address, PC `0xF7CEBC67`), with the checksums that cover them. These even differ between two PC binaries with the same input (the scripted run and the Gate 0 test binary differ in 11 SRAM bytes). **(3) clock fields:** `time_saved` and `time_chapter_started` in the saved `PlaySt` come from `GetGameClock()`, which counts the GBA's lag frames. Inside game block 0, at each side's own offset, the unit data match: slots 0-1 equal, slots 2-50 zero on both sides. | The GBA writes its own struct layout, so a GBA save and a PC save are not interchangeable, and Gate 0 cannot compare SRAM byte for byte. No fault: the PC reads back what it wrote. Not visible in play yet. | Needs a decision and a plan, not a one-site guard. For (1): make the host structs match agbcc (for example `__attribute__((packed))` or split bitfields under `PC_PORT` for the structs in `sram-layout.h`, checked against the GBA's DWARF sizes and member offsets), or serialize to the GBA layout at the SRAM boundary. The `gen_host_link` size warnings (`bonusclaim.o`, `icon.o`, `prepscreen.o` and four more) may be the same kind and should be checked with it. For (2): the GBA value is whatever its stack held, so it is not reproducible from the C source alone; decide whether to match the GBA's bytes for this save path (trace them in mGBA) or accept and mask them. For (3): the lag question of PLAN.md 3.4. **Done 2026-10-19 for (1) as D-18** (host structs take agbcc's layout; the `gen_host_link` warnings for `bonusclaim.o`, `icon.o`, `prepscreen.o` and five more were the same cause and are gone). SRAM at tick 60,000 after D-18: 45 of the 32 KiB differ, all of them (2), (3) and the checksums over them (table in the D-18 details). Besides the two stack bytes named here, the same class also covers `GMapSaveInfo` padding (bits 4-7 of byte 0x1C, bytes 0x21-0x23), the last `supplyItems` byte and `gmUnit` bytes 6-7. Accepted and logged, not fixed. **Cause 2 on the Continue path (2026-10-20, chapter saves, `pending/merged/chapsave.md` and its review):** after a Continue from the title menu, `InvalidateSuspendSave` (`src/bmsave.c`) fills only `kind` of its stack `struct SaveBlockInfo`, and `WriteSaveBlockInfo` sets `magic16`, `offset`, `size` and `checksum32` and writes the rest as found. So the two suspend block infos' `magic32` and `_pad_07` (SRAM `0x94-0x9B` and `0xA4-0xAB`, 8 to 10 bytes with the checksums) get `0x080A3121` on the GBA (a return address in `GetSaveReadAddr`, `bmsave-lib.c`, left on its stack; on the scripted run's path it was `0x080D0011`, above) and the PC's own stack bytes (`0x00040624` in that run), and `_pad_07` differs too (`00` against `03`). The game never reads them (`magic16` is 0, so `ReadSaveBlockInfo` stops there), and after a Resume the SRAM is equal. Same decision as the rest of cause 2: logged, not fixed; a guard would have to rebuild the GBA's stack contents. In `chapsave-frelia`'s end SRAM the three bytes before the pidStats copies are the last-`supplyItems`-byte case (section 3, "Round 3 integration"). **Cause 2 has its own entry since the round-5 fold: P-18** (every known site, and the open user choice). |

| P-26 (open: static list, not swept; `pending/folded/integrate5.md` "Static view", corrected by `review-integrate5.md`) | Host integer divisions in game code: 93 instructions in 52 functions of the Gate 0 build's `src/*.o` (80 signed `idiv`, 12 unsigned 32-bit `div`, and one 8-bit `divb` in `sub_80463A8`, `src/sio_bat.c`, `proc->unk_3a % gLinkArenaSt.unk_A0`, GBA `bl __umodsi3` at `0x080463D6`). The GBA ROM has 386 libgcc division calls in 202 functions (188 `__divsi3`, 87 `__modsi3`, 42 `__udivsi3`, 69 `__umodsi3`); GCC turns constant divisors into multiplications, so these 93 are the only places in game code that can trap. Reached with a zero divisor so far: only `sub_80131F0` (D-46), on the six bot routes and the 249,800-tick same-buttons run, GBA side and PC side. The NULL probe gives the GBA's result for 32-bit divides only, so the `divb` stays fatal even with it (link arena only, deferred by D5). A shipped trap handler (SIGFPE, and a Windows vectored handler) only as a crash net, the user's call: it would change shipped behaviour from "crash" to "the GBA's answer, logged". **Round 10 (audit10 CC-3, `pending/folded/audit10-compiler.md`, low):** `PortModsi3`/`PortUdivsi3`/`PortUmodsi3` beside `PortDivsi3`, used only where a zero divisor is shown reachable; seen: `GetGameWinPerc` (`gamerankings.c:406-411`) divides by `PidStatsGetTotalBattleAmt()` unguarded, but at the end-of-game ranking battles are > 0.
