**Stubs first hit in the same run: fixed 2026-10-19 (B-6, B-7).** `SetEventId_0x84` and `ResetAllPlayerUnitState` (`src/eventcall.c`, cast used as an lvalue) at frame 12,210, and `GetCurrentPromotedLevelBonus` (`src/bmdifficulty.c`, const and non-const data in one section) were generated stubs that returned 0 or did nothing. Both files now build, so these three and every other function in them run the real code, checked as B-6 and B-7 describe. The scripted run can now differ from the commits before B-6 and B-7 from frame 12,210 on (flag 0x84 is set), and from 12,916 on (promoted units get their autolevel and its RNG draws). The audio stubs (`m4a*`, `MPlayStart`) that the scripted run still hit then are gone too: since the audio merge (`0da9e831`, B-13) the FULL build's `autostubs.c` has 59 generated stubs, none of them a sound-driver function, and the 60,000-tick scripted Gate 0 run on the merge logs no generated stub. The port stubs `DecodeString` and `DrawGlyph` (`arm.s`) are ported since D-12; no `[stub]` line for either appears in the scripted run or attract mode.

**Known text-pixel difference (closed 2026-10-19 by D-12: both routines are ported; the text below is the old note).** `DrawGlyph` and `DecodeString` (`asm/arm.s`, GBA `0x08000564` and `0x080006E4`) are still logging stubs on the PC (`port/asm/arm_pc.c:173-182`), reached through `ramfunc_pc.c` (`DrawGlyphRam`, `CallARM_DecompText`). So the pixels of decoded text differ from the GBA on every screen that shows text, including the terrain name in the window D-9 lets through. Tilemaps, digits and window frames are not affected. In the scripted run and in attract mode only the `DecodeString` stub is logged (frames 4,499 and 7,634); the `DrawGlyph` stub is not logged in either run. Porting the two routines from `arm.s` (PLAN.md 3.5) removes this difference. It is not a guard item and needs no fidelity decision.

**Gate 0 compare of the scripted run (2026-10-19, after D-10; a report, no game code changed for it).** The Gate 0 harness (`port/test/`) replayed the scripted run's inputs as a key file (A on ticks 4,301 and 4,302, then every 90 ticks; on the PC tick t reads the `FE8PC_AUTOPRESS` mask of frame t-1). The test binary hit the same fault at frame 21,581, so the inputs are the same; the trace stops at tick 21,580, just before it. The GBA trace took 21,732 frames for the 21,581 ticks (lag ticks at 1 and from 3,791 on; the PC has none). Leaving out `gGameClock` and `gBmSt.prevVCount`, the traces match to tick 8,627. **First difference: tick 8,628, `gLCDControlBuffer` +0x22 (`bgoffset[1]`, BG1 y), GBA 0, PC 1.** On the PC, BG1 then scrolls to 24 by tick 8,679 and back from 8,893; on the GBA it stays 0. Then `gOam` (8,630), `gPaletteBuffer` (8,677), `gBG2TilemapBuffer` (8,678) and the rest. Cause, from the proc arrays (an mGBA Lua dump and the PC's exit dump, neither kept): both sides start the Prologue world-map talk (`gProcScr_Talk`) at tick 8,609. On the GBA it is still running at tick 9,000 (`gProcScr_TalkSkipListener`, then `gProcScr_TalkWaitForInput`). On the PC it has ended by tick 8,619, before the next A press, and the event script has run on to `EventB6_WmMoveBigMap` (`ProcScr_GmapRmUpdateExt`, the BG1 scroll). The `DecodeString` stub does not write the decoded message, so the PC talk has no text to print and ends at once. So the stub changes event timing too, not only text pixels, and Gate 0 traces past the first talk need `DecodeString` ported first.

**Gate 0 compare of the scripted run after the integration (2026-10-19, merge `ee874c9f` = D-11, D-12 and the exact D-3 and D-5; a report, no game code changed for it).** Same key file (`build/gate0/scripted_a90.keys`) and the same GBA trace (the ROM is byte-identical). The PC test binary (`build/pc-gate0`) faults at frame 15,800 (P-11), so the PC trace has 15,797 complete ticks, and ticks 0-15,796 were compared (output `build/gate0/compare_i3.txt`). Leaving out `gGameClock` and `gBmSt.prevVCount`:
- **First difference: tick 5,876, `gLCDControlBuffer` +0x4A (`bg2affin.pb`, low byte), GBA 0x25, PC 0x26, 4 bytes.** Ticks 5,876-5,922 (44 ticks) differ in `bg2affin` only. At 5,876 the GBA has pa 376, pb 37, pc -37, pd 376 and the PC has pa 376, pb 38, pc -38, pd 376 (`dx`, `dy` follow); on some later ticks `pa`/`pd` also differ by 1. This is the oracle limit in section 5, not a port divergence: mGBA's HLE `BgAffineSet` uses floating point and truncates toward zero, while D-11 uses the hardware's integer method, where `asr` rounds a negative product down. On the `port-text` branch alone (the old floating-point `BgAffineSet`) the LCD buffer matched on every tick to 15,789. After tick 5,922 the LCD buffer matches again on every compared tick.
- Then the two clock effects already seen on `port-text`: `gOam` from tick 8,630 (one byte, entry 0 attr0 y, the "press A" arrow, which `TalkWaitForInput_OnIdle`, `scene.c:1405`, animates from `GetGameClock() / 2`) and `gPlaySt` +0x04 (`time_chapter_started`, set from `GetGameClock()` at `bmio.c:1005`) from tick 15,184. Both come from the GBA's lag ticks; the PC has none.
- Equal on every compared tick: `gKeyStatusIgnoredSt`, `gKeyComboResetEN`, `gSoftwareResetFlag`, `gMainCallback`, `gBmSt` (without `prevVCount`), `gPaletteBuffer` and all four BG tilemaps. The tick-8,628 difference (BG1 y) is gone.

**Gate 0 compare of the scripted run after D-13 and D-14 (2026-10-19; a report, no game code changed for it).** Key file `build/gate0/scripted_a90_long.keys`: the same pattern as `scripted_a90.keys` (whose 795 entries end at tick 40,031) carried on to tick 61,001; the first 795 entries are the same. GBA trace: 60,000 ticks in 60,514 frames, from a copy of the ROM with the same SHA-1; its first 21,581 ticks equal the old trace. PC: the Gate 0 test binary (`build/pc-gate0`) runs all 60,000 ticks with no fault (59,999 frames, 55,744 `OnMain` calls), the same trace in two runs. Ticks 0-59,999 compared (`build/gate0/compare_p11.txt`), with `gGameClock` and `gBmSt.prevVCount` left out and the new `--mask-oracle-limits` option of `gate0_compare.py`, which masks `bg2affin` pa, pb, pc and pd on ticks 5,876-5,922 only (section 5):
- The mask hid a difference on 44 ticks (5,876-5,922).
- **First remaining difference: tick 5,876, `gLCDControlBuffer` +0x50 (`bg2affin.dx`), GBA 0x23, PC 0x12; 2 bytes (the low bytes of dx and dy).** dx and dy come out of the same `BgAffineSet` call and are computed from pa..pd (dx = texX - pa*scrX - pb*scrY), so this is the same oracle limit. They differ on the same 44 ticks and nowhere else. The mask was kept to pa..pd as asked; widening it to `+0x48-0x57` would hide these too.
- Then, in tick order: `gOam` from 8,630 and `gPlaySt` +0x04 from 15,184 (the known clock effects; `gPlaySt` +0x00-01, `time_saved`, and +0x05 join later, also from `GetGameClock()`); `gPaletteBuffer` palette 4 from 33,733 (33,733-35,649, 41,188-44,367, 48,388-51,747 and one tick at 56,949); `gBG0TilemapBuffer` and `gBG1TilemapBuffer` at 41,189-41,191; `gLCDControlBuffer` `bgoffset[2]` (+0x24, +0x26, twice +0x44) at 56,940-57,252. **Not traced yet** (BG0/BG1 41,189-41,191 and the palette ticks that are not lag-only: traced since, section 5, "GBA lag paths found in round 1"). They look like lag or clock phase, not logic: the PC's palette 4 at 33,733 is the GBA's palette 4 at 33,761-33,764 and every 63-64 ticks after (a cycling palette, out of phase); GBA lag ticks come just before each start (33,641, 33,642, 33,718; 41,111, 41,112, 41,188; 48,311, 48,312, 48,388); the PC's BG0/BG1 cells at 41,190 are the GBA's at 41,189 (a sliding window one tick behind); and the PC's `bgoffset[2]` at 56,940 is the GBA's at 57,000. Procs on the VBlank tree run once per VBlank, so GBA lag frames run them more often than the PC does.
- Around the first NULL-face read (tick 15,800) and the save (56,681) only the clock fields of `gPlaySt` (and `gOam` near the save) differ.
- Equal on every compared tick: `gKeyStatusIgnoredSt`, `gKeyComboResetEN`, `gSoftwareResetFlag`, `gMainCallback`, `gBmSt` (without `prevVCount`), `gBG2TilemapBuffer` and `gBG3TilemapBuffer`.
- SRAM is not in the trace. A separate whole-SRAM compare at tick 60,000 found P-12.

**Same loop shape, still latent.** `worldmap_rm.c` has five more `do { proc = Proc_FindNext(...); ... proc->field ... } while (proc != NULL);` loops: `EndGmapRmBorder1` (line 775), `SetGmapRmBorder1FlagForIndex` (827), `EndWmPlaceDotByIndex` (1172), `IsWmPlaceDotActiveAtIndex` (1197) and `SetWmPlaceDotFlagForIndex` (1216, P-4). When no proc matches, each one reads or writes through NULL at an offset below `0x40`. Writes are dropped (H-1), and with B-5 the loop's NULL test is kept, so the loop then ends as on the GBA instead of hanging as in P-4. Reads fault and need a guard like D-5 (now `PORT_NULL_READ`; these loops run on the world map, where the HBlank IRQ is on, so the latch would be post-IRQ [inferred from D-5's measurement]). Same shape, also latent (2026-10-20): `EndGmAutoMuFor` (`worldmap_automu.c:324-333`, D-35's sibling), which retail cannot reach with a NULL proc.

**For the next decision:** the first world map hit three NULL-pointer reads in the BIOS area within 438 frames (8,412, 8,677 and 8,850), then a hang caused by the host compiler at 11,864. Fixing each site with a guard will keep adding entries here. Two things to decide: (1) whether to add `-fno-delete-null-pointer-checks` to the host build (P-4; done 2026-10-19 as B-5), and (2) whether development builds may fall back to a logged policy value for BIOS-area reads while the sites are being fixed. Release builds would still get guarded fixes. (2) is done as a diagnostic mode only: the NULL probe, `FE8PC_NULLPROBE=1` (section 3, "Round 3 integration"); every other build still faults (H-3), and each site gets a guarded fix.

## 5. Gate 0 oracle limits (not port divergences)

- 2026-10-19: the user has no BIOS dump from their own GBA. Gate 0 therefore runs mGBA 0.10.5 with its built-in high-level (HLE) BIOS. We cannot download or ship a BIOS.
- Known limits of this oracle:
  - Open-bus reads from the BIOS area may return bytes from mGBA's HLE BIOS image, not the real BIOS latch values (`0xE3A02004` after a SWI, `0xE55EC002` after an IRQ) that the port reproduces. No traced field has shown it (OL-1 stays listed, never masked). Round 3 (2026-10-20) measured the latch at every NULL-fix site: mGBA's HLE BIOS returned the real latch words there (`0xE3A02004` after its SWI return, `0xE55EC002` after its IRQ return). The limit left is timing: whether an IRQ lands between the last SWI and the read on hardware, which each entry argues from the event order, with margins from about 5 scanlines (D-24) to 148 IRQs (D-35).
  - mGBA's HLE `BgAffineSet` and `ObjAffineSet` use floating point. The port uses the hardware's integer method with the truncated sine table (D-11). **Hit:** scripted run, ticks 5,876-5,922, `gLCDControlBuffer.bg2affin` (section 4, Gate 0 compare after the integration). The PC values follow the integer method; the GBA trace values follow mGBA's float method. pa, pb, pc and pd differ on 44 of these ticks, and dx and dy (computed from them in the same call) on the same 44. `port/test/tools/gate0_compare.py --mask-oracle-limits` masked pa..pd on these ticks (`ORACLE_LIMIT_MASKS`, added 2026-10-19 in commit `17c4e916`); dx and dy were not masked. No other `bg2affin` difference up to tick 59,999. Since the Gate 0 upgrade (merged in `35012930`) this is **OL-2** in `port/test/oracle_limits.json`: only `bg2affin`/`bg3affin`, only where pa..pd differ by at most 1 and x, y by at most 201 (so dx and dy are covered too); the script's table is gone and `--mask-oracle-limits` now means `--masks port/test/oracle_limits.json`. Scripted run 2026-10-19 (upgrade branch): covers ticks 5,876-5,922 (44), largest x/y delta 183. ObjAffineSet is **OL-3** (OAM attr3, delta at most 1): first hit 2026-10-20 on the route compares from ch3 (2 ticks in 67,778-67,813, delta 1; section 3, "Round 3 integration"). Open-bus reads are **OL-1**, listed but never masked.
  - **The same limit in frames** (renderer, D-15 details). In the P2.1 no-key run, the title screen's rotating lights (BG2 affine, rotated, ticks 6,568-6,655, 88 frames) differ from mGBA by 12 to 4,802 pixels per frame. The check compares the shadow `bg2affin` (`gLCDControlBuffer` +0x48) that each frame shows, which is the one sampled at tick T-1. Every one of the 88 frames has a different `bg2affin`, nothing else in `gLCDControlBuffer` differs, and no frame with an equal `bg2affin` differs. The values differ mainly in dx and dy, by up to about 0.7 px (tick 6,568: dy 59,565 against 59,736), and once in pa and pd (tick 6,653: 32 against 31). The diff images show it only at the edges of the lights. A 12,000-tick run (before D-17) also differed only at 5 class-demo frames (7,740 to 11,340), with `gLCDControlBuffer` different there too: the same rotation path (`opinfo.c:810`). (The open choice for rotated affine checkpoints that stood here was decided as P2.2b, next bullet.)
  - **P2.2b, decided 2026-10-19** (folded from `pending/p22b.md`, branch `port-p22b` `09702151`, merged on `port-integrate`): a rotated-affine frame passes when the only difference is the known `bg2affin` oracle limit. Gate 0 applies it through OL-2's `"frames": true` (`port/test/oracle_limits.json`, README "Oracle-limit frames" and "Affine replay"): a `frame15` difference at tick t is masked in RESULT only when (1) every difference at tick t-1, in every field (`frame15` included), lies inside OL-2, all of it in `bg2affin`, and there is at least one; (2) at tick t-1 BG2 is shown (DISPCNT not force-blanked, BG2 on, mode 1-5); (3) nothing else differs at tick t; (4) the affine replay proves it: the PC run again, with that frame drawn with the GBA's `bg2affin` of tick t-1 in BG2PA-BG2Y, draws exactly the GBA's frame, the same frame redrawn with the PC's own registers is the PC trace's frame, and the replay equals the PC trace outside the frames at ticks t-1 and t. A `bg3affin` difference leaves the frame reported: the user's scope decision (2026-10-19 night) is `bg2affin` only; extend it to `bg3affin` (BG3 in mode 2) when a trace shows that limit. Scripted run (60,000 ticks): frame 5,880 masked (drawn with the GBA's `bg2affin` the PC gives the GBA's `7f8e62bc`; redrawn with its own registers the PC trace's `2e28b4d6`). No-key lights (6,560-6,660, every tick): all 88 rotated frames masked, the 13 others equal outright. Negative checks (mutated copies): an arbitrary PC frame at 5,880, an arbitrary replay frame, a PC-side picture change in both runs, no `--affine-replay`, the replay changed outside the frame, a replay marked `regs`, `bg3affin` +1 on top, and a replay that ends early are each reported (exit 1). **What it cannot see** (the user put this at low priority, 2026-10-19 night): whether the PC draws right with its *own* matrix. The proof shows "PC state + GBA matrix = GBA picture"; a renderer fault that shows only with the PC's exact values would pass. A GBA-side counterpart (mGBA drawing with the PC's `bg2affin`) would close it; deferred. The redraw with the PC's registers is drawn at once after line 159, so a frame that HBlank handlers change mid-frame is reported, never passed.
  - mGBA blends in 8-bit space; the GBA, and the shipped PC build, in 5-bit space. With the 5-bit math the 7,000-tick no-key run has 63 differing frames outside the lights, most by one step in one channel. Frame checkpoints therefore run the PC with `FE8PC_BLEND=mgba8`, and `pc-trace` does so.
  - **OL-4 (sound capture; proposed in `sound-wiring.md`).** For an enabled FIFO DMA, mGBA stores the destination control in DMAxCNT_H bits 5-6 as "fixed" (`0xB640`) where the driver wrote `0xB600`. Nothing reads those bits back (the driver tests only bit 9, `m4a.c:315`; the FIFO model only the enable bit and the timing). `port/test/audio/m4a_replay.py:93-99` (the comment and `cnth`, on `port-integrate`; this line cited `:124-130`, right at `0da9e831` but moved by `port-sndcheck`, as its reviewer flagged) leaves bits 5-6 out. Not in `oracle_limits.json`: Gate 0 does not trace the DMA registers.
  - **OL-5 (2026-10-20; proposed in `pending/merged/groupF-design.md` and `nullfix-groupF.md`, mask on `port-integrate2` `48295519`): mGBA's AGB Print window sign-extends cartridge halfword loads past a 16 MiB image.** mGBA 0.10.5 (`src/gba/memory.c`, `GBALoad16`) sends an out-of-range cartridge load whose low 24 address bits are below `0xFE0000` (`AGB_PRINT_TOP`; `AGB_PRINT_BASE` is `0xFD0000`) through `_agbPrintLoad`, which returns `int16_t`, so `LDRH` at `0x0B2BFFE6` gives `0xFFFFFFF3`. The ARM7TDMI's `LDRH` zero-extends, so no hardware gives that, whatever the cartridge drives; D-27 zero-extends. **Hit:** route ch1 and every longer route, ticks 20,272-20,277, and `chapsave-frelia`, 4,309-4,314: `gBG3TilemapBuffer` cells (col 25, row 4) and (cols 21-25, row 5), GBA `61df 61df 61df 65df 65df 61ff`, PC `6000` each (row pointer `0x0B2C0000` at map row -6, columns -3..-1). A scratch build copying mGBA's sign extension matched the GBA on all 1,024 cells of every tick, so these 6 cells are the whole effect. Mask in `port/test/oracle_limits.json`: field `gBG3TilemapBuffer`, check `cells` (new in `port/test/tools/gate0_compare.py`, `check_cells`: listed u16 cells, offsets `0x132` and `0x16A`-`0x172`, each covered only when the GBA and PC halfwords are exactly a listed pair), condition `"chapter": "0x38"` (new optional key, `mask_condition`: `gPlaySt.chapterIndex` on both sides). It uses the chapter and the exact values rather than a tick range, because chapter-save runs reach the spot at other ticks. Negative checks (scratch copies of the file): one changed value covers 5 of 6 cells and reports the sixth; another chapter covers 0 ticks. What a retail cartridge drives there (the undriven bus or mirrored ROM) is a separate open user choice (D-27); either way the GBA zero-extends. **Second hit (round 9, `pending/folded/r9-ch8exp.md`):** route_ch8, the end of Chapter 8 (the map change 43 to 56, Castle Frelia again), ticks 86,709-86,714, the same 6 cells and pairs, covered by the mask (cov9's G5 (b) was this limit; its compare had the raw dumps set aside, so masks were not evaluated: read "masks not evaluated" before calling a BG difference new). On Ephraim's ending (a scratch key file) 16 more BG3 cells differ on 86,716-86,721 in every build, GBA `0x8000`/`0x8222` against PC `0x6000`: that is D-27's latch gap (the second `RenderBmMap` of that map change reads the BIOS area with the post-IRQ latch, the tick's last lag VBlank coming after the last SWI on that branch), not OL-5, and it cannot be seen (black screen, `frame15` equal).
  - **OL-6 (2026-10-21, round 9, `pending/folded/r9-frames.md`): mGBA cuts horizontally flipped text-BG tiles at window segments.** mGBA 0.10.5 draws each BG once per window segment of a line (`video-software.c`, `_breakWindow`), and the flip branch of `software-mode0.c`'s `DRAW_BACKGROUND_MODE_0_TILE_SUFFIX_16` starts a segment's first, partial tile at the segment's end with the tile's pixel 0, so a segment that starts inside a flipped tile and ends before that tile's last pixel comes out shifted (the PPU test ROM's variant 2: WIN0 [130, 135) over a flipped tile on 128-135 gives indices 5 4 3 2 1 where the hardware rule gives 6 5 4 3 2; the hardware draws a BG whatever the windows are). **Hit:** round 8's 85 minimap-window frames (the window's tips; first 10,929, line 100, BG3 tile `0x021` with h+v flip). Not a mask: Gate 0's PC runs set `FE8PC_MGBA_BGCLIP=1` (next to `FE8PC_BLEND=mgba8`, the same kind of switch; `port/test/Makefile`, `konami.mk`), which copies the cut in `port/render/bg_line.c` (`RenderMgbaBgClip`); the shipped build draws as the hardware does. Copied for 16-colour text BGs only: mGBA's 256-colour suffix has the same cut (the review's 8bpp scene differs by 320 pixels with the switch on) and mosaic takes another path; harmless unless FE8 shows one. **A candidate, not a mask: OBJ-window sprites and transparent pixels.** D-15's lift rule follows mGBA 0.10.5, where an OBJ-window sprite's clear pixels lift nothing; mGBA 0.11's change log fixes exactly that ("transparent OBJWIN pixel/sprite priority interaction", mgba.io/i/2809), so the hardware probably lifts there. FE8 uses OBJ-window sprites (`bmudisp.c`, `bmusailment.c`, `opanim-main.c`); no frame has shown a difference. A newer oracle or a hardware test would settle it.
  - **OL-7 (2026-10-21, round 9's fix passes; `pending/folded/r9-uiarena.md` section 8, which proposed it as OL-6, a number round 9's frames limit already holds): mGBA's open bus after a DMA.** mGBA 0.10.5's `GBALoadBad` returns `gba->bus` during a DMA, or for a load whose PC is one instruction past `gba->dmaPC`, the PC at the last DMA update, which stays until the next DMA; `gba->bus` is refreshed only at event processing (to the next prefetch word) and by each DMA unit. So in a loop the stale word comes back on every pass for up to 16 lines (FE8's sound FIFO DMA runs every 20,064 cycles, 14 times a frame), and the capture's own breakpoints refresh `gba->bus` too, so the oracle's value there also depends on the harness (with the review's breakpoint at `0x03003CD4` every stale word was `0xE51F8048`). The port gives GBATEK's prefetch word (`[$+8]` in ARM mode). Real hardware probably also shows a DMA's last word for a read right after a DMA (mGBA's change log: "GBA DMA: Linger last DMA on bus", a game-compatibility fix; the window is not documented and was not checked here), while mGBA keeps it for every loop pass until the next DMA. Neither can be followed without a cycle model (PLAN 3.4). **Hit:** the arena opponent's flood on Eirika 10's map (H-10): terrain loads in unused space; stale words in 4 of 6 forced arenas and in 2 of 8 full route variants (15 and 18 loads); one forced run changed 30 bytes of the flood's fill pools; EWRAM, the opponent's square and the random numbers were equal in every run, and a bound shows that on that map the square cannot depend on these cells. No traced field and no frame differs. Listed, no mask. Any other open-bus guard (`PortGbaLoad*`, the THUMB loads of D-55, D-56 and D-57) can meet it in principle; none did in the traced runs (the THUMB sites had the rule off in every traced run). **Second hit, with a consequence (round 10, C10-3, `pending/folded/r10-traps.md`):** D-60's death-record load (`ldrb` at `0x08026448`, the open bus 0x42 by GBATEK). At `route_e18` 26,515 the sound DMA's stale word comes back for five tiles in a row: with the capture's own breakpoints 0x00 x4 and 0xFC (no record but pid 0's, which `GetPidStats(0)` places on `gUnused_0203E884`, `gPidStatsData[-1]`), with no breakpoint in the loop death records for Seth (0x02) and Franz (0x04) as well. So mGBA's save data (two characters' trap deaths, in the suspend saves and the chapter's save) differs from the PC's, which keeps 0x42. It reaches a traced field at the chapter's end: `GetChapterDeathCount` counts Seth (dead since an earlier chapter, his `deathLoc` now 0x12) as a death in Chapter 18, so `gPlaySt.unk_30.survivalRank` (+0x3E bits 5-7) is 2 on mGBA and 3 on the PC from tick 92,398 to the end (365 ticks), and the chapter's save (its `PlaySt` copy, SRAM `0x4002`) has it too. (D-60's exact fix does not make that byte equal: `GetChapterDeathCount` counts dead army units only, so character 0x42's record is never counted.) 1 of 42 trap updates on the three starts. The next chapter is not affected: `route_e19` boots both sides from `e19_start.sav`, which mGBA made. **Masked (2026-10-22, `port-r10-petrify` `727a4720`, `pending/folded/r10-petrify.md`; the reviewers' recommended option, the user said "I trust your judgement"):** `oracle_limits.json` OL-7 covers `gPlaySt.unk_30.survivalRank` (+0x3E bits 5-7, check `bitfield`, new, general: a later bit-field limit can reuse it) only for the pair GBA 2 / PC 3 with every other bit of the byte equal, and only with chapter 0x13 loaded on both sides (Eirika's Chapter 19: `GameControl_ChapterSwitch` writes Chapter 18's ranks and loads the next chapter in one tick; 0x12 covered nothing). Hit: `route_e18` 92,398-92,762 (365 ticks), nothing else differing; the route is MATCH on 0-92,762 with it. The chapter save the route ends with (SRAM, the `PlaySt` copy at `0x4002`, and Seth's and Franz's `pidStats`) is not traced and not masked. The mask's self-test is a scratch script (`r10pet_masktest.py`; `gate0_compare.py` has no test suite). An oracle-side alternative (the petrify review): with one extra GBA breakpoint at `0x08026448`, mGBA gives rank 3 and `route_e18` MATCHes with no mask; that oracle still depends on its breakpoints, so the narrow mask stays.
- Rule: where a trace difference comes from one of these sites, judge it against hardware facts (GBATEK, the mgba-emu/suite hardware test vectors), not against the oracle. Log each such case here as an oracle limit, not as a port divergence. Gate 0 masks them through `port/test/oracle_limits.json` (`port/test/README.md`, "Rules and masks"); a new limit needs an entry here first. A real BIOS dump would remove OL-1 to OL-3.
- **GBA lag is not an oracle limit, and is labelled, not masked** (decided for the user, 2026-10-19, at the merge of `port-gate0`). The GBA lags (a VBlank comes while `OnMain` is still running); the PC does not (PLAN.md 3.4). Gate 0 takes the lag out of `gGameClock` and its two `gPlaySt` copies exactly (the clock rule). What the game does with the clock after a lag (for example the talk's "press A" arrow, `scene.c:1405`) still differs. The standard Gate 0 result first included the clock replay (the PC run again with the GBA's `gGameClock` set before each `OnVBlank`); since `port-lagcheck` that is a diagnostic only, and the lag check is the lag replay below. The verdict is `MATCH`, `LAG-ONLY` (exit 3, reported, not fatal in `make`) or `DIFFERENT`. A gGameClock or clock-copy rule failure is never lag-only.
- **Lag check v2 (2026-10-19, `port-lagcheck` `ec4092a5`, tightened in the round 2 review; folded from `pending/lagcheck-v2.md`; on `port-integrate`).** A RESULT difference at tick t is lag-only only when it is reproduced by the lag replay at the GBA's positions, for the whole tick. The lag replay is the PC run again with every GBA lag VBlank run, as the game's own `OnVBlank` with `main_loop_ended` 0, before the same main-loop event of the same tick as on the GBA. The position measure counts `RunProcessRecursive` entries and returns, `RunProcessScript` entries, `GetGameClock` and `SetGameTime`, outside `OnVBlank` (GBA: eight debugger breakpoints in `gate0_capture.lua`, trace format 3 with `events` and `lag_at`; PC: two more `--wrap`s and `proc.o` with `-finstrument-functions` in the Gate 0 test build only). The replay's RESULT must have no difference at t, with its `gGameClock` and the `gPlaySt` clock copies equal to the GBA's outright (enforced since round 2; before, the clock rule could take an extra replay `OnVBlank` out as lag), and on every tick up to t its event count and lag positions must be the GBA's (strict prefix: after the replay's first sequence mismatch no later tick is lag-only). A tick where only the replay differs (replay-only) fails the gate (round 2; before, it was only listed). Lag stays unmodelled in the game (PLAN 3.4); this is harness only, in `port/test/`. **Result** (60,000-tick scripted route): before (clock replay) 25,390 lag-only, 167 not, 0 replay-only; after, 25,557 lag-only, 0 not, 0 replay-only, `GATE 0: LAG-ONLY` (the integration run is in section 3, "Round 2 integration"). The lag replay compared on its own with the GBA is equal in every field on every tick apart from `gBmSt.prevVCount`, `bg2affin` on the 44 OL-2 ticks and the OL-2 frame 5,880. Wording (round 2): such ticks are "reproduced by the lag replay at the GBA's positions", not "proven" or "GBA lag". **Remaining risk:** on the lag-only ticks, 25,557 of 60,000 (43% of the route), the PC's own lag-free run is checked only through the replay; a port fault that the replayed lag VBlanks happen to hide there would not show. **Not resolved:** two lag-relevant actions inside one proc step with no event between them; a lag VBlank after a tick's last event but before `OnMain`'s tail (`PushSpriteLayerObjects`; low OAM, weather maps only); events in HBlank, VCount, timer or serial IRQ handlers (none on this route; a mismatch shows and stops the labelling). The `_irq`/`_resume` correction for a lag VBlank at the first instruction of an entry breakpoint is in place but was not exercised (0 of 501 on this route). **Round 9's fix passes (`port-integrate9` `b9e41d53`, cov9 A2):** a lag VBlank at a proc script's head, after `RunProcessScript`'s entry and before its first command, is placed there (`lag_head`; Ch2 Supply screen `ProcScr_SlidingWallBg`: `DIFFERENT` on 291 ticks before, `MATCH` after). The fourteenth breakpoint `gate0_rpscmd` (+0x3C) tells the loop's later passes from the head. The PC runs the VBlank before `RunProcessScript` reads anything of the proc; whenever the GBA's came after its first read (the `proc_script` load at +4, pc > 0x08003398, the IRQ's pc being the instruction not yet run; or at the veneer's `bx`; or at the command's first instruction, with every read done), the capture checks after that VBlank's tree-0 walk that the proc's script, lock count, idle callback and current command (and a RAM script's opcode) are unchanged, and stops otherwise. On the CHECK_LONG routes 23 of the 24 heads are checked (6 in the call), none stops. Still not resolved: a lag VBlank inside a starting proc's first commands runs after `Proc_Start` has cleared `PROC_FLAG_STARTING` (reported by the compare, not seen). The PC side of the 14 heads that came after known route stops (all of ch8 and e10 and some of e9, e11, e12, e13) is proven now that those routes match to their ends. **Round 10 (C10-10 (c), the scout's Ch18-skirmish debug jump, ticks 2,667, 2,671, 2,685; recorded as the lag replay's limit, integration 11's choice):** the GBA's lag VBlank came inside `ColorFadeTick` in `ChapterIntro_LoopFastFadeToMap`, before `ResetMapPaletteAnimations` in the same proc step; the replay runs it after that step, so the VBlank tree's `BMapVSync_UpdateMapPalAnimations` steps one VBlank early on the PC until `BMapVSync_Start` (tick 2,690): `gPaletteBuffer` BG palette 9 colours 12-15 one step early on 3 ticks. Measured on both sides (`pending/folded/r10-frames.md` section 3). Debug-jump path only so far; the alternative, a finer lag position inside a long ARM routine in the capture and the replay, is not taken.
- **GBA capture limit found in round 3 (2026-10-20; fixed on `port-integrate4` by `dc40eb37`, below; `pending/merged/integrate2.md`, `integrate4.md`).** `port/test/oracle/gate0_capture.lua` stops route ch2 at tick 28,865: "4 lag VBlanks placed, the counters say 5". In the battle animation main loop (`MainUpdate_8055C68`, waits from lr `0x08055CC5`), a VBlank IRQ came after the loop had set `main_loop_ended` but before its `VBlankIntrWait` (VBlank 30,331, `main_loop_ended` 1, 19 events). `OnVBlank` then flushed the frame and cleared the flag, so the capture took the loop's following wait (flag 0) for an inner wait, and two main-loop iterations merged into one tick. Route ch5 has 3 such VBlanks (ticks 28,865, 75,618 and 108,956). No earlier run got that far with lag check v2: main's PC stopped at 3,714, so its long route rows were skipped. Until a fix lands, the `CHECK_LONG` route rows ch2-ch5 fail at the GBA capture on every branch. A diagnostic fix exists (scratch, not committed: `~/gate0-scratch/int2/diag/early_flush_capture.diff`, script `wf-fe8/int2_diag_capture.sh`): an `OnVBlank` with `main_loop_ended` set while the CPU is not in the main loop's wait counts as a lag VBlank at the tick's last event position; the main loop's wait that follows is the tick's sample, and the VBlank that ends it is the tick's own. With it `route_ch5` captures all 135,505 ticks (142,341 frames, 56,354 `OnMain` calls, the same tick numbering as round 3's traces), and the PC lag replay places every lag VBlank at the GBA's position except 3 inside the host-ABI cluster (67,5xx-67,8xx). It changes the tick definition at those spots, so it needs a review and a user decision before it goes in. The route results for ch2-ch5 in section 3, "Round 3 integration", come from that diagnostic capture. **Fixed (2026-10-20, `port-integrate4` `dc40eb37`, the early flush VBlank; README "Early flush VBlank"; harness only, in `port/test/`).** Every main loop ends `gBmSt.main_loop_ended = 1; gBmSt.prevVCount = REG_VCOUNT; VBlankIntrWait();` (`OnMain`, `MainUpdate_8055C68`, `InBattleMainRoutine`). A VBlank in that gap runs `OnVBlank` with the flag set: it flushes the frame and clears the flag, and the wait then waits one more frame, with no flush. `gate0_capture.lua` now counts such an `OnVBlank` (flag set, before the main loop's wait breakpoint) as an early flush: a lag VBlank at the tick's last event position (`lag_at`), marked `"early_flush":1` on the tick line, and the tick is sampled at the main loop's wait that follows; an unexpected order fails the capture, and its last line counts them. The lag replay (`gate0_hook.c`) runs the tick's early flush at the sample point after its other lag VBlanks, with the flag as the main loop left it (1), so `OnVBlank` flushes and clears it; the frame step's own `OnVBlank` then finds it clear and does not flush, as on the GBA. `gate0_compare.py` requires the replay's early-flush ticks to be the GBA's, with the positions and events. `sram_capture.lua` counts ticks the same way, so `SRAM_AT=tick:T` stays aligned past an early flush (route ch2 `tick:31000`: equal to `gate0_capture`'s `GATE0_SRAM`; the old count gives 6 bytes different). Results: route ch5 captures all 135,505 ticks (142,341 frames, 56,354 `OnMain` calls, 3 early flushes: 28,865 in `MainUpdate_8055C68` after 19 events, 75,618 after 31 and 108,956 after 23, both in `InBattleMainRoutine`); the lag replay runs all 6,823 lag VBlanks, the 3 early flushes included, at the GBA's positions; the 60,000-tick scripted capture and its lag replay are byte-identical to the old ones (0 early flushes there). `sound_capture.lua`'s key-rule check still counts ticks the old way; at an early flush it would report a count mismatch, not go wrong silently (its two runs have none). **Finding: the bot routes count one tick differently.** `route_bot.lua` and `route_check.lua` count a tick at each frame callback that sees the flag set. At 28,865 and 75,618 they count the early-flush tick one frame before the capture writes it (then the ticks line up again). At 108,956 the frame event came a few cycles before the flag store and the IRQ after it, so the bot saw the flag clear, counted no tick and gave the next `OnMain` the same key mask again: from there the run the bot recorded is one `OnMain` behind the capture's. It is the same play: a scratch copy of `route_check.lua` that logs each tick shows both runs go through the same 1,190 changes of (RNG, chapter, phase, turn) at the same tick numbers and end in the same state (chapter 5, turn 1, `gRNSeeds` `a3b069620808`, `gLCGRNValue` `02652cda`). Open user choice (`integrate4.md` decision 1): keep the routes (recommended) or re-record them with the wait-based tick.
- **Complete runs (2026-10-20, `port-followups` `20bd51dd`, round 4; README).** Gate 0 fails as `INCOMPLETE` (exit 1) when any compared trace (the GBA's, the PC's, the lag replay's, the affine replay's) has fewer than the requested ticks, or a PC run's log has a `[fault]` line (`OnFault`, `port/hw/main_pc.c`; the `[bios-write]` lines of H-1 are not faults). The verdict on the ticks compared is printed first (`GATE 0 on the ticks compared: ...`), then `GATE 0: INCOMPLETE: <reason>`; `compare.json` gains `verdict_on_compared_ticks`, `incomplete` and `runs`. A missing trace or log is a harness error (exit 2). check-all's Gate 0 rows also require every PC run of the row complete (all ticks, no `[fault]`, no "stopped with status" line), whatever the compare says. Before it, a lag replay cut at 199 of 300 ticks gave `MATCH on ticks 0-198`, exit 0, in route mode. The clock replay (a diagnostic) is not one of the checked runs.
- **Raster-timing frames (decision 9, 2026-10-20; `port-harness8b` `88d742e8`, on `port-integrate6`; README "Raster-timing frames").** A `frame15` difference whose only cause is where the GBA's raster was when the main loop wrote is labelled "raster-timing frame", counted apart and left out of RESULT, only when the PC's own frame step, drawn again with the GBA's recorded store timing, is exactly the GBA's frame and the watched memory equals the GBA's at the frame's end; in the lag check such a tick is "lag-only, raster-timing frame", counted apart. MATCH, LAG-ONLY and DIFFERENT keep their meaning. The rule, its proof and what it does not cover are in P-17 (section 4). **Round 9** (`port-integrate8` `b3a86d7d`; README "Raster-timing frames"): the GBA record and the redraw also cover `REG_IE` and the BG scroll registers (I/O regions `ie` and `bgofs`, below `REG_IME`), and `gate0_compare.py` refuses a GBA trace whose `raster_log` header lacks them, so every GBA capture made before that commit must be made again before it can be compared. `monkey-check EVERY_FRAME=1` uses the same rule on every frame (`port-integrate7` `b5ce904e`, `pending/folded/r9-base.md`), and its frames pass when every differing frame is a raster-timing frame. **Check-point limit** (`pending/folded/r9-heal.md`; not fixed): `RasterEndFrame` checks the watched regions after line 159 is drawn and before its HBlank IRQ, while the GBA's block ends at line 160; a line handler that writes watched memory on line 159 (the map animation's HP-window gradient, `OnHBlank_ManimFrameGradient`, palette entries 0x11 and 0x21, during every heal) gets `differs` on equal frames (Prologue 164, Ch2 fort 152, TAS 159). They cannot be labelled; harmless while the pictures are equal. Fix: take the check after line 159's HBlank IRQ (a harness change with its own review). **Round 10** (`port-r10-frames` `0c43f929`, H-18): the record and the redraw also cover `REG_DISPSTAT` (I/O region `dispstat`, bits 3-15; the runner leaves the status flags out), and a store that turns an IRQ source on waits for the check of the GBA's next line event (`PortGate0IrqDue`, now also before `LineStart`'s and `LineHBlank`'s `REG_DISPSTAT` tests). So the HBlank IRQ that `OnVBlank`'s late `FlushLCDControl` turns on (after a slow tree-0 walk, line 182 on the GBA, 160 on the PC) is labelled exactly: C10-10 (b) (`mk10A_route_ch7_tick1639_s5` 21,060) and round 9's two monkey frames (`mkA_prologue_s1` 5,922, `mkB_ch5_start_s6_t16000` 6,411; round 9's "new frame kind", HBlank events from line 176 against 161). The whole TAS run, every frame: the same 1,684 frames labelled as before, 0 reported. Every GBA capture made before that commit must be made again before it can be compared (the `raster_log` header).
- **HLE BIOS copies and lag (format 4, 2026-10-20, `port-harness8b`; README "Lag position", kind 6).** mGBA 0.10.5's HLE BIOS image (read with `emu.memory.bios`, disassembled; the review found the same bytes in `libmgba.so`): the SWI table (0xD4) sends `LZ77UnCompWram`/`LZ77UnCompVram` (0x11, 0x12) and the math calls 0x06-0x0A (`Div` to `ArcTan2`) to 0x348, the C HLE (`svc 0xF00000`), which writes all the data at once, then a stall loop at 0x348 with IRQs on; `CpuSet` (0x214) and `CpuFastSet` (0x2A8) are ARM loops that an IRQ can interrupt between any two instructions. A real BIOS runs LZ77 as code too, so a lag VBlank inside an LZ77 call lands after all of its writes on mGBA and part way on hardware: an oracle limit, judged against hardware facts. For the two copies the capture records where a lag VBlank fell (`lag_split`, from the interrupted registers) and the lag replay splits the copy there, exact to the instruction; a lag VBlank inside a main-loop copy into other memory is listed as `lag_in_other_copy`. Route ch5: 17 splits, 123 `lag_in_other_copy` ticks, every traced field equal. **H-col-2 (cov9; the cause found in round 9, `pending/folded/r9-base.md`; the fix held, `pending/r9-harness.md`):** a lag VBlank nested inside an HBlank handler that had interrupted a main-loop 16-bit `CpuSet` fill into IWRAM (`HBlank_BgVerticalScroll` in Chapter 4's preparations, `mkB_ch4_start_s2.keys` tick 3,527): the capture reads the copy's progress from the live `r5`, which is then the handler's, and stops ("progress -50361596 written, 2 read does not add up"). The held fix reads the interrupted frame's registers (`runner_irq_reg`); not applied this round (its review found a hole in the same patch's A2 check; the fix pass `fp9-harness` closes it, not yet re-reviewed). Until then that Chapter 4 screen cannot be captured on the GBA side; G8's Chapter 5 trigger can. **Fixed on `port-integrate9` (`b9e41d53`):** the runner keeps the interrupted registers at each IRQ entry (`runner_irq_reg`), and the capture takes the main loop's from the outermost entry (24 bytes stored at 3,527, as the handler's push on the System stack shows) and checks the record at every lag VBlank; `mkB_ch4_start_s2` now captures to its end (section 3, "Round 9 fix passes and integration 9").
- **HLE BIOS LZ77 timing against the console: the TAS (2026-10-20, `pending/folded/tas-route.md`).** alyosha's console input list for the 1843M TAS (timed on a real GBA, 4096-cycle units from power-on) stays in sync on mGBA for 768 ticks only (power-on to the Prologue's world map, tick 767). Between the subtitle skip and press 10 (Start on that world map) mGBA has 7 lag VBlanks where the console had 6: tick 715 is one `LZ77UnCompVram` of 19,488 bytes that the HLE BIOS charges 1,001,101 cycles (about 51 a byte; it ends 132 lines into its fifth frame), and tick 673 has 609,314 cycles of HLE LZ77 (4 VRAM and 5 WRAM calls; 68 lines into its fourth frame). A real BIOS decompressing about 14 % (tick 673) or 16 % (tick 715) faster removes the frame, so press 10 lands one frame early on mGBA and the run leaves the TAS's plan. Most likely cause: the HLE BIOS's LZ77 timing. Not proven (no BIOS dump). In tick terms mGBA probably agrees with the console: the press works from mGBA's tick 769, where the console, one lag frame ahead, would have read it. Effects for Gate 0: lag positions are mGBA's, so around long BIOS calls they may differ from the console's; past tick 767 the TAS is a same-buttons test only (DECISIONS.md, 2026-10-20 evening: keep the short route; coverage from the bot routes and menu monkeys; no BIOS dump, resync or replacement BIOS for now). The VBA-rr movie is lost on its first press (VBA-rr reaches the health and safety screen 4 frames earlier).
- **Save states (harness; README "Save states"; `port-savestate` on main, hardened by `port-harness8a` `493476bf`).** A photo resumed by the binary that took it is exact (every trace line and raw record equal to the full run). A photo resumed by a changed binary goes through the relink, which is best effort: it checks each rewritten word against the GBA's own photo (the GBA never holds a PC address), matches function statics by their enclosing function, refuses a binary that adds game `.data` unless `SAVESTATE_NEW_GAME_DATA=initial` is set (loud, part of the patch key; `CONST_DATA` tables are writable `.data` under `PC_PORT`), and remakes its patch whenever the tool or the photo's files change. Two classes still pass the relink wrongly and fail its audit: a leftover byte of an old host address, and the live `MenuItemProc.yTile` (harmless on route ch5, not by construction). So a result that matters takes its photo with the binary under test. The NULL probe refuses save states. **Round 9.** H-col-1 (cov9; `pending/folded/r9-base.md`): the relink refuses some photos onto another build of the *same* source, not only a changed one (6 of 11 parts from 5 photos: `gStatusScreenSt+0x8` holds `0x10004000`/`0x10008000`, `gPrepUnitList+0x84`/`+0xF4` hold `0x1000F222`/`0x10000F88`, host words no symbol covers), so a photo resumes reliably only on the binary that took it. The held `r9-harness` fix treats those words as overlay addresses (its review relinked all 28 PC parts of the 16 r9 photos); not applied this round. Test-build photos cannot be resumed (`savestate.py`'s `PORT_TABLE` lacks the test-build statics of `port/hw/frame.o`; the cov9 scout's H1). The ready-made photos are taken again with each integration branch's own Gate 0 binary: round 9's are in `~/fe8-snapshots/ready/<route>/tick<T>/` (section 3, "Round 9 integration"). **H-col-1 fixed on `port-integrate9` (`b9e41d53`):** a declared pointer that an overlaid variable (`EWRAM_OVERLAY`) declares as no pointer is left alone only when the GBA's photo holds the same 32-bit value (the other variable's data: `gStatusScreenSt.font.vramDest` over `gBanimOaml`'s OAM words, `gPrepUnitList.units[33]`/`[61]` over `gBanimScrRight`'s bytes); every other typed pointer must still resolve, and a photo without a gba part keeps the old refusal. All 17 r9 PC parts relink into another build of the same source. The ready-made photos of `port-integrate9` are in `~/fe8-snapshots/ready9/`. **Round 10 and integration 11 (H-11, H-12):** the relink classifies `port/hw/memory.o`'s `sMapped` and `buf` (main's `79e83239`; before, no relink onto main's binary worked) and game objects' own log-once flags (`GAME_SESSION`), drops stub counters the new binary does not have, and a photo taken before round 10 needs `SAVESTATE_NEW_GAME_DATA=initial` on a round-10 binary (B-20's 23 read-only tables). The `c10-*` photos made with `port-cover10`'s binary (before `uiarena.c` was built, B-19) still refuse a round-9-or-later binary (three new game `.data` proc scripts and the arena stub counters: round 8's decision 7); make them again where still needed. Since integration 11 `sTrapPool` is traced (H-20): an older photo's prologue line lacks it and is compared without it. The ready-made photos of `port-integrate11` are in `~/fe8-snapshots/ready11/` (section 3).
- **Windowed compares of probe runs (2026-10-20, `port-integrate5`; scratch tool `wf-fe8/i5_window.py`, not committed).** A run that needs the NULL probe cannot start from a save state, so a long compare is cut into windows of the full power-on traces with a `resume` header and the lag replay's prefix worked out from the full traces (as `savestate.py lag_prefix` gives it). On 15,900-50,000 that gives the save-state resume's `compare.json` exactly; each 50,000-tick window peaked at 1.9 GB. An every-frame compare can hash the GBA's PNGs first (`wf-fe8/i6_pnghash.py`, checked against `gate0_compare.png_15bit_hash`), which the compare otherwise does in pure Python at about 23 ms a frame.
- **A lag VBlank at a proc script's head (cov9 A2; `pending/r9-harness.md`, the fix held).** On Ch2's Supply screen a GBA lag VBlank came inside `RunProcessScript` after the new VBlank-tree proc `ProcScr_SlidingWallBg` was started but before its first command; the lag replay places a lag VBlank only before or after a whole event, so `gLCDControlBuffer` `bgoffset[3]` was one step ahead on the PC for 291 ticks (4,491-5,651, `mkA_ch2_pp2_s3.keys`, snapshot `~/fe8-snapshots/cov9-mkA-C2s3/tick4485`). The held fix records such a VBlank as a `lag_head` position, and the capture stops if that VBlank's tree-0 walk changed something the head check cannot account for; its review found that `checkHead` skips the check when the VBlank lands on a command handler's first instruction (the handlers sit below `RPS_FIRST_CHECK`; route_ch5 59,429, route_e9 27,678, route_e10 42,116), and its fix pass (`~/gate0-scratch/fp9-harness/fix.patch`, `pending/r9-harness.md` "Fix pass fp9") closes that hole (23 of the 24 CHECK_LONG heads checked, none stops); held for this round, not applied. A harness gap, not a game finding. **Applied on `port-integrate9` (`b9e41d53`, re-approved after the fix pass):** "Lag check v2" above has the rule as it stands.
- **Key-file lines of any length (cov9 H-A; `port-integrate9` `b9e41d53`).** The PC hook read key files in 256-byte lines: a longer comment either stopped the PC run at tick 0 (status 4) or, when its rest looked like hex, shifted every later mask by one tick on the PC only, with no message. `gate0_hook.c` `ReadKeys` now uses `getline`, as the GBA side (Lua `io.lines`) always did.
- **The monkey's tick rule (cov9 H-B; `b9e41d53`).** `make monkey MONKEY_TICK=capture` makes the menu monkey count ticks the Gate 0 capture's way (`route_lib.lua`'s `L.onTick`, with the breakpoints), so it follows an early flush VBlank; such a file says `# Tick rule: capture` in its header. The default stays `frame`, so every committed file in `inputs/monkey/` regenerates byte for byte. H6 (re-record the old routes with the capture rule) was left as a decision; the recommendation is no. **Seen in round 10 (the gone review):** `la2_s104`/`la2_s105`'s MONKEY REPLAY reports a difference GBA against GBA at 25,390 / 23,535, one tick after an early-flush VBlank: the monkey's frame tick rule against the capture's (the Gate 0 compare of the same capture matches); not fixed.
- **Sound on any route (`port-snd10` `1421977e`, merged on `port-integrate9` `ff411886`; README "Sound on any route", `pending/folded/snd10.md`, `snd10-findings.md`, `snd10-monkey.md`, `snd-story.md`).** `make -f port/test/Makefile snd-route ROUTE_KEYS=...` (or `MONKEY_KEYS=`, with `GATE0_SRAM_IN` or `GATE0_STATE_IN`): one GBA capture that also writes the sound driver's whole state every VBlank and the m4a calls between them, the PC lag replay of that trace with its own sound records, then `audio/snd_route.py`, record by record and byte by byte (PCM lanes included), in chunks, plus the snd rows' own replay check and the PC self-check with their old rules; `SND_GATE0=1` also runs the route's Gate 0 compare (reported, not part of SND ROUTE). The PC side is the lag replay, so a shipped (lag-free) run's sound is still checked only by the snd rows' self-check. Three harness bugs were fixed on the branch: H-snd-1 (the lag replay's lag frames made no sound record and played no FIFO/PSG samples), H-snd-2 (an m4a call is no main-loop event, so a lag VBlank before an `m4aSongNumStart` could run after it on the PC: `lag_snd`, the calls since the event, and `LagReplaySndDue`), H-snd-3 (a lag VBlank inside an m4a call with its player locked, route_ch5 tick 102,053: `lag_snd_split` [index, TrackStop calls, locks], run at the same place inside the call). None changes a traced game field (route_ch1's traces are byte-identical to the base). **At the merge** the two harness branches clashed in one place: snd10 kept the sound count in bits 20-30 of each `sLagUsed` entry and the r9 harness uses bit 30 for `LAG_USED_HEAD`; the count now uses bits 20-29 (`LAG_USED_SND_MAX` 0x3FF, checked where it is read and stored). **Open, harness only** (no game sound difference): H-story-1 (a lag VBlank that can only be placed at the return of the main loop's m4a call re-enters `LagReplaySplitExit` with `sSndMainOpen` still set until the 256 slots are full: route_ch6 tick 31,576 and route_ch8 20,426 stop INCOMPLETE; the diagnostic guard `wf-fe8/sstory_diag_guard.diff`, clearing `sSndMainOpen` first, makes both pass whole; not committed); H-story-2 (route_ch7 tick 41,152: a flush VBlank whose frame callback came before the wait breakpoint and its IRQ after it, so the GBA's frame index is one record later on that tick and check 3 refuses to pair; paired at D 3 every record is equal); H-sndmk-1 (snd-route refuses the committed power-on monkey file `mkA_prologue_s1.keys`, whose header names an SRAM FNV-1a; a header-edited copy with the same key lines passes). **Round 10 (`port-r10-sound`, `pending/folded/r10-sound.md`) and integration 11:** H-story-1 (SG2), H-story-2 (SG4) and H-sndmk-1 (SG5) are fixed (H-13, H-14, H-15), a lag VBlank nested in an IRQ handler's m4a call is replayed with the GBA's locks (H-16), the snd-route rows run in CHECK_LONG (B-23), and since integration 11 the PCM lanes of a record the GBA's mixer was caught writing are excused on the VBlanks a not-clean `m4aSoundMain` spans (H-17; route e15 passes). The sweep of every route and monkey file on round 10's base (54 runs, 3,210,873 PC records): all fully equal but 8 excused under the old rule, 20 differing (b21) and e15's lanes on one record. **Open, deferred to round 11 (integration 11's choice, the sound group's recommendation):** a lag VBlank inside `m4aMPlayImmInit`, which writes without a lock, is placed before the call (`route_b21` tick 19,566: GBA at `0x080D0414`, after that track's `flags = 0x80`, r4 `0x03002478`, r5 4 tracks left; 20 PC records differ at GBA v 20,585-20,604: one PCM channel's envelope volumes, two track fields, PCM lanes, then equal); the exact replay splits the call by track and by store (as `LagSplitCall` splits a BIOS copy), with a review of every unlocked writer in `m4a.c`. The OnMain-count records (the README's known kind, for example ch7 v 38,122) are not probed.
- **Known limits of check-all rows (2026-10-24, coverage 12 `b144de8d`, H-23; integration 12).** `port/test/known_limits.json` lists, per check-all row, the exact fields and tick windows of a recorded limit of the test tool; a row whose compare is DIFFERENT only inside them is KNOWN, not FAIL (`port/tools/check_all.py`; `port/test/README.md` "Known limits of check-all rows"). The entries: `route-br9-lag`, **N-C11-1** (coverage 11): the lag replay's limit, C10-10 (c)'s kind: 4 GBA lag VBlanks inside a BIOS LZ77 decompression into VRAM put palette 9 colours 12-15 (the map's flicker) one step out of order, `gPaletteBuffer` 62,549-62,563, and again at the change to floor 10, 96,999-97,215 (GBA lag ticks 96,999 and 97,000; inferred the same kind, not probed); every other field equal, no frame differs. `route-i21-lag`, **LAG-FRAME**: Lyon's Naglfar on a GBA lag tick, `frame15` 62,520, every traced field equal (round 8's kind 2; coverage 12's C12-SW-1). `route-er3-lag`, **H-c12-pr-1** (coverage 12, `pending/folded/c12-postgame-real.md`): a GBA lag VBlank in `efxExcalibur_Loop_Main`'s step runs after `efxExcaliburBGCOL3`'s `Proc_Start` on the PC run (46,646), so that proc takes another slot; at four later phase changes `TerrainDisplay_Loop_OnSideChange` reads another leftover `windowQuadrant` from the recycled slot (`src/player_interface.c:1154`, retail) and the side windows slide in a tick apart: `gBG0TilemapBuffer`/`gBG1TilemapBuffer` 66,706-66,708, 88,310-88,312, 101,076-101,078, 133,303-133,305, with the `sequence` next to each; every game value equal (the "Knock-on: proc slot order" kind). `route_er4` (not a row) shows the same at 2,724. `route-gallery-lag` (the item gallery, cheat-assisted), two entries: **H-r13-gal-1** (fix round 13, `b58b4583`, P-41): the same kind as H-c12-pr-1: at 93,316 (Excalibur) the GBA's lag VBlank freed proc slot 56 before the next `Proc_Start`'s `AllocateProcess`, the lag replay runs it after (that proc takes slot 15), and in turns 21-26 `gProcScr_TerrainDisplay`'s step reads leftover proc-slot data (retail): `gBG0TilemapBuffer` 94,512, 96,645-96,648, 98,134-98,136, 104,697-104,700, 110,506, `gBG1TilemapBuffer` the same but 96,645-96,647 and 104,697-104,699, `frame15` 104,700, and the `sequence` on 16 ticks from 94,511 to 110,505; every game value equal. **LAG-FRAME** (fix round 14, `f06060ab`, P-42's battle frames, C12-GAL-6): `frame15` 27,278; 120,882, 120,888, 120,894; 127,456; 129,436, 129,440, 129,444, 129,448, 129,488, 129,492; 130,087, each a frame on a GBA lag tick whose lag VBlank came inside a step, every traced field equal (seen with every frame only; CHECK_LONG's samples miss them). Integrations 13 to 15: `route-gallery-lag` KNOWN (H-r13-gal-1 used), with no difference outside the windows.
- **The lag replay's position limit, coverage 12's new sites (2026-10-24, `pending/folded/c12-findings.md` group 6; not resolved, as C10-10 (c)).** A GBA lag VBlank in the middle of one step runs later on the PC run. Inside a BIOS LZ77 decompression: palette 9 colours 12-15 one step apart (Ch18 after a map change, 14 ticks; Ch18's opening, 1 tick; monkey-post's three skirmishes, 47 ticks; `route_br9`'s second window), Chapter 16's ending light (`ProcScr_089A448C`, `sub_8080408`, one frame). Between a tree-0 proc's start and its setup: Ch5x's water tiles (`BMapVSync_InitMapAnimations`, 39 frames; `watch/rp_ch5x_bmvsync.keys`), Ch30's narration (`TalkAdvance`'s proc: frames, one `gOam` tick, the event count on 4 ticks). After a `Proc_Start` the GBA made later: H-c12-pr-1 (above). Between two stores of one function: `GMScreenVSync_AddCopyRequest` (the GBA loses a tile-copy request, a retail race on slow frames, one frame; N-C12-I1). Inside a sound call: `TrackStop`, `MPlayStart`'s lock (coverage 12's sound shard: `route_bt1`, `br2`, `i14`, `b21` fail snd-route there; round 10's `m4aMPlayImmInit` is the same kind). None of them is met by a player: the plain PC has no lag VBlanks.

### GBA lag paths found in round 1 (harness notes, 2026-10-19)

The PC does not model lag, by design (PLAN.md 3.4: "Lag frames are not modelled"; lag-frame emulation is out of scope, PLAN.md section 7). Round 1 traced the ticks of the 60,000-tick scripted run that the clock replay leaves "not lag-only" (168 of the 25,558 RESULT ticks at B-12; the audio merge leaves the traces unchanged). Every tick traced is GBA lag that the clock replay does not reproduce. These are harness labelling issues, not port bugs: no game or port code changes for them, and the lag check is being fixed in `port/test`. Three paths:

1. **Extra tree-0 proc steps in lag VBlanks.** `OnVBlank` (`src/bm.c:303-324`) runs `Proc_Run(gProcTreeRootArray[0])` (`PROC_TREE_VSYNC`, line 310) on every VBlank, also on a lag VBlank inside `OnMain`, where `main_loop_ended` is 0 and nothing is flushed. A VSync proc started earlier in that `OnMain` then gets an extra step. Tick 12,480, `frame15` (32 pixels of "...threatened by a"): in tick 12,402 `CleanTalkObjects` (`src/scene.c:2498-2508`) starts the text-scroll proc `ProcScr_08591624` on tree 0 (line 2500) at scanline 150, and the lag VBlank at scanline 160 runs its first `TalkAdvance_Loop` step (line 2518) inside that tick. The scroll ends, and the talk goes on, one tick earlier on the GBA: each glyph shows at tick t on the GBA and t+1 on the PC until the A press at 12,492. It depends on where the overrun lands: at 9,342 the lag VBlank came before `CleanTalkObjects`, and nothing shifted.
2. **Clock reads later in a tick that had a lag VBlank.** The lag VBlank's `IncrementGameClock` (`bm.c:307`) makes every later `GetGameClock()` in that `OnMain` see the clock 1 higher per lag VBlank. The clock replay sets `gGameClock` only at tick boundaries (`port/test/pc/gate0_hook.c:374`), so its whole tick sees the start-of-tick value. `gOam` at 8 ticks: 25,383 and 46,713 (battle gauge arrows, `src/banim-ekrgauge.c:252`), 34,860 and 49,032 (the hand's x wobble, `src/uiutils.c:452`), 40,121, 46,436, 48,388 and 48,608 (rescue icon blink, `src/bmudisp.c:1048`). `gPaletteBuffer` at 4 ticks, 41,950, 48,608, 48,879 and 49,032: the range squares (`MoveLimitView_OnLoop`, `src/playerphase.c:1309`, BG palettes 4-5) and the green text (`GreenText_OnLoop`, `src/fontgrp.c:1054`, BG palette 0 colour 14).
3. **Clock edges inside lag VBlanks.** A tree-0 handler that acts only on exact clock values sees every value the lag VBlanks pass through; the replay jumps over them. Tick 21,660, `frame15`: `SyncUnitSpriteSheet` (`src/bmudisp.c:635-650`, run by `sProc_BMVSync` on tree 0, `src/bmio.c:139` and `299`) copies a map-sprite sheet only when `clock % 72` is 0, 32, 36 or 68. The GBA's 5 lag VBlanks in tick 21,648 ran it at clock 17,100 (`% 72` = 36). The replay goes from 34 to 40, so the PC keeps the old sheet until the 68 edge at 21,677.

**Knock-on: proc slot order, then a retail uninitialised read.** Path 1 can also change which proc slot a later proc gets. At tick 39,083 a lag VBlank gave the battle hit-flash proc `efxFlashBG` (tree 0, `src/banim-efxflashbg.c:20`, 4 VBlanks long) a second step, so it ended and was freed one tick earlier than on the PC, before `efxFlashHPBar` (tree 4, `src/banim-efxflashunit.c:28`) instead of after it. The LIFO free list (`src/proc.c:140-153`) then had slots 14 and 23 swapped from tick 39,085. At 41,188 `InitPlayerPhaseInterface` (`src/player_interface.c:1447`) starts the terrain window, the goal window and the minimug box (UI1) in tree 3, and the terrain window runs first: `TerrainDisplay_Loop_OnSideChange` reads `ui1Proc->windowQuadrant` (+0x57, line 1154) before `MMB_Init` (line 1230) has set it. `Proc_Start` clears only the proc header, so the byte is whatever the slot held before: 0 in GBA slot 23, 3 in PC slot 14. 3 is the terrain window's quadrant, so the PC returns early and slides the window in one tick late: `gBG0TilemapBuffer` and `gBG1TilemapBuffer` at 41,189-41,191. **This read must stay as retail:** no `BUGFIX` path and no clearing of proc memory. The PC reads what its own lag-free slot history left there, as the GBA reads what its history left. D-9 (the NULL `piProc` read in the same function) is not involved.

**Coverage.** Traced, and confirmed by a second independent check: 12,480, 21,660, the 8 `gOam` ticks, the 4 `gPaletteBuffer` ticks and BG0/BG1 41,189-41,191. 5,880 is the OL-2 frame (P2.2b). The rest of the 168 are `frame15` ticks (one frame every 60 ticks), most of them between 33,660 and 51,420. The lag-replay prototype below labels all but 10 of them lag-only, which fits paths 1 and 3, but they were not traced one by one. Those 10, `frame15` ticks 38,820-39,360 (around the battle animation of 38,864-39,424), are cleared by neither prototype and are not traced yet.

**Done since** (`port-lagcheck`, lag check v2 above): the lag replay at the GBA's positions reproduces every one of the 167 not-lag-only ticks of the clock replay (and the 10 `frame15` ticks 38,820-39,360 below), so on this route none is left; the prototypes below are superseded.

**Harness fix (`port/test` only, in progress at the time; prototypes not committed).**
- **Read-exact clock replay** (`~/gate0-scratch/oam25383/readexact-replay.patch`, GBA side `lagpos.lua`): the GBA capture records where each lag VBlank fell in the tick's sequence of `GetGameClock` reads (breakpoints at `0x08000D2C` and `0x080152E2`), and the PC (`-Wl,--wrap=GetGameClock`) adds each +1 before the same read. Exact on this route: read counts and both `gPlaySt` clock copies equal the GBA's on every tick. It clears path 2 (168 to 158). Gaps: a `SetGameTime` write after a lag VBlank in the same tick, and ticks with an inner `VBlankIntrWait` (neither on this route).
- **Lag (VBlank) replay** (`~/gate0-scratch/t12480/lag_replay_prototype.diff`; the same idea in `~/gate0-scratch/bg41189/gate0_hook_probe_lagreplay.diff`): before tick t is sampled, `__real_OnVBlank` runs once per GBA lag VBlank of that tick, with `main_loop_ended` cleared. It clears paths 1 and 3 and the knock-on, but it puts every lag VBlank at the end of `OnMain`, so it is not GBA-exact (12 frames differ only in this replay; the 9,342 case). Use it only as a second labelling replay beside the clock replay. With "lag-only if the clock replay or the lag replay matches", 20 ticks stay not lag-only (the path-2 ticks and the 10 frames above), plus the 5,880 frame.
- Exact placement for path 1 would need the GBA capture to record where in `OnMain` each lag VBlank landed, with a matching marker on the PC. Not on this route, but noted by the reviewers: on a weather map, lag VBlanks also run the tree-0 weather VSync and `SyncLoOam`, a lag path into OAM that does not go through the clock.
